# Canine babesiosis research map

[Back to article index](index.md) · [Datasets](datasets.md) · [Search log](search-log.md)

This map synthesises the canine articles in the [index](index.md), published 2006-10-23 to 2026-10-23, by pathophysiological category. Each article's categories are assigned automatically by the NLP classifier (`scripts/categorise_articles.py`), with no human review, so a few articles sit in a category that is not their main subject; the category sections name these. Categories were revised from the provisional list by adding **SEV (multisystem clinical phenotype, severity and outcome)** for the large body of multi-organ clinicopathological and outcome studies that have no dominant organ or mechanism. Each article has one primary category (counts reconcile with the index) and any number of secondary tags (counts overlap and are not additive). Article counts are reported separately from independent cohorts: articles sharing a cohort identifier (see [Cohorts](#cohorts)) are not independent replication.

Throughout, a **syndrome** is a clinical complication (e.g. AKI, ARDS, cerebral babesiosis), a **mechanism** is a proposed causal process (e.g. cell-free haemoglobin–driven endothelial injury), and a **severity biomarker** is a measured variable associated with severity or outcome (e.g. CRP, lactate); associations are not treated as mechanisms.

## Cross-cutting themes

Links are relative to the review root; Q1–Q5 refer to the [research questions](#research-questions) below.

**1. Haemolysis, haem, endothelium and AKI.** ERY, END, REN and OXO still describe adjacent parts of one proposed chain, never measured in the same dogs:

- haemolysis ([CB082](articles/CB082_2021_experimental-babesia-rossi-infection-induces-hemolytic.md), CH09 experiment; [CB522](articles/CB522_2016_septic-shock-similarities-differences-postmortem-findings.md), necropsy);
- oxidative change (lipid peroxidation raised in every OXO study that measured it, from [CB353](articles/CB353_2008_erythrocytic-antioxidant-defense-lipid-peroxides-level.md) in 2008);
- endothelial activation ([CB446](articles/CB446_2013_markers-coagulation-activation-endothelial-stimulation-inflammation.md), [CB004](articles/CB004_2017_blood-markers-fibrinolysis-endothelial-activation-babesiosis.md)) and capillary endothelial degeneration in fatal kidneys ([CB341](articles/CB341_2007_histological-ultrastructural-studies-renal-lesions-babesia.md), 2007);
- tubular injury before azotaemia ([CB435](articles/CB435_2012_assessment-renal-dysfunction-using-urinary-markers.md) in 2012, then [CB053](articles/CB053_2020_evaluation-acute-kidney-injury-complicated-or.md), in *B. rossi*; CH01 and [CB024](articles/CB024_2018_glomerular-tubular-kidney-damage-markers-babesiosis.md) in *B. canis*).

The 2006–2016 work weakens a simple haemoglobin-load reading. In CH29 ([CB325](articles/CB325_2006_elevated-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-babesiosis.md), [CB412](articles/CB412_2011_changes-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-babesiosis.md)), urea and the urea:creatinine ratio rose while creatinine, cystatin-C and GFR stayed normal, and induced haemoglobinaemia did not reproduce the ratio; in [BG002](articles/BG002_1996_comparative-role-haemoglobinaemia-hypoxia-development-babesial.md) (1996) haemoglobinaemia alone did not cause nephropathy. Urea, creatinine, liver enzymes and MDA did not differ by anaemia status ([CB365](articles/CB365_2009_influence-anaemia-azotaemia-infected-babesia-canis.md), [CB424](articles/CB424_2012_increased-ast-alt-ratio-azotaemic-infected.md), [CB409](articles/CB409_2011_liver-enzyme-activity-infected-babesia-canis.md), [CB394](articles/CB394_2010_malondialdehyde-levels-serum-infected-babesia-canis.md)), though anaemia grade is not haemolysis rate. Warsaw correlations add a haemodynamic co-factor (hypotension, TNF-α, aldosterone; [CB463](articles/CB463_2014_association-between-decreased-blood-pressure-azotaemia.md), [CB473](articles/CB473_2014_increased-concentration-serum-tnf-alpha-correlations.md), [CB485](articles/CB485_2014_hyperaldosteronism-association-hypotension-azotaemia-babesiosis.md)). Cell-free haemoglobin and its scavengers are unmeasured in dogs, so that link rests on malaria ([HU002](articles/HU002_2017_cell-free-hemoglobin-mediated-oxidative-stress.md), [HU010](articles/HU010_2018_intravascular-haemolysis-severe-plasmodium-knowlesi-malaria.md)). Q1 points to co-factors, not one pathway.

**2. Inflammation and coagulation are coupled, but only across papers.** INF, COA, PUL and NEU converge on thromboinflammation in fatal *B. rossi*:

- cytokines: IL-6 and MCP-1 higher in non-survivors ([CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md), CH03; [CB037](articles/CB037_2019_disease-severity-blood-cytokine-concentrations-natural.md)), while CRP and SAA did not separate outcome ([CB376](articles/CB376_2009_c-reactive-protein-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), 2009; [CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md));
- consumption: lower factor and protein C activity, longer PT and aPTT and higher D-dimer in non-survivors ([CB438](articles/CB438_2012_mortality-virulent-babesiosis-associated-consumptive-coagulopathy.md), 2012; then [CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md), CH03), with minimal fibrinolysis ([CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md));
- tissue fibrin: intra-alveolar fibrin ([CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md), CH04) and cerebral thrombosis ([CB189](articles/CB189_2020_encephalic-babesiosis-clinical-epidemiological-pathological-aspects.md), species unspecified).

[CB438](articles/CB438_2012_mortality-virulent-babesiosis-associated-consumptive-coagulopathy.md) makes the coagulation–death link older and multi-source, but all from Pretoria. Uncomplicated *B. rossi* keeps clot strength, with high fibrinogen ([CB439](articles/CB439_2012_hemostatic-abnormalities-uncomplicated-babesiosis-babesia-rossi.md)), so consumption marks the fatal tail. [CB356](articles/CB356_2008_scintigraphic-evaluation-pulmonary-perfusion-pattern-hospitalised.md) found no large-vessel lung perfusion defects, pointing to the microvasculature. The cytokine and coagulation axes were measured in the same CH03 dogs but never analysed jointly (Q2); the cytokines are public ([S-CB517](datasets.md#s-cb517), [S-CB050](datasets.md#s-cb050)) and the coagulation data request-only (RQ-[CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md)). NETs and ADAMTS13 are unmeasured in dogs (von Willebrand factor activity only in [CB446](articles/CB446_2013_markers-coagulation-activation-endothelial-stimulation-inflammation.md)); their role is inferred from sepsis and malaria ([HU008](articles/HU008_2019_novel-assay-neutrophil-extracellular-trap-formation.md), [HU009](articles/HU009_2009_severe-plasmodium-falciparum-malaria-associated-circulating.md)).

**3. *B. rossi* versus *B. canis*, *B. gibsoni* and *B. vogeli*.** Species, setting and design remain entangled:

- *B. rossi*: high fibrinogen, consumption in non-survivors, low and falling IL-8, and higher cortisol with lower T4 in severe cases (CH31, [CB040](articles/CB040_2019_clinical-pathological-description-320-cases-naturally.md), [CB105](articles/CB105_2022_serial-changes-concentrations-cortisol-thyroid-hormones.md)). Hypoglycaemia is not insulin-driven, and 16 of 94 dogs were hyperglycaemic ([CB340](articles/CB340_2007_plasma-insulin-concentrations-hypoglycaemic-babesia-canis.md)); hypoglycaemia therefore does not separate *B. rossi* from *B. canis*.
- *B. canis*: thrombin generation with antithrombin III consumption ([CB374](articles/CB374_2009_alterations-some-blood-coagulation-parameters-naturally.md), [CB011](articles/CB011_2017_alteration-haemostatic-parameters-uncomplicated-babesiosis.md), which disagree on DIC); higher IL-8 and KC-like in complicated disease (CH23, one Zagreb source, since [CB510](articles/CB510_2015_serum-levels-chemokines-keratinocyte-chemoattractant-interleukin.md) and [CB511](articles/CB511_2015_razine-kemokina-kc-i-il-8.md) are one study); and an organ-count survival gradient ([CB399](articles/CB399_2010_systemic-inflammatory-response-syndrome-multiple-organ.md), [CB043](articles/CB043_2019_clinical-occurrence-babesiosis-coastal-area-black.md)).
- *B. gibsoni*: immune-mediated (CH17, one source), proteinuric ([CB404](articles/CB404_2011_resolution-proteinuric-nephropathy-associated-babesia-gibsoni.md), [CB018](articles/CB018_2018_azotemia-proteinuria-infected-babesia-gibsoni.md)) and cerebral (CH16, one source) phenotypes, mostly from India.
- *B. vogeli*: mild in intact dogs ([CB021](articles/CB021_2018_first-description-pathogenicity-babesia-vogeli-experimentally.md), [CB339](articles/CB339_2007_parasitological-molecular-characterization-venezuelan-isolate-babesia.md)), severe with splenectomy or co-infection.

The mortality gap has narrowed: 6–12% in *B. rossi* cohorts ([CB338](articles/CB338_2007_endocrine-predictors-mortality-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), [CB352](articles/CB352_2008_adrenal-response-low-dose-acth-stimulation.md), [CB050](articles/CB050_2020_neutrophil-myeloperoxidase-index-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), [CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md)) against 6–7% in *B. canis* ([CB149](articles/CB149_2026_retrospective-analysis-anamnesis-clinical-signs-therapy.md), [CB100](articles/CB100_2022_clinical-hematologic-findings-babesia-canis-infection.md)). The only within-study species contrast is CH32 in Italy ([CB357](articles/CB357_2008_babesia-canis-canis-babesia-canis-vogeli.md), [CB369](articles/CB369_2009_detection-erythrocyte-binding-igm-igg-flow.md)), where erythrocyte-bound IgG or IgM occurred in 4 of 6 *B. c. vogeli* and 0 of 24 *B. c. canis* dogs. Assay, timing, referral and co-infection confound every other contrast, IL-8 included (Q3).

**4. Most of the work is descriptive; experiments are older than they looked.** Descriptive series and case reports outnumber associational studies in every category. Most experiments are from 2006–2016: [CB370](articles/CB370_2009_systemic-inflammatory-responses-experimentally-infected-babesia.md) (*B. canis*), [CB509](articles/CB509_2015_clinical-haematological-cytokine-acute-phase-protein.md) (*B. gibsoni*), [CB474](articles/CB474_2014_clinico-biochemical-responses-experimental-infection-babesia.md) (*B. canis*) and [CB419](articles/CB419_2011_thrombocytopenia-platelet-activity-experimentally-infected-rangelia.md) (*Rangelia vitalii*); INF counts 11 experimental papers, 8 from round 4. A few older studies manipulate a variable (induced haemoglobinaemia, [CB412](articles/CB412_2011_changes-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-babesiosis.md); red cell stroma infusion, [CB401](articles/CB401_2010_soluble-parasite-antigens-babesia-canis-do.md); infected-dog serum on reticulocytes in vitro, [CB337](articles/CB337_2007_suppressive-effect-culture-supernatant-erythrocytes-serum.md)). All are small, and none blocks a pathway in infected dogs. Prognosis is no stronger: [CB040](articles/CB040_2019_clinical-pathological-description-320-cases-naturally.md) found no independent predictor, the [CB074](articles/CB074_2021_prognostic-markers-their-discriminant-score-predicting.md) score and [CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md) cut-offs were set on the same dogs, [CB202](articles/CB202_2020_serum-hepcidin-clinical-prognostic-marker-discriminate.md) is confounded by species, and nothing is externally validated. Cohort reuse inflates apparent replication: CH03 holds 6 articles, CH09 5, CH31 and CH16 4 each, and [CB510](articles/CB510_2015_serum-levels-chemokines-keratinocyte-chemoattractant-interleukin.md) and [CB511](articles/CB511_2015_razine-kemokina-kc-i-il-8.md) are one study published twice.

**5. Mechanistic work and data sit in three centres.** Onderstepoort (*B. rossi*), Croatia (mostly Zagreb, *B. canis*) and Warsaw (*B. canis*) hold 16 of the 38 cohorts (6, 5 and 5), which may overlap within a centre unstated.

- Of 15 independent sources with verified individual-level data (21 records), 6 are South African *B. rossi* and 4 Croatian (three dog cohorts, one wolf survey). [DS08](datasets.md#ds08) is probably Zagreb too (country not in the abstract). The rest are single sources: Germany ([S-CB106](datasets.md#s-cb106)), Switzerland ([S-CB516](datasets.md#s-cb516)), the USA ([S-CB042](datasets.md#s-cb042), *B. vulpes*) and Australia ([S-CB013](datasets.md#s-cb013), 7 *B. vogeli* dogs).
- The 2006–2016 work adds one source (S-[CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md), laboratory data as a figure only) and one record to an existing one (S-[CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md), CH03). The founding endocrine (CH31), coagulation ([CB438](articles/CB438_2012_mortality-virulent-babesiosis-associated-consumptive-coagulopathy.md)) and renal (CH29) studies have no data.
- Neither the Polish work nor the Indian *B. gibsoni* literature that dominates ERY, NEU and CAR has a verified individual-level dataset ([S-CB093](datasets.md#s-cb093) is summary statistics; RQ-[CB109](articles/CB109_2023_haematological-indices-immune-response-profiles-naturally.md), RQ-[CB536](articles/CB536_2016_prevalence-dirofilaria-repens-cats-healthy-concurrent.md), RQ-[CB134](articles/CB134_2025_clinical-clinicopathological-features-108-infected-babesia.md) and RQ-[CB300](articles/CB300_2026_therapeutic-studies-post-therapeutic-follow-up.md) are request-only).

Reanalysis can address Q2 in part (S-[CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md), S-[CB050](articles/CB050_2020_neutrophil-myeloperoxidase-index-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md)), Q3 ([S-CB017](datasets.md#s-cb017) against S-[CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md), with different assays), Q4 ([DS03](datasets.md#ds03)) and Q5 ([DS01](datasets.md#ds01), [DS02](datasets.md#ds02), [DS06](datasets.md#ds06)). Apart from S-[CB017](articles/CB017_2018_mcp-1-kc-like-il-8.md), all of these are *B. rossi*.

**6. Measurement artefacts and case definitions cut across categories.** These include:

- spuriously high analyser reticulocyte counts in large-form babesiosis ([CB538](articles/CB538_2016_spurious-reticulocyte-profiles-large-form-babesiosis.md), [CB540](articles/CB540_2016_spurious-reticulocyte-profiles-babesiosis.md); Q4);
- urea rising without a fall in GFR ([CB325](articles/CB325_2006_elevated-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-babesiosis.md), [CB412](articles/CB412_2011_changes-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-babesiosis.md), [CB478](articles/CB478_2014_renal-function-integrity-babesiosis.md)), so urea-based azotaemia counts overstate renal disease;
- assays: human cystatin-C ([CB325](articles/CB325_2006_elevated-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-babesiosis.md) against [CB501](articles/CB501_2015_assessment-renal-function-using-cystatin-c.md)), non-standard Ukrainian fibrinogen, SOD and catalase normalisation, and haemolysis interfering with AST, bilirubin and coagulation optics;
- detection floors: TNF-α undetectable in 5 of 11 dogs ([CB473](articles/CB473_2014_increased-concentration-serum-tnf-alpha-correlations.md)), insulin mostly below the assay limit ([CB340](articles/CB340_2007_plasma-insulin-concentrations-hypoglycaemic-babesia-canis.md));
- case definitions: CSF erythrocytes that may be contamination (CH16), differing SIRS definitions, species by smear alone;
- sampling after treatment ([CB017](articles/CB017_2018_mcp-1-kc-like-il-8.md) at 6–8 h after imidocarb; [CB499](articles/CB499_2015_lipid-peroxidation-naturally-infected-babesia-canis.md) from day 1), when treatment transiently amplified inflammation in [CB098](articles/CB098_2022_kinetics-inflammatory-response-during-experimental-babesia.md) (Q5).

Pre-analytical and case-definition standards are a prerequisite for every question, not a refinement.

**7. The founding decade set the agenda.** Extending the window to 2006 shows that many 2016–2026 findings repeat older work:

- coagulation: [CB374](articles/CB374_2009_alterations-some-blood-coagulation-parameters-naturally.md) (2009) before [CB011](articles/CB011_2017_alteration-haemostatic-parameters-uncomplicated-babesiosis.md) in *B. canis*; [CB438](articles/CB438_2012_mortality-virulent-babesiosis-associated-consumptive-coagulopathy.md) (2012) before [CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md) in *B. rossi*;
- kidney: [CB435](articles/CB435_2012_assessment-renal-dysfunction-using-urinary-markers.md) (2012) before [CB053](articles/CB053_2020_evaluation-acute-kidney-injury-complicated-or.md); the urea caveat from [CB325](articles/CB325_2006_elevated-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-babesiosis.md) (2006);
- marrow: [CB418](articles/CB418_2011_serial-haematology-results-transfused-non-transfused.md) (2011) and [CB337](articles/CB337_2007_suppressive-effect-culture-supernatant-erythrocytes-serum.md) (2007) before [CB115](articles/CB115_2023_regenerative-response-naturally-experimentally-infected-babesia.md) (Q4);
- endocrine: [CB338](articles/CB338_2007_endocrine-predictors-mortality-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md) and [CB342](articles/CB342_2007_response-pituitary-adrenal-pituitary-thyroidal-axes.md) (2007) before [CB040](articles/CB040_2019_clinical-pathological-description-320-cases-naturally.md) and [CB105](articles/CB105_2022_serial-changes-concentrations-cortisol-thyroid-hormones.md);
- acute-phase response, lipid peroxidation and organ count: [CB323](articles/CB323_2006_evidence-acute-phase-response-naturally-infected.md) (2006), [CB353](articles/CB353_2008_erythrocytic-antioxidant-defense-lipid-peroxides-level.md) (2008), [CB399](articles/CB399_2010_systemic-inflammatory-response-syndrome-multiple-organ.md) (2010);
- lung: the ARDS lesion in [CB447](articles/CB447_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-acute-respiratory-distress-syndrome.md) (2013, *B. canis*) before [CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md) (2023).

This adds sources, not quality: most round-4 papers are abstract-only (11 of 13 in COA, 23 of 35 in INF), so group sizes and methods are often missing. After 2016 the base shifts towards small Indian *B. gibsoni* series, Ukrainian clinical work and omics; earlier *B. gibsoni* work is mostly experimental or single cases ([CB335](articles/CB335_2007_babesia-gibsoni-detection-during-experimental-infections.md), [CB509](articles/CB509_2015_clinical-haematological-cytokine-acute-phase-protein.md), [CB404](articles/CB404_2011_resolution-proteinuric-nephropathy-associated-babesia-gibsoni.md)).

**8. Reading an automatically categorised map.** The categories are unchecked, final NLP assignments. Three consequences follow:

- Off-topic and low-confidence primaries are common: 3 of 7 PUL primaries do not study the lung, 5 of 14 END primaries do not study vessels or perfusion, 48 of 59 SEV primaries are low confidence, and INF has 18 low-confidence labels. Category size (ERY 96, PUL 7) partly reflects the classifier.
- Key papers sit outside the expected category: [CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md), [CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md) and [CB147](articles/CB147_2026_liver-histopathology-naturally-acquired-babesia-rossi.md) are not END, the Onderstepoort coagulation, cytokine and renal studies are not SEV, and no *B. rossi* article is an NEU primary. Absence from a category is not absence of evidence.
- One paper recurs across categories: [CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md) (15 natural cases) is cited in six syntheses, [CB473](articles/CB473_2014_increased-concentration-serum-tnf-alpha-correlations.md) and [CB497](articles/CB497_2015_cardiac-lesions-babesiosis.md) in at least three. Cross-category agreement can be one study seen several times, and category counts must not be summed.

## Category synthesis

### ERY — Erythrocyte injury, haemolysis, anaemia and immune-mediated processes

**Question this direction asks**

How do *Babesia* infections reduce red-cell mass in dogs: direct parasite lysis, antibody-mediated destruction, macrophage and splenic clearance, or an inadequate marrow response? Which red-cell measures track severity and outcome, and do the answers differ by species?

**Evidence base**

96 primary articles, 2006-2026. The category assignment is automatic (NLP) and unchecked; see the [search log](search-log.md).
- Design: 50 descriptive series or surveys, 20 associational, 14 case reports (1-3 dogs), 8 narrative reviews, 2 prognostic cohorts ([CB067](articles/CB067_2020_analysis-risk-factors-associated-recurrence-babesiosis.md), [CB153](articles/CB153_2026_concurrent-babesia-gibsoni-infection-negative-prognostics.md)) and 2 experimental ([CB337](articles/CB337_2007_suppressive-effect-culture-supernatant-erythrocytes-serum.md), in vitro; [CB421](articles/CB421_2011_experimental-infection-babesia-microti.md), 2 dogs, no controls). The only other experimental dogs are the 5-dog arm of [CB115](articles/CB115_2023_regenerative-response-naturally-experimentally-infected-babesia.md).
- Species as named in the notes, often by smear only: *B. gibsoni* 27, *B. canis* 14, *B. vogeli* 6, *B. rossi* 4, two named species 7, other piroplasms 5, unnamed large *Babesia* 3, unspecified 22, plus the 8 reviews. All are dogs except the wolves of [CB005](articles/CB005_2017_prevalence-impact-babesia-canis-theileria-sp.md).
- 28 papers are round 4 (2006-2016). 52 of 96 are abstract-only, including 19 of the 28 round-4 papers.
- Independent sources: 92 (84 without the reviews). CH02 ([CB002](articles/CB002_2017_hematological-changes-affected-babesiosis.md), [CB015](articles/CB015_2017_clinicopathological-alterations-naturally-occurring-babesia-gibsoni.md)), CH05 ([CB178](articles/CB178_2019_electrophoretic-pattern-serum-proteins-babesiosis.md), [CB065](articles/CB065_2020_differences-serum-protein-electrophoretic-pattern-naturally.md)) and CH17 ([CB247](articles/CB247_2024_comparison-efficacy-different-therapeutic-regimen-treatment.md), [CB248](articles/CB248_2024_diagnosis-immune-mediated-haemolytic-anemia-affected.md), [CB257](articles/CB257_2024_haemato-biochemical-changes-affected-immune-mediated.md)) are one source each. Eleven other cohorts contribute one paper each.
- Looking off-topic for ERY, not reclassified: [CB005](articles/CB005_2017_prevalence-impact-babesia-canis-theileria-sp.md), [CB069](articles/CB069_2020_elevated-serum-manganese-concentration-possible-predisposing.md) (manganese in cerebral babesiosis), [CB351](articles/CB351_2008_unusual-form-babesiosis.md) (neurological signs), [CB361](articles/CB361_2009_possible-emergence-drug-resistant-variants-babesia.md) (atovaquone resistance), [CB265](articles/CB265_2025_male-fertility-impairment-associated-babesia-gibsoni.md) (semen quality), [CB127](articles/CB127_2024_molecular-prevalence-epidemiology-phylogenetic-analysis-babesia.md) (prevalence in healthy dogs).

Most series are small, smear-diagnosed regional papers with healthy-only controls or none.

**What has been studied**

*Syndromes.*
- **Anaemia is common, but thrombocytopenia is more consistent.** In 248 Warsaw dogs, RBC was low in 26.2% and Hct in 31.4%, against thrombocytopenia in 99.5% ([CB326](articles/CB326_2006_hematological-changes-during-course-babesiosis-caused.md), CH30, abstract only). In 113 smear-positive dogs, RBC was low in 62.8% and platelets in 100% ([CB432](articles/CB432_2012_clinic-laboratory-diagnosis-babesiosis-babesia-canis.md)). In 60 PCR-confirmed *B. gibsoni* dogs, 81.7% were anaemic, about half with PCV <20% ([CB086](articles/CB086_2021_clinical-characteristics-naturally-babesia-gibsoni-infected.md)).
- **Regeneration varies.** Anaemia was mildly to moderately regenerative in 86 uncomplicated *B. rossi* dogs ([CB418](articles/CB418_2011_serial-haematology-results-transfused-non-transfused.md)). A raised reticulocyte count occurred in only 5 of 113 dogs ([CB432](articles/CB432_2012_clinic-laboratory-diagnosis-babesiosis-babesia-canis.md)) but reticulocytosis in 13 of 21 ([CB164](articles/CB164_2017_anemia-description-babesia-spp-infected.md)). Analyser counts can mislead: 20 of 92 dogs with large-form babesiosis had abnormal reticulocyte profiles ([CB538](articles/CB538_2016_spurious-reticulocyte-profiles-large-form-babesiosis.md)), and one dog read 60 by analyser and 3.6 ×10^9/L manually ([CB540](articles/CB540_2016_spurious-reticulocyte-profiles-babesiosis.md)).
- **Secondary IMHA or Evans syndrome** is reported mainly in case reports ([CB495](articles/CB495_2015_diagnosis-treatment-immune-mediated-hemolytic-anemia.md), [CB250](articles/CB250_2024_anemia-hemol-tica-secund-ria-babesiose.md), [CB264](articles/CB264_2025_un-cas-clinique-classique-de-piroplasmose.md), [CB306](articles/CB306_2026_severe-erythrocyte-dysplasia-peripheral-blood-smears.md), [CB047](articles/CB047_2019_molecular-diagnosis-babesia-canis-infection-concurrent.md)). In [CB264](articles/CB264_2025_un-cas-clinique-classique-de-piroplasmose.md) it appeared on day 7 after imidocarb began.

*Proposed mechanisms (largely untested).*
- **Immune-mediated destruction:** Coombs-positive in 28/64 (43.8%) *B. gibsoni* dogs, saline agglutination in 32.8% and spherocytes in 28.1% ([CB248](articles/CB248_2024_diagnosis-immune-mediated-haemolytic-anemia-affected.md), CH17). *B. gibsoni* accounted for 75% of haemoparasite-associated IMHA in Mizoram ([CB235](articles/CB235_2023_first-report-immune-mediated-haemolytic-anaemiaassociated.md)). Neither separates autoantibody from antibody on parasitised cells.
- **Haemolysis and phagocytic clearance:** the urea:creatinine ratio was 41.35 in 25 babesiosis dogs against 13.6-15.36 in controls, IMHA and induced haemoglobinaemia, with normal GFR ([CB412](articles/CB412_2011_changes-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-babesiosis.md), CH29, abstract only), so haemoglobin load alone did not reproduce it. Necropsy of 5 dogs showed extravascular haemolysis ([CB522](articles/CB522_2016_septic-shock-similarities-differences-postmortem-findings.md)). Fatal *B. rossi* marrow had more CD204+ macrophages ([CB129](articles/CB129_2024_cytological-histopathological-bone-marrow-findings-natural.md), CH04), and two experimental *B. gibsoni* dogs had haemosiderin-laden splenic macrophages ([CB397](articles/CB397_2010_babesia-gibsoni-detection-blood-smears-formalin.md)).
- **Inadequate erythropoiesis:** *B. rossi* absolute reticulocyte count exceeded controls but was below IMHA dogs (p=0.041) and fell about 48 h after parasitaemia in 5 experimental dogs ([CB115](articles/CB115_2023_regenerative-response-naturally-experimentally-infected-babesia.md), CH09). Fatal cases showed dyserythropoiesis and more marrow iron ([CB129](articles/CB129_2024_cytological-histopathological-bone-marrow-findings-natural.md)). *B. canis* dogs were non-regenerative on admission and reticulocytotic by day 14 ([CB251](articles/CB251_2024_reticulocyte-response-paired-blood-samples-babesia.md)). Serum EPO was 87.0 vs 16.4 mU/mL in controls, but ~9% of infected dogs had low values ([CB297](articles/CB297_2026_serum-erythropoietin-levels-babesia-affected.md), CH20). Serum from *B. gibsoni*-infected dogs slowed canine reticulocyte maturation in vitro (3 infected and 4 healthy donors; [CB337](articles/CB337_2007_suppressive-effect-culture-supernatant-erythrocytes-serum.md)).
- **Membrane and oxidative injury:** poikilocytosis in 90.5% of 21 dogs ([CB164](articles/CB164_2017_anemia-description-babesia-spp-infected.md)) but 25% of 248 ([CB326](articles/CB326_2006_hematological-changes-during-course-babesiosis-caused.md)). Osmotic fragility was not raised in babesiosis ([CB225](articles/CB225_2023_association-increased-osmotic-fragility-red-blood.md), CH28) and was reduced in 13 transfusion-requiring dogs ([CB315](articles/CB315_2026_clinicopathological-evaluation-molecular-detection-haemoparasites-severely.md)). SEM normocytes were 77.3% vs about 99% in controls ([CB070](articles/CB070_2021_ultramicroscopic-erythrocytes-profile-component-babesiosis-pathogenesis.md), CH26).
- **Cytokines and host factors:** cytokine-driven haemolysis is review-level only ([CB236](articles/CB236_2023_unraveling-pathogenesis-anemia-babesiosis-exploring-role.md), [CB239](articles/CB239_2023_anemia-babesiosis-diagnosis-therapeutic-management.md)); neither ties its mechanisms to canine primary studies. DEA 1 type did not change PCR positivity (63.0% vs 60.3%; [CB143](articles/CB143_2025_relationship-between-erythrocyte-antigen-dea-susceptibility.md)).

*Severity and outcome markers (associations only).*
- Of 80 anaemic Nigerian dogs, 18 (22.5%) died, and non-survivors had lower PCV, Hb and RBC on univariate testing ([CB238](articles/CB238_2023_prevalence-role-anemia-mortalities-babesiosis.md)). Admission haematocrit was also lower in 8 non-survivors than 7 survivors ([CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md), CH21). In 320 *B. rossi* dogs no variable was an independent predictor of death; low T4, bilirubin, urea and cortisol discriminated best ([CB040](articles/CB040_2019_clinical-pathological-description-320-cases-naturally.md)).
- In IMHA, *B. gibsoni* co-infection predicted death (HR 2.6, p=0.024; 42 of 55 dogs died within 60 days), as did marked spherocytosis (HR 2.1) ([CB153](articles/CB153_2026_concurrent-babesia-gibsoni-infection-negative-prognostics.md)).
- Recurrence was 11.8%, but 28.7% with secondary IMHA ([CB067](articles/CB067_2020_analysis-risk-factors-associated-recurrence-babesiosis.md)).
- An LHD% cut-off of 1.83% gave 80.43% sensitivity and 100% specificity, against anaemia status, not an iron reference standard ([CB286](articles/CB286_2025_low-haemoglobin-density-anaemic-indicator-babesiosis.md), CH08).

**Convergence and conflict**

- **Cytopenias.** Thrombocytopenia (84.2% in [CB539](articles/CB539_2016_perfil-hematol-gico-de-c-es.md) up to 100%) is more consistent than anaemia (26.2% in [CB326](articles/CB326_2006_hematological-changes-during-course-babesiosis-caused.md) up to 82% in [CB099](articles/CB099_2022_occurrence-babesiosis-rhine-main-area-hesse.md)). Anaemia is common, not near-universal.
- **Marrow response.** The Onderstepoort *B. rossi* studies agree: red-cell responses weaker than the haemolysis predicts ([CB418](articles/CB418_2011_serial-haematology-results-transfused-non-transfused.md)), ARC below IMHA with an early experimental fall ([CB115](articles/CB115_2023_regenerative-response-naturally-experimentally-infected-babesia.md)) and dyserythropoiesis ([CB129](articles/CB129_2024_cytological-histopathological-bone-marrow-findings-natural.md)). These are separate cohorts from one group. *B. canis* ([CB251](articles/CB251_2024_reticulocyte-response-paired-blood-samples-babesia.md)) and non-regenerative *B. gibsoni* ([CB214](articles/CB214_2022_comparative-evaluation-darbepoetin-therapy-non-regenerative.md)) point the same way. But analyser artefact ([CB538](articles/CB538_2016_spurious-reticulocyte-profiles-large-form-babesiosis.md), [CB540](articles/CB540_2016_spurious-reticulocyte-profiles-babesiosis.md)) may overstate reticulocytes, and the notes do not say which analysers it affects.
- **Iron conflicts.** Transferrin saturation was low in *B. canis* ([CB177](articles/CB177_2018_changes-erythrocytopoesis-indices-babesiosis.md)) but raised in non-regenerative *B. gibsoni* ([CB214](articles/CB214_2022_comparative-evaluation-darbepoetin-therapy-non-regenerative.md)); fatal *B. rossi* marrow held more iron ([CB129](articles/CB129_2024_cytological-histopathological-bone-marrow-findings-natural.md)).
- **Parasite load.** In 342 *B. canis* dogs, parasitaemia and CRP correlated with haematological severity ([CB141](articles/CB141_2025_impact-levels-parasitemia-antibodies-acute-phase.md), CH12), and RBC, Hb and Hct were inversely related to parasitaemia in 18 cases ([CB459](articles/CB459_2013_correlation-between-hematological-parameters-babesia-spp.md)). In 7 *B. vogeli* dogs, load did not track cytopenias, although anaemia odds were raised (OR 11.8; [CB013](articles/CB013_2017_vector-borne-zoonotic-diseases-north-west.md)).
- **Spleen.** Severe disease followed splenectomy in experimental *B. vogeli* ([CB021](articles/CB021_2018_first-description-pathogenicity-babesia-vogeli-experimentally.md)) and in single cases of *B. canis* ([CB527](articles/CB527_2016_fatal-babesia-canis-canis-infection-splenectomized.md)), *B. vulpes* ([CB132](articles/CB132_2025_successful-treatment-severe-babesia-vulpes-infection.md)) and *B. gibsoni* ([CB377](articles/CB377_2009_clinicopathological-findings-molecular-detection-characterization-babesia.md)). Yet six splenectomised dogs with a large unnamed *Babesia* had only mild anaemia ([CB382](articles/CB382_2010_babesiosis-caused-large-babesia-species-7.md)), as did a *B. vogeli* isolate in 2 splenectomised dogs ([CB339](articles/CB339_2007_parasitological-molecular-characterization-venezuelan-isolate-babesia.md)).
- **Anaemia and organ injury.** Older studies found no difference between anaemic and non-anaemic dogs in urea and creatinine ([CB365](articles/CB365_2009_influence-anaemia-azotaemia-infected-babesia-canis.md), 127 dogs), AST/ALT ([CB424](articles/CB424_2012_increased-ast-alt-ratio-azotaemic-infected.md), 182 dogs, CH34) or MDA ([CB394](articles/CB394_2010_malondialdehyde-levels-serum-infected-babesia-canis.md), CH33). Anaemia grade is not a stand-in for organ injury.
- **Species.** Immune-mediated evidence comes almost entirely from *B. gibsoni* in Indian referral populations, with CH17 ([CB247](articles/CB247_2024_comparison-efficacy-different-therapeutic-regimen-treatment.md), [CB248](articles/CB248_2024_diagnosis-immune-mediated-haemolytic-anemia-affected.md), [CB257](articles/CB257_2024_haemato-biochemical-changes-affected-immune-mediated.md)) one source, not three. *B. vogeli* is mild in intact experimental dogs ([CB021](articles/CB021_2018_first-description-pathogenicity-babesia-vogeli-experimentally.md), [CB339](articles/CB339_2007_parasitological-molecular-characterization-venezuelan-isolate-babesia.md)) and small field series ([CB048](articles/CB048_2020_molecular-detection-hematological-changes-naturally-infected.md) and [CB131](articles/CB131_2025_molecular-detection-hematological-alterations-infected-babesia.md) have 9 and 4 positive dogs).

**Unresolved questions**

- Does DAT positivity in *B. gibsoni* reflect autoantibody? Test eluate specificity and persistence after PCR clearance, in dogs not selected for IMHA ([CB248](articles/CB248_2024_diagnosis-immune-mediated-haemolytic-anemia-affected.md), [CB235](articles/CB235_2023_first-report-immune-mediated-haemolytic-anaemiaassociated.md), [CB153](articles/CB153_2026_concurrent-babesia-gibsoni-infection-negative-prognostics.md)).
- Is the early reticulocyte fall caused by a circulating factor ([CB337](articles/CB337_2007_suppressive-effect-culture-supernatant-erythrocytes-serum.md), [CB115](articles/CB115_2023_regenerative-response-naturally-experimentally-infected-babesia.md))? Measure ARC (manual check), EPO, hepcidin and marrow cytology serially in experimental infection.
- Is erythropoiesis iron-restricted? Ferritin, hepcidin, CHr and marrow iron in the same dogs could reconcile [CB177](articles/CB177_2018_changes-erythrocytopoesis-indices-babesiosis.md), [CB214](articles/CB214_2022_comparative-evaluation-darbepoetin-therapy-non-regenerative.md) and [CB129](articles/CB129_2024_cytological-histopathological-bone-marrow-findings-natural.md).
- Does spleen status set severity? Challenge intact and splenectomised dogs with one isolate ([CB021](articles/CB021_2018_first-description-pathogenicity-babesia-vogeli-experimentally.md), [CB339](articles/CB339_2007_parasitological-molecular-characterization-venezuelan-isolate-babesia.md) and [CB382](articles/CB382_2010_babesiosis-caused-large-babesia-species-7.md) disagree).
- Does quantitative parasite load predict anaemia after adjusting for species and co-infection ([CB141](articles/CB141_2025_impact-levels-parasitemia-antibodies-acute-phase.md) against [CB013](articles/CB013_2017_vector-borne-zoonotic-diseases-north-west.md))?
- Do spherocytosis or DAT add prognostic value beyond admission PCV in cohorts not selected for IMHA ([CB040](articles/CB040_2019_clinical-pathological-description-320-cases-naturally.md), [CB153](articles/CB153_2026_concurrent-babesia-gibsoni-infection-negative-prognostics.md))?

**Data available**

- **[DS03](datasets.md#ds03)** ([CB115](articles/CB115_2023_regenerative-response-naturally-experimentally-infected-babesia.md)), public: per-sample ARC and 11 other reticulocyte and haematology variables, 139 animals, with longitudinal experimental dogs.
- **[S-CB013](datasets.md#s-cb013)**, public: per-dog PCV, platelets and *Babesia*/*Anaplasma* Ct, 96 dogs (7 *B. vogeli*).
- **[DS05](datasets.md#ds05)** ([CB126](articles/CB126_2024_prevalence-co-infections-ehrlichia-spp-or.md)), public: 150 *B. rossi* and *B. vogeli* dogs, 13-variable CBC with signalment.
- **[S-CB152](datasets.md#s-cb152)**, public: aggregate sampling locations and case counts only.
- **[S-CB005](datasets.md#s-cb005)**, public: per-wolf PCR and cause of death, 109 Croatian wolves (not dog data).
- **From secondary-tagged notes, public:** [DS04](datasets.md#ds04) and [S-CB147](datasets.md#s-cb147) (haematology and liver histology, 10 fatal *B. rossi* dogs, 4 controls; CH04); [S-CB148](datasets.md#s-cb148) and [S-CB106](datasets.md#s-cb106) (summary laboratory statistics, 23 *B. canis* dogs; PCR follow-up of 9); [S-CB042](datasets.md#s-cb042) (48 *B. vulpes* dogs); [S-CB516](datasets.md#s-cb516) (15 *B. canis* cases); [S-CB091](datasets.md#s-cb091), [S-CB038](datasets.md#s-cb038) and [S-CB139](datasets.md#s-cb139) (omics or summary statistics only).
- **Request-only:** RQ-[CB065](articles/CB065_2020_differences-serum-protein-electrophoretic-pattern-naturally.md) (57 dogs), RQ-[CB134](articles/CB134_2025_clinical-clinicopathological-features-108-infected-babesia.md) (108), RQ-[CB143](articles/CB143_2025_relationship-between-erythrocyte-antigen-dea-susceptibility.md) (124), RQ-[CB238](articles/CB238_2023_prevalence-role-anemia-mortalities-babesiosis.md) (103), RQ-[CB031](articles/CB031_2019_seroprevalence-ehrlichiosis-microscopic-screening-babesiosis-harare.md) (56).

### INF — Inflammation, innate/adaptive immunity and immunopathology

**Question this direction asks**

What systemic and tissue host response accompanies canine babesiosis, how does it vary with species, severity, treatment and prior exposure, and does it drive organ dysfunction or merely accompany it? Most studies measure. Only experiments can order events in time, and almost none test causation.

**Evidence base**

- **Assignment.** 91 primary articles (2006–2026), 35 from round 4 (2006–2016). The category is assigned automatically by an NLP classifier, with no human review. 18 assignments are low confidence. Several look off-topic for INF because they mainly report treatment, prevalence or isolate characterisation: [CB336](articles/CB336_2007_possible-treatment-strategy-clinical-factors-estimate.md), [CB339](articles/CB339_2007_parasitological-molecular-characterization-venezuelan-isolate-babesia.md), [CB406](articles/CB406_2011_clinical-haemato-biochemical-therapeutic-studies-babesiosis.md), [CB442](articles/CB442_2013_babesiosis-treatment-three-different-medicines.md), [CB445](articles/CB445_2013_ehrlichiosis-babesiosis-anaplasmosis-hepatozoonosis-st-kitts.md), [CB295](articles/CB295_2026_diagnosis-therapeutic-management-babesia-gibsoni-induced.md). They are kept as assigned.
- **Design.** 9 narrative reviews (none with a reproducible search), 7 case reports, 11 experimental infection or vaccination papers (8 in round 4), 3 necropsy papers, 61 natural-infection studies (4 proteomic). Seven compare survivors with non-survivors: [CB376](articles/CB376_2009_c-reactive-protein-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), [CB468](articles/CB468_2014_concentrations-infl-ammatory-markers-amino-terminal.md), [CB486](articles/CB486_2014_serum-cortisol-insulin-concentrations-naturally-infected.md), [CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md), [CB037](articles/CB037_2019_disease-severity-blood-cytokine-concentrations-natural.md), [CB050](articles/CB050_2020_neutrophil-myeloperoxidase-index-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), [CB202](articles/CB202_2020_serum-hepcidin-clinical-prognostic-marker-discriminate.md).
- **Species.** *B. canis* 39, *B. rossi* 12, *B. gibsoni* 13, *B. vogeli* 7, *B. vulpes* 1, mixed or unidentified 10, plus 9 reviews. Round 4: 16 *B. canis*, 2 *B. rossi*, 5 *B. gibsoni*, 2 *B. vogeli*, 6 mixed or unidentified, 4 reviews.
- **Abstract-only:** 46 of 91, including 23 of 35 round-4 papers, so many older numbers lack group sizes.
- **Independence.** 81 independent sources, 9 of them reviews. Shared cohorts: CH03 ([CB008](articles/CB008_2017_flow-cytometric-assessment-lymphocyte-immunophenotypes-naturally.md), [CB050](articles/CB050_2020_neutrophil-myeloperoxidase-index-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), [CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md)), CH04 ([CB054](articles/CB054_2020_pathology-spleen-lethal-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), [CB147](articles/CB147_2026_liver-histopathology-naturally-acquired-babesia-rossi.md)), CH07 ([CB073](articles/CB073_2021_systemic-inflammatory-response-syndrome-naturally-infected.md), [CB089](articles/CB089_2022_seasonal-differences-intensity-acute-phase-response.md), [CB123](articles/CB123_2024_age-sex-breed-effect-laboratory-parameters.md)), CH09 ([CB082](articles/CB082_2021_experimental-babesia-rossi-infection-induces-hemolytic.md), [CB098](articles/CB098_2022_kinetics-inflammatory-response-during-experimental-babesia.md), [CB101](articles/CB101_2022_dataset-inflammatory-response-during-experimental-infection.md)), CH12 ([CB141](articles/CB141_2025_impact-levels-parasitemia-antibodies-acute-phase.md), [CB149](articles/CB149_2026_retrospective-analysis-anamnesis-clinical-signs-therapy.md)), CH23 ([CB017](articles/CB017_2018_mcp-1-kc-like-il-8.md), [CB510](articles/CB510_2015_serum-levels-chemokines-keratinocyte-chemoattractant-interleukin.md), [CB511](articles/CB511_2015_razine-kemokina-kc-i-il-8.md)). [CB510](articles/CB510_2015_serum-levels-chemokines-keratinocyte-chemoattractant-interleukin.md) and [CB511](articles/CB511_2015_razine-kemokina-kc-i-il-8.md) are one study published twice. Overlap is possible but unstated between CH07 and [CB057](articles/CB057_2020_evidence-acute-phase-reaction-asymptomatic-naturally.md)/CB233 (CH28). Peer review is unverified for several small-journal papers (for example [CB255](articles/CB255_2024_clinico-pathological-evaluation-babesia-gibsoni-associatedrenal.md), [CB281](articles/CB281_2025_autoimmune-hemolytic-anemia-secondary-hemoparasitic-infections.md)). See the [search log](search-log.md).

**What has been studied**

*Syndromes.*
- **Acute-phase response (APR).** Seen in natural *B. canis* in Croatia ([CB323](articles/CB323_2006_evidence-acute-phase-response-naturally-infected.md), 2006), Türkiye ([CB058](articles/CB058_2020_serum-amyloid-haptoglobin-ceruloplasmin-albumin-levels.md)), Thailand ([CB081](articles/CB081_2021_characterization-serum-protein-electrophoresis-patterns-c.md)), Slovakia ([CB222](articles/CB222_2022_evaluation-acute-phase-proteins-suffering-various.md)) and Germany ([CB133](articles/CB133_2025_apolipoprotein-1-does-not-appear-be.md)). In [CB222](articles/CB222_2022_evaluation-acute-phase-proteins-suffering-various.md) it was the smallest of four diseases, yet SAA and CRP were about 120-fold and 23-fold above controls. It is also present in PCR-positive asymptomatic carriers ([CB057](articles/CB057_2020_evidence-acute-phase-reaction-asymptomatic-naturally.md)).
- **SIRS.** 59% of 54 *B. canis* dogs ([CB073](articles/CB073_2021_systemic-inflammatory-response-syndrome-naturally-infected.md)); 68% of 332, MODS in 10% ([CB399](articles/CB399_2010_systemic-inflammatory-response-syndrome-multiple-organ.md), secondary).
- **Spleen and macrophages.** White and red pulp merged and macrophages expanded in 9 fatal *B. rossi* cases ([CB054](articles/CB054_2020_pathology-spleen-lethal-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), CH04); Kupffer cell and mononuclear change in the liver ([CB147](articles/CB147_2026_liver-histopathology-naturally-acquired-babesia-rossi.md)). Splenomegaly with rupture in an asymptomatic dog ([CB348](articles/CB348_2008_asymptomatic-babesiosis-cause-splenomegaly-splenectomy.md)); haemophagocytosis after splenectomy in one dog ([CB132](articles/CB132_2025_successful-treatment-severe-babesia-vulpes-infection.md)).
- Brain, lung and thromboinflammation: secondary notes only ([CB189](articles/CB189_2020_encephalic-babesiosis-clinical-epidemiological-pathological-aspects.md), [CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md)).

*Proposed mechanisms (largely untested).*
- **Stereotyped inflammatory programme.** In experimental *B. canis*, dose shifted the timing of CRP, fibrinogen and platelet curves but not their shape, and CRP rose before parasites appeared ([CB370](articles/CB370_2009_systemic-inflammatory-responses-experimentally-infected-babesia.md)). Experimental *B. gibsoni* gave the same CRP-first order, with cytokines later ([CB509](articles/CB509_2015_clinical-haematological-cytokine-acute-phase-protein.md)). In *B. rossi*, inflammation preceded signs and treatment transiently amplified it ([CB098](articles/CB098_2022_kinetics-inflammatory-response-during-experimental-babesia.md)).
- **Trigger.** Soluble parasite antigens did not activate kallikrein directly. CRP also rose after infusion of normal red cell stroma, read as a self-molecule contribution ([CB401](articles/CB401_2010_soluble-parasite-antigens-babesia-canis-do.md)).
- **Cytokine-driven injury.** A "cytokine storm" ([CB037](articles/CB037_2019_disease-severity-blood-cytokine-concentrations-natural.md)). TNF-α and IFN-γ rose in *B. vogeli*, confounded by *E. canis* ([CB075](articles/CB075_2021_prevalence-genetic-biochemical-evaluation-immune-response.md), [CB298](articles/CB298_2026_circulating-babesia-sp-ehrlichia-sp-egyptian.md)). IFN-γ/SOCS3 mRNA in severe *B. canis* ([CB109](articles/CB109_2023_haematological-indices-immune-response-profiles-naturally.md)). PGE2 stayed high after therapy in MODS but not SIRS ([CB470](articles/CB470_2014_serum-concentrations-eicosanoids-lipids-naturally-infected.md), abstract).
- **Lymphocyte depression.** CD3+, CD4+, CD8+ and CD21+ were lower than in controls in 10 *B. canis* dogs and rose after imidocarb ([CB490](articles/CB490_2015_changes-selected-subpopulations-lymphocytes-infected-babesia.md)). CD3/CD4 were lowest in complicated *B. rossi* ([CB008](articles/CB008_2017_flow-cytometric-assessment-lymphocyte-immunophenotypes-naturally.md)). Proportions only.
- **Immune-mediated anaemia.** Erythrocyte-bound IgG/IgM in 4 of 6 *B. c. vogeli* and 0 of 24 *B. c. canis* dogs ([CB369](articles/CB369_2009_detection-erythrocyte-binding-igm-igg-flow.md), CH32). Antibodies to *B. gibsoni* Hsp70 cross-reacted with canine Hsp70, which was not on the red cell surface ([CB413](articles/CB413_2011_detection-anti-babesia-gibsoni-heat-shock.md)).
- **Gelatinases.** Low proMMP-2/-9 tracked cytopenias ([CB084](articles/CB084_2021_low-serum-levels-promatrix-metalloproteinase-2.md)).
- **Acquired immunity.** 31% of healthy endemic-area dogs were *B. rossi* seropositive ([CB052](articles/CB052_2020_long-term-follow-up-owned-free.md)). Antibody level correlated inversely with parasitaemia (rho −0.666) and a haematological severity score (−0.652) in CH12 ([CB141](articles/CB141_2025_impact-levels-parasitemia-antibodies-acute-phase.md)), though prior exposure is confounded. Young dogs were mostly seronegative with higher parasitaemia ([CB123](articles/CB123_2024_age-sex-breed-effect-laboratory-parameters.md)). Soluble-antigen vaccines protected against disease even where *B. canis* parasitaemia stayed unchanged ([CB375](articles/CB375_2009_comparison-babesia-rossi-babesia-canis-isolates.md), review).

*Severity and outcome markers (associations only).*
- **Positive.** IL-6 and MCP-1 were higher in the 12 *B. rossi* non-survivors ([CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md)); IL-6, MCP-1 and TNF-α in 9 ([CB037](articles/CB037_2019_disease-severity-blood-cytokine-concentrations-natural.md)). MCP-1 and IL-7 separated complicated *B. canis* (AUC 0.906, 0.896, unvalidated; [CB017](articles/CB017_2018_mcp-1-kc-like-il-8.md)). KC-like and IL-8 were higher in complicated disease ([CB510](articles/CB510_2015_serum-levels-chemokines-keratinocyte-chemoattractant-interleukin.md), abstract). Hepcidin gave AUC 0.984, confounded by species ([CB202](articles/CB202_2020_serum-hepcidin-clinical-prognostic-marker-discriminate.md)).
- **Negative or mixed.** Admission CRP did not differ (107.5 vs 122.1 mg/l, 57 survivors vs 11 non-survivors; [CB376](articles/CB376_2009_c-reactive-protein-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md)), nor did CRP or SAA in [CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md). Procalcitonin separated infected from healthy dogs but not survivors; NT-pCNP did the reverse ([CB468](articles/CB468_2014_concentrations-infl-ammatory-markers-amino-terminal.md), abstract). MPXI exceeded controls in non-survivors but was not compared with survivors ([CB050](articles/CB050_2020_neutrophil-myeloperoxidase-index-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md)). Cortisol and insulin were reported to predict mortality ([CB486](articles/CB486_2014_serum-cortisol-insulin-concentrations-naturally-infected.md), abstract).
- **Proteomics (hypothesis-generating).** [CB476](articles/CB476_2014_identification-serum-biomarkers-naturally-infected-babesia.md) (pooled 2DE), [CB528](articles/CB528_2016_plasma-biomarkers-sirs-mods-associated-babesiosis.md) (five dogs per group; lower complement inhibitors in MODS), [CB112](articles/CB112_2023_serum-proteome-profiling-naturally-acquired-babesia.md), [CB019](articles/CB019_2018_lc-ms-ms-analysis-serum-phosphoproteome.md).

**Convergence and conflict**

- **Agreement.** The APR recurs across countries. Experiments in *B. canis*, *B. gibsoni* and *B. rossi* all show inflammation before or alongside parasitaemia. This extends the earlier *B. rossi*-only (CH09) finding to two more species. In *B. rossi*, CRP failed to separate outcome in two sources ([CB376](articles/CB376_2009_c-reactive-protein-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), 2009; [CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md)) while cytokines did ([CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md), [CB037](articles/CB037_2019_disease-severity-blood-cytokine-concentrations-natural.md)); MCP-1 also tracks severity in *B. canis* ([CB017](articles/CB017_2018_mcp-1-kc-like-il-8.md)). T-cell depression appears in *B. canis* ([CB490](articles/CB490_2015_changes-selected-subpopulations-lymphocytes-infected-babesia.md)) and *B. rossi* ([CB008](articles/CB008_2017_flow-cytometric-assessment-lymphocyte-immunophenotypes-naturally.md)), and lymphopenia in 82% of 34 PCR-confirmed dogs ([CB100](articles/CB100_2022_clinical-hematologic-findings-babesia-canis-infection.md)).
- **IL-8.** Lower in infected *B. rossi* and falling with severity ([CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md), [CB037](articles/CB037_2019_disease-severity-blood-cytokine-concentrations-natural.md)); higher in complicated *B. canis* ([CB510](articles/CB510_2015_serum-levels-chemokines-keratinocyte-chemoattractant-interleukin.md), [CB017](articles/CB017_2018_mcp-1-kc-like-il-8.md)). Species, assay and timing differ, so the contrast is unproven.
- **Leukogram.** SIRS went with leukopenia ([CB073](articles/CB073_2021_systemic-inflammatory-response-syndrome-naturally-infected.md)), but complicated disease went with leukocytosis in [CB355](articles/CB355_2008_case-controlled-retrospective-study-causes-implications.md) (35 x 10^9/L or more; species unstated) and [CB510](articles/CB510_2015_serum-levels-chemokines-keratinocyte-chemoattractant-interleukin.md). Case definitions probably explain this.
- **Serum proteins.** Alpha-1 globulin was reduced in 54.8% of dogs in [CB405](articles/CB405_2011_abnormalities-serum-proteins-course-babesiosis.md) but alpha globulins were higher in young dogs in [CB123](articles/CB123_2024_age-sex-breed-effect-laboratory-parameters.md). Haptoglobin was low ([CB058](articles/CB058_2020_serum-amyloid-haptoglobin-ceruloplasmin-albumin-levels.md)), unchanged ([CB232](articles/CB232_2023_effect-different-diseases-hepatozoon-canis-distemper.md)) or rose then fell after treatment ([CB323](articles/CB323_2006_evidence-acute-phase-response-naturally-infected.md)), so timing may matter. ApoA-I was higher in SIRS ([CB528](articles/CB528_2016_plasma-biomarkers-sirs-mods-associated-babesiosis.md)), unchanged in [CB133](articles/CB133_2025_apolipoprotein-1-does-not-appear-be.md).
- **Antibody-mediated haemolysis.** Present in *B. c. vogeli* ([CB369](articles/CB369_2009_detection-erythrocyte-binding-igm-igg-flow.md)), unsupported for *B. gibsoni* Hsp70 ([CB413](articles/CB413_2011_detection-anti-babesia-gibsoni-heat-shock.md)). Case reports of immune-mediated anaemia lack confirmatory tests.
- **Mortality.** The earlier contrast of *B. rossi* (11% [CB050](articles/CB050_2020_neutrophil-myeloperoxidase-index-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md); 12% [CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md)) against 1 of 31 *B. canis* ([CB017](articles/CB017_2018_mcp-1-kc-like-il-8.md)) narrows: 6.7% in 342 German dogs ([CB149](articles/CB149_2026_retrospective-analysis-anamnesis-clinical-signs-therapy.md)), 6% in [CB100](articles/CB100_2022_clinical-hematologic-findings-babesia-canis-infection.md). Virulence differences remain argued ([CB415](articles/CB415_2011_why-southern-african-babesiosis-so-virulent.md)), but referral populations differ.

**Unresolved questions**

- Do IL-6 and MCP-1 at admission predict death in an independent *B. rossi* cohort beyond age and parasitaemia, where CRP and SAA do not?
- Is the IL-8 contrast between *B. canis* and *B. rossi* real? Use one multiplex assay, pre-treatment sampling, neutrophil counts and MPXI in both species.
- Does treatment cause a CRP and cytokine surge in natural infection? [CB017](articles/CB017_2018_mcp-1-kc-like-il-8.md) sampled 6–8 h after imidocarb; compare pre-treatment with 6–24 h.
- Is T- and B-cell depression ([CB490](articles/CB490_2015_changes-selected-subpopulations-lymphocytes-infected-babesia.md), [CB008](articles/CB008_2017_flow-cytometric-assessment-lymphocyte-immunophenotypes-naturally.md)) apoptosis, redistribution or loss? It needs absolute counts and function tests.
- Does one SIRS definition at a fixed sampling day reconcile leukopenic SIRS ([CB073](articles/CB073_2021_systemic-inflammatory-response-syndrome-naturally-infected.md)) with leukocytosis in complicated disease ([CB355](articles/CB355_2008_case-controlled-retrospective-study-causes-implications.md), [CB510](articles/CB510_2015_serum-levels-chemokines-keratinocyte-chemoattractant-interleukin.md))?
- Does admission antibody status predict milder disease independently of age, parasite load and travel history ([CB141](articles/CB141_2025_impact-levels-parasitemia-antibodies-acute-phase.md), [CB123](articles/CB123_2024_age-sex-breed-effect-laboratory-parameters.md))?
- Do erythrocyte-bound IgG/IgM occur in natural *B. rossi* and *B. gibsoni* infection, and track the haemoglobin fall ([CB369](articles/CB369_2009_detection-erythrocyte-binding-igm-igg-flow.md), [CB413](articles/CB413_2011_detection-anti-babesia-gibsoni-heat-shock.md))?

**Data available**

- [DS01](datasets.md#ds01), [DS02](datasets.md#ds02) (CH09): status 1; 6 beagles, per-dog clinical, CBC, CRP and cytokines over 8 days.
- [DS06](datasets.md#ds06), [S-CB082](datasets.md#s-cb082) (CH09): status 1; whole-blood RNA-seq counts (12,362 genes, 28 samples); supplementary tables.
- [S-CB017](datasets.md#s-cb017): status 1; 47 dogs, 9 cytokines plus haematology, days 1 and 7.
- [S-CB517](datasets.md#s-cb517): status 1; 112 dogs, leukocytes, SAA, CRP, 7 cytokines. [S-CB050](datasets.md#s-cb050): status 1; 161 rows, cytokines, MPXI, outcome; its first cohort is the [CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md) cohort (CH03). RQ-[CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md) is request-only for the same dogs.
- [S-CB476](datasets.md#s-cb476): status 1; protein list only. [DS09](datasets.md#ds09): status 1; processed phosphoproteome, 20 dogs. [DS07](datasets.md#ds07), [S-CB112](datasets.md#s-cb112): status 1; *B. rossi* serum proteome, 52 dogs.
- [DS04](datasets.md#ds04), [S-CB147](datasets.md#s-cb147): status 1; liver histology scores and immunohistochemistry counts, 14 dogs (CH04).
- [S-CB130](datasets.md#s-cb130), [S-CB133](datasets.md#s-cb133): status 1; summary statistics only.
- Request-only, unverified: RQ-[CB222](articles/CB222_2022_evaluation-acute-phase-proteins-suffering-various.md), RQ-[CB109](articles/CB109_2023_haematological-indices-immune-response-profiles-naturally.md), RQ-[CB065](articles/CB065_2020_differences-serum-protein-electrophoretic-pattern-naturally.md).
- Via secondary tags: [S-CB516](datasets.md#s-cb516) (status 1; 15 natural *B. canis* cases, individual descriptions) and [DS05](datasets.md#ds05) (status 1; 150 dogs, admission haematology and biochemistry).

### END — Endothelium, microcirculation, perfusion and tissue hypoxia

**Question this direction asks**

Is organ dysfunction in canine babesiosis driven by microvascular events (sequestration, endothelial activation or injury, vascular leak, hypovolaemic or distributive shock, tissue hypoxia) rather than by parasite burden or anaemia alone? Can hypoperfusion be measured ante-mortem and linked to outcome?

**Evidence base**

The category assignment is automatic (NLP classifier, no human review). The base is 14 primary articles (2009-2025; 4 from round 4, 2006-2016) and 35 secondary-tagged articles (15 from round 4). Search methods are in the [search log](search-log.md).

Primary articles by design:
- 6 case reports or very small series (n=1 to 4): [CB454](articles/CB454_2013_generalized-alopecia-vasculitis-like-changes-babesiosis.md), [CB182](articles/CB182_2019_histopathologial-alterations-babesia-gibsoni-infectiona-case.md), [CB066](articles/CB066_2020_babesiosis-disease-rarely-considered-context-male.md), [CB140](articles/CB140_2025_fatal-coinfection-hepatozoon-canis-babesia-vogeli.md), [CB271](articles/CB271_2025_diagnosis-management-splenic-torsion-cane-corso.md), [CB283](articles/CB283_2025_vasculitis-associated-ehrlichia-canis-babesia-canis.md).
- 2 histopathology series: [CB087](articles/CB087_2021_morphofunctional-changes-spleen-components-pathogenetic-substantiation.md) and [CB208](articles/CB208_2021_role-significance-morphological-changes-intraorgan-vessels.md) (CH26).
- 1 each: retrospective case series ([CB372](articles/CB372_2009_septic-shock-babesiosis.md), CH33); blood-pressure case-control ([CB487](articles/CB487_2014_arterial-blood-pressure-values-naturally-infected.md)); clinicopathological case-control ([CB022](articles/CB022_2018_haemato-biochemical-changes-infected-babesia-sp.md)); infusion comparison ([CB200](articles/CB200_2020_plasma-replacement-solutions-intensive-care-unit.md), CH13); proteomic case-control ([CB122](articles/CB122_2024_characterization-lc-ms-ms-based-proteomic.md)); experimental method study in 2 beagles ([CB397](articles/CB397_2010_babesia-gibsoni-detection-blood-smears-formalin.md)).

By evidence type, 12 are descriptive and 2 associational ([CB487](articles/CB487_2014_arterial-blood-pressure-values-naturally-infected.md), [CB122](articles/CB122_2024_characterization-lc-ms-ms-based-proteomic.md)). None is mechanistic or prognostic.

By species: 4 B. canis ([CB487](articles/CB487_2014_arterial-blood-pressure-values-naturally-infected.md), [CB066](articles/CB066_2020_babesiosis-disease-rarely-considered-context-male.md), [CB122](articles/CB122_2024_characterization-lc-ms-ms-based-proteomic.md), [CB283](articles/CB283_2025_vasculitis-associated-ehrlichia-canis-babesia-canis.md)), 4 B. gibsoni ([CB397](articles/CB397_2010_babesia-gibsoni-detection-blood-smears-formalin.md), [CB454](articles/CB454_2013_generalized-alopecia-vasculitis-like-changes-babesiosis.md), [CB182](articles/CB182_2019_histopathologial-alterations-babesia-gibsoni-infectiona-case.md); [CB022](articles/CB022_2018_haemato-biochemical-changes-infected-babesia-sp.md) suspected), 1 B. vogeli inside a four-pathogen co-infection ([CB140](articles/CB140_2025_fatal-coinfection-hepatozoon-canis-babesia-vogeli.md)), and 5 unspecified ([CB372](articles/CB372_2009_septic-shock-babesiosis.md), [CB200](articles/CB200_2020_plasma-replacement-solutions-intensive-care-unit.md), [CB087](articles/CB087_2021_morphofunctional-changes-spleen-components-pathogenetic-substantiation.md), [CB208](articles/CB208_2021_role-significance-morphological-changes-intraorgan-vessels.md), [CB271](articles/CB271_2025_diagnosis-management-splenic-torsion-cane-corso.md)). [CB208](articles/CB208_2021_role-significance-morphological-changes-intraorgan-vessels.md) pools dogs with wild rodents. No primary article is on B. rossi.

Ten are abstract-only ([CB372](articles/CB372_2009_septic-shock-babesiosis.md), [CB487](articles/CB487_2014_arterial-blood-pressure-values-naturally-infected.md), [CB182](articles/CB182_2019_histopathologial-alterations-babesia-gibsoni-infectiona-case.md), [CB200](articles/CB200_2020_plasma-replacement-solutions-intensive-care-unit.md), [CB087](articles/CB087_2021_morphofunctional-changes-spleen-components-pathogenetic-substantiation.md), [CB208](articles/CB208_2021_role-significance-morphological-changes-intraorgan-vessels.md), [CB122](articles/CB122_2024_characterization-lc-ms-ms-based-proteomic.md), [CB140](articles/CB140_2025_fatal-coinfection-hepatozoon-canis-babesia-vogeli.md), [CB271](articles/CB271_2025_diagnosis-management-splenic-torsion-cane-corso.md), [CB283](articles/CB283_2025_vasculitis-associated-ehrlichia-canis-babesia-canis.md)); four have full text. Peer review of [CB200](articles/CB200_2020_plasma-replacement-solutions-intensive-care-unit.md) and [CB208](articles/CB208_2021_role-significance-morphological-changes-intraorgan-vessels.md) was not verified.

There are 13 independent sources. [CB087](articles/CB087_2021_morphofunctional-changes-spleen-components-pathogenetic-substantiation.md) and [CB208](articles/CB208_2021_role-significance-morphological-changes-intraorgan-vessels.md) are one source (CH26). [CB200](articles/CB200_2020_plasma-replacement-solutions-intensive-care-unit.md) (CH13) and [CB372](articles/CB372_2009_septic-shock-babesiosis.md) (CH33) have cohort partners outside this category, so none of these is replication.

Five primary articles have low NLP confidence ([CB372](articles/CB372_2009_septic-shock-babesiosis.md), [CB397](articles/CB397_2010_babesia-gibsoni-detection-blood-smears-formalin.md), [CB087](articles/CB087_2021_morphofunctional-changes-spleen-components-pathogenetic-substantiation.md), [CB208](articles/CB208_2021_role-significance-morphological-changes-intraorgan-vessels.md), [CB271](articles/CB271_2025_diagnosis-management-splenic-torsion-cane-corso.md)). Five look off-topic for END, because their main finding does not concern vessels or perfusion: [CB022](articles/CB022_2018_haemato-biochemical-changes-infected-babesia-sp.md) (haematology), [CB066](articles/CB066_2020_babesiosis-disease-rarely-considered-context-male.md) (infertility), [CB140](articles/CB140_2025_fatal-coinfection-hepatozoon-canis-babesia-vogeli.md) (co-infection), [CB271](articles/CB271_2025_diagnosis-management-splenic-torsion-cane-corso.md) (splenic torsion) and [CB397](articles/CB397_2010_babesia-gibsoni-detection-blood-smears-formalin.md) (probe method).

No primary article measured endothelial function, microcirculatory flow or tissue oxygenation. The secondary set adds arterial pressure, lactate and central venous PO2.

**What has been studied**

*Syndromes.*
- **Shock and hypotension:** in [CB372](articles/CB372_2009_septic-shock-babesiosis.md) (Zagreb, 2007), 10 of 86 babesiosis admissions met septic-shock criteria, which included hypotension unresponsive to treatment. Kidney was involved in 9, and all 10 died within 1 to 4 days, against 2.6% mortality in the other cases. In [CB487](articles/CB487_2014_arterial-blood-pressure-values-naturally-infected.md) (B. canis; 12 complicated, 18 uncomplicated, 20 healthy) mean arterial pressure was 60 in complicated cases, 116 in uncomplicated and 106 in healthy dogs. [CB463](articles/CB463_2014_association-between-decreased-blood-pressure-azotaemia.md) found MAP below 80 mmHg in 7 of 48 infected dogs. [CB200](articles/CB200_2020_plasma-replacement-solutions-intensive-care-unit.md) (CH13, species unspecified) reports a circulating-volume deficit of about 30%, a shock index about twice normal and more erythrocyte and platelet aggregation. [CB220](articles/CB220_2022_features-hepatopathy-hematological-complications-acute-spontaneous.md) reports deficits of 24% (anaemic form) and 34% (icteric form).
- **Oxygen delivery:** in [CB368](articles/CB368_2009_prospective-randomized-comparison-oxyglobin-hb-200.md) (B. rossi, 12 anaemic dogs) tachycardia and low central venous PO2 were present on admission. Oxyglobin and red-cell transfusion gave broadly similar results over 72 hours.
- **Post-mortem and histology:**
  - [CB341](articles/CB341_2007_histological-ultrastructural-studies-renal-lesions-babesia.md) (B. canis, 8 fatal cases): tubular necrosis and degeneration of glomerular and interstitial capillary endothelium.
  - [CB497](articles/CB497_2015_cardiac-lesions-babesiosis.md) and [CB522](articles/CB522_2016_septic-shock-similarities-differences-postmortem-findings.md) (CH38, B. canis): haemorrhage in 12 of 20, pericardial effusion in 6 of 20 and endomysial oedema in 16 of 20; in 5 dogs, leukostasis and oedema.
  - [CB182](articles/CB182_2019_histopathologial-alterations-babesia-gibsoni-infectiona-case.md) (B. gibsoni, n=1): congestion, oedema and tubular necrosis.
  - [CB189](articles/CB189_2020_encephalic-babesiosis-clinical-epidemiological-pathological-aspects.md) (n=7): cerebral endothelial hypertrophy, leukostasis, thrombosis and vasculitis.
  - [CB279](articles/CB279_2025_molecular-diagnosis-histopathological-analysis-cerebral-babesiosis.md) (B. gibsoni, CH16): vessel occlusion with piroplasms in 3 necropsies.
  - CH26: splenic endothelial shedding and microvessel wall defects with diapedesis.
- **Skin vasculitis:** [CB454](articles/CB454_2013_generalized-alopecia-vasculitis-like-changes-babesiosis.md) (B. gibsoni) showed vasculitis-like or ischaemic skin lesions that improved with antiprotozoal treatment and relapsed off it, after prior prednisolone. [CB283](articles/CB283_2025_vasculitis-associated-ehrlichia-canis-babesia-canis.md) describes generalised vasculitis with E. canis co-infection.
- **Sequelae:** four infertile male dogs after B. canis ([CB066](articles/CB066_2020_babesiosis-disease-rarely-considered-context-male.md)). [CB265](articles/CB265_2025_male-fertility-impairment-associated-babesia-gibsoni.md) found Babesia DNA in semen of 6 B. gibsoni dogs and lower sperm motility than in 8 controls, six months after treatment.

*Proposed mechanisms (largely untested).*
- Sequestration: [CB041](articles/CB041_2019_mechanisms-involved-persistence-babesia-canis-infection.md) (review) proposes endothelial attachment with coagulation-mediated trapping. [CB335](articles/CB335_2007_babesia-gibsoni-detection-during-experimental-infections.md) detected B. gibsoni in tissue in chronic infection, which the authors say may indicate sequestration. [CB397](articles/CB397_2010_babesia-gibsoni-detection-blood-smears-formalin.md) labelled infected red cells in splenic and renal vessels but tested no adhesion. [CB010](articles/CB010_2017_genome-wide-analysis-gene-expression-protein.md) predicts 103 candidate variant surface antigen models in B. canis (CH21).
- Hypoxic injury: [CB341](articles/CB341_2007_histological-ultrastructural-studies-renal-lesions-babesia.md) attributes tubular necrosis to probable hypoxia; [CB522](articles/CB522_2016_septic-shock-similarities-differences-postmortem-findings.md) attributes lesions to septic shock, hypoxic necrosis and DIC.
- Neurohumoral and cytokine drivers: serum TNF-alpha correlated inversely with arterial pressures in 11 dogs ([CB473](articles/CB473_2014_increased-concentration-serum-tnf-alpha-correlations.md), CH37). Aldosterone correlated negatively with pressures in [CB485](articles/CB485_2014_hyperaldosteronism-association-hypotension-azotaemia-babesiosis.md), though only 4 of 14 dogs were above the reference interval.
- Testicular microcirculatory disturbance ([CB066](articles/CB066_2020_babesiosis-disease-rarely-considered-context-male.md)), not tested. [CB265](articles/CB265_2025_male-fertility-impairment-associated-babesia-gibsoni.md) suggests parasite DNA in semen as an alternative.

*Severity and outcome markers (associations only).*
- Pressure: lower pressures went with complicated disease ([CB487](articles/CB487_2014_arterial-blood-pressure-values-naturally-infected.md), no outcome data in the abstract) and with higher creatinine and urea ([CB463](articles/CB463_2014_association-between-decreased-blood-pressure-azotaemia.md)). MAP correlated only modestly with tonicity ([CB044](articles/CB044_2019_hyponatraemia-syndrome-inappropriate-antidiuretic-hormone-secretion.md)).
- Lactate: in [CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md) (B. canis, 7 survivors, 8 non-survivors), non-survivors had higher admission lactate and lower haematocrit. Lactate and leukocyte count separated the groups with 100% sensitivity and specificity in this small cohort (full text).
- Vesicle proteins: [CB122](articles/CB122_2024_characterization-lc-ms-ms-based-proteomic.md) found 73 of 147 vesicle-associated proteins only in infected dogs, enriched for immune, integrin and VEGF annotations. Vesicle size and number did not differ.

**Convergence and conflict**

Independent sources agree on tissue congestion, oedema, effusion and haemorrhage in fatal cases: B. canis ([CB341](articles/CB341_2007_histological-ultrastructural-studies-renal-lesions-babesia.md), CH38), B. gibsoni ([CB182](articles/CB182_2019_histopathologial-alterations-babesia-gibsoni-infectiona-case.md), [CB188](articles/CB188_2020_pathomorphological-parasitological-molecular-diagnosis-babesia-gibsoni.md)), and unspecified species (CH26, [CB189](articles/CB189_2020_encephalic-babesiosis-clinical-epidemiological-pathological-aspects.md)). These lesions are not specific. [CB522](articles/CB522_2016_septic-shock-similarities-differences-postmortem-findings.md) found most organ lesions in 5 babesiosis dogs resembled those in 5 leptospirosis dogs.

Hypotension in severe B. canis disease is reported by several sources: [CB372](articles/CB372_2009_septic-shock-babesiosis.md), [CB487](articles/CB487_2014_arterial-blood-pressure-values-naturally-infected.md), [CB463](articles/CB463_2014_association-between-decreased-blood-pressure-azotaemia.md), [CB485](articles/CB485_2014_hyperaldosteronism-association-hypotension-azotaemia-babesiosis.md), [CB473](articles/CB473_2014_increased-concentration-serum-tnf-alpha-correlations.md). Overlap among [CB463](articles/CB463_2014_association-between-decreased-blood-pressure-azotaemia.md), [CB485](articles/CB485_2014_hyperaldosteronism-association-hypotension-azotaemia-babesiosis.md) and [CB473](articles/CB473_2014_increased-concentration-serum-tnf-alpha-correlations.md) is not assessed (only [CB473](articles/CB473_2014_increased-concentration-serum-tnf-alpha-correlations.md) has a cohort). Most are small and not outcome-linked. Hypotension is not universal. Uncomplicated cases in [CB487](articles/CB487_2014_arterial-blood-pressure-values-naturally-infected.md) had higher pressure than healthy dogs, [CB463](articles/CB463_2014_association-between-decreased-blood-pressure-azotaemia.md) found MAP below 80 in only 7 of 48, and [CB200](articles/CB200_2020_plasma-replacement-solutions-intensive-care-unit.md) describes hypotension in moderate shock.

Mortality in [CB372](articles/CB372_2009_septic-shock-babesiosis.md) follows partly from its definition, which includes treatment-resistant hypotension. Coagulation claims in [CB208](articles/CB208_2021_role-significance-morphological-changes-intraorgan-vessels.md) (DIC, thrombosis) rest on histology without coagulation tests.

What the 2006–2016 evidence adds:
- Shock evidence is not limited to one cohort. Several teams report B. canis hypotension and septic shock ([CB372](articles/CB372_2009_septic-shock-babesiosis.md), [CB487](articles/CB487_2014_arterial-blood-pressure-values-naturally-infected.md), [CB463](articles/CB463_2014_association-between-decreased-blood-pressure-azotaemia.md), [CB485](articles/CB485_2014_hyperaldosteronism-association-hypotension-azotaemia-babesiosis.md), [CB473](articles/CB473_2014_increased-concentration-serum-tnf-alpha-correlations.md)), though none measured perfusion directly.
- The TNF-alpha, hypotension and renal ischaemia proposal now has a primary association ([CB473](articles/CB473_2014_increased-concentration-serum-tnf-alpha-correlations.md), 11 dogs), not only a review.
- Lactate has been evaluated: [CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md), in *B. canis*. Central venous PO2 appears in [CB368](articles/CB368_2009_prospective-randomized-comparison-oxyglobin-hb-200.md) (B. rossi). Both are systemic markers, not tissue measures.
- *B. rossi* tissue and coagulation findings (for example [CB080](articles/CB080_2021_disorders-blood-circulation-probable-cause-death.md), [CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md), [CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md), [CB147](articles/CB147_2026_liver-histopathology-naturally-acquired-babesia-rossi.md)) sit in other categories under the automatic assignment; see INF, COA and PUL.
- B. vogeli still has no data on this axis, except as a co-infection in [CB140](articles/CB140_2025_fatal-coinfection-hepatozoon-canis-babesia-vogeli.md).

**Unresolved questions**

- Does admission MAP below 80 mmHg (the threshold in [CB463](articles/CB463_2014_association-between-decreased-blood-pressure-azotaemia.md)) predict death in B. canis, in a cohort with recorded outcome? [CB487](articles/CB487_2014_arterial-blood-pressure-values-naturally-infected.md) reports none in its abstract.
- Does the [CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md) lactate cut-off hold in a new B. canis cohort, and in B. rossi, after adjustment for haematocrit?
- Is the hypotension hypovolaemic, distributive or cardiac? Measure blood volume by indicator dilution with echocardiography. The [CB200](articles/CB200_2020_plasma-replacement-solutions-intensive-care-unit.md) and [CB220](articles/CB220_2022_features-hepatopathy-hematological-complications-acute-spontaneous.md) volume-deficit methods are not stated in the abstracts, and [CB497](articles/CB497_2015_cardiac-lesions-babesiosis.md) found myocardial lesions in all 20 dogs.
- Do TNF-alpha and aldosterone predict pressure independently of azotaemia, given that [CB463](articles/CB463_2014_association-between-decreased-blood-pressure-azotaemia.md) links pressure to creatinine and urea?
- Is sequestration demonstrable? Compare tissue parasite load (qPCR or in situ hybridisation with proper controls) with peripheral parasitaemia in matched organs.
- Are the endothelial lesions of [CB087](articles/CB087_2021_morphofunctional-changes-spleen-components-pathogenetic-substantiation.md), [CB208](articles/CB208_2021_role-significance-morphological-changes-intraorgan-vessels.md) and [CB341](articles/CB341_2007_histological-ultrastructural-studies-renal-lesions-babesia.md) specific to Babesia? Use blinded semi-quantitative scoring in dogs only, with non-babesial controls.
- Is the vasculitis in [CB454](articles/CB454_2013_generalized-alopecia-vasculitis-like-changes-babesiosis.md) and [CB283](articles/CB283_2025_vasculitis-associated-ehrlichia-canis-babesia-canis.md) due to Babesia, co-infection or immune-mediated disease? Take skin biopsies at first presentation with PCR for co-infections.

**Data available**

- [DS08](datasets.md#ds08) (status 1, partial download; [CB122](articles/CB122_2024_characterization-lc-ms-ms-based-proteomic.md)): PRIDE PXD047647, processed serum extracellular-vesicle proteomics, healthy versus B. canis dogs, per-sample protein tables. Sample counts differ between the abstract and the repository record.
- [S-CB516](datasets.md#s-cb516) (status 1; [CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md)): individual descriptions and clinical signs of 15 natural B. canis cases, plus a differential white-cell figure.
- [P-PXD005117](datasets.md#p-pxd005117) (status 2, files unverified; [CB010](articles/CB010_2017_genome-wide-analysis-gene-expression-protein.md), CH21): B. canis secreted proteome.
- [P-PRJEB15861](datasets.md#p-prjeb15861) (status 2, files unverified; [CB010](articles/CB010_2017_genome-wide-analysis-gene-expression-protein.md)): PacBio B. canis genome.
- [S-CB013](datasets.md#s-cb013) (status 1; [CB013](articles/CB013_2017_vector-borne-zoonotic-diseases-north-west.md)): per-dog qPCR, PCV and platelets for 96 Australian dogs.
- [S-CB038](datasets.md#s-cb038) (status 1; [CB038](articles/CB038_2019_babesiosis-among-working-organised-kennels-india.md)): summary statistics only, in a supplement document.

None is described as holding arterial pressure, lactate or microcirculatory data.

### COA — Coagulation, platelets, immunothrombosis and DIC

**Question this direction asks**

Is haemostatic change in canine babesiosis a bystander (thrombocytopenia without bleeding), an adaptive acute-phase response, or a driver of organ failure through immunothrombosis, microthrombosis and DIC? Does the answer differ by *Babesia* species?

**Evidence base**

Forty primary articles from 2006 to 2026: 13 from round 4 (2006-2016) and 27 from 2017-2026. The category assignment is automatic (NLP), with no human review. See the [search log](search-log.md) for methods.

- **Design:** 15 associational studies (case-control, biomarker or cohort comparisons); 1 mixed descriptive/associational study ([CB199](articles/CB199_2020_disseminated-intravascular-coagulation-syndrome-complication-acute.md)); 1 prognostic cohort ([CB438](articles/CB438_2012_mortality-virulent-babesiosis-associated-consumptive-coagulopathy.md)); 1 experimental infection ([CB419](articles/CB419_2011_thrombocytopenia-platelet-activity-experimentally-infected-rangelia.md), *Rangelia vitalii*, 7 infected and 5 control dogs); 14 descriptive series or surveys; 7 case reports or very small series; 1 narrative review ([CB104](articles/CB104_2022_babesia-north-america-update.md)).
