- **Species:** *B. rossi* 6 (five name South Africa); *B. canis* 7; *B. gibsoni* 7; *B. vogeli* 2; three named species together 3; *B. vulpes* 1; *R. vitalii* 1; large or unspecified *Babesia* 12; one multi-species review.
- **Text read:** 29 of 40 are abstract-only (11 of the 13 round 4 papers); 11 were read in full.
- **Independent sources:** 36. Cohort CH03 (Onderstepoort *B. rossi*, Oct 2011-Apr 2013) holds [CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md), [CB504](articles/CB504_2015_platelet-activation-platelet-leukocyte-interaction-naturally.md) and [CB513](articles/CB513_2015_platelet-indices-babesia-rossi-infection.md). CH13 (Zhytomyr) holds [CB199](articles/CB199_2020_disseminated-intravascular-coagulation-syndrome-complication-acute.md) and [CB217](articles/CB217_2022_shock-status-anti-shock-infusion-therapy.md). CH24 (Mannuthy) holds [CB049](articles/CB049_2020_clinico-haematological-evaluation-molecular-identification-babesia.md) and [CB183](articles/CB183_2019_haemato-biochemical-changes-babesia-gibsoni-infection.md). [CB011](articles/CB011_2017_alteration-haemostatic-parameters-uncomplicated-babesiosis.md) (CH22), [CB439](articles/CB439_2012_hemostatic-abnormalities-uncomplicated-babesiosis-babesia-rossi.md) (CH36) and [CB148](articles/CB148_2026_clinicopathological-abnormalities-outcome-acute-babesia-canis.md) (CH11) share cohorts only with articles outside this category. [CB212](articles/CB212_2021_splenomegaly-development-disseminated-intravascular-coagulation-syndrome.md) and [CB213](articles/CB213_2021_pathogenetic-therapy-vascular-platelet-hemostasis-disorders.md) look like the same Ukrainian programme ([CB213](articles/CB213_2021_pathogenetic-therapy-vascular-platelet-hemostasis-disorders.md) notes a possible overlap with [CB212](articles/CB212_2021_splenomegaly-development-disseminated-intravascular-coagulation-syndrome.md)) but have no cohort ID. If they belong to CH13, the count is 34. The notes do not say whether [CB438](articles/CB438_2012_mortality-virulent-babesiosis-associated-consumptive-coagulopathy.md) or [CB034](articles/CB034_2019_thromboelastographic-platelet-mapping-complicated-babesia-rossi.md) share dogs with CH03.
- **Off-topic for COA** (not reclassified): [CB030](articles/CB030_2019_analysis-amino-acid-sequences-18s-rna.md) (18S genotypes, serum proteome), [CB168](articles/CB168_2017_management-dermatological-lesions-associated-babesia-gibsoni.md) (skin lesions), [CB104](articles/CB104_2022_babesia-north-america-update.md) (taxonomy review), [CB138](articles/CB138_2025_comprehensive-study-ticks-tick-borne-diseases.md) (Nepal prevalence survey), [CB049](articles/CB049_2020_clinico-haematological-evaluation-molecular-identification-babesia.md) (microscopy versus PCR) and [CB268](articles/CB268_2025_babesiosis-among-indian-native-breeds-molecular.md) (kennel outbreak). In [CB539](articles/CB539_2016_perfil-hematol-gico-de-c-es.md), [CB183](articles/CB183_2019_haemato-biochemical-changes-babesia-gibsoni-infection.md), [CB269](articles/CB269_2025_babesiosis-insights-into-haematological-disruptions.md), [CB270](articles/CB270_2025_haematological-alterations-naturally-occurring-babesia-vogeli.md) and [CB184](articles/CB184_2020_change-blood-hematologi-cal-parameters-acute.md), platelets are one finding in a general haematology profile.

**What has been studied**

*Syndromes.*

- **Thrombocytopenia** is near-universal: 99.5% of 248 dogs with large *Babesia* ([CB326](articles/CB326_2006_hematological-changes-during-course-babesiosis-caused.md), CH30), 100% of 34 ([CB100](articles/CB100_2022_clinical-hematologic-findings-babesia-canis-infection.md)), all 49 ([CB106](articles/CB106_2023_autochthonous-babesia-canis-infections-49-germany.md)), all 23 in [CB148](articles/CB148_2026_clinicopathological-abnormalities-outcome-acute-babesia-canis.md), and 84.2% of 101 in [CB539](articles/CB539_2016_perfil-hematol-gico-de-c-es.md). In [CB326](articles/CB326_2006_hematological-changes-during-course-babesiosis-caused.md) and [CB539](articles/CB539_2016_perfil-hematol-gico-de-c-es.md) it was more frequent than anaemia (low red cell count 26.2%; anaemia 50.5%). Depth varies: day 0 mean 29 versus 259 ×10⁹/L in [CB374](articles/CB374_2009_alterations-some-blood-coagulation-parameters-naturally.md); 178 versus 288 in [CB199](articles/CB199_2020_disseminated-intravascular-coagulation-syndrome-complication-acute.md); 26.67 in acute versus 116.8 in chronic cases in [CB184](articles/CB184_2020_change-blood-hematologi-cal-parameters-acute.md) (n not reported).
- **Bleeding is not the rule.** Low platelets without bleeding signs are described in [CB439](articles/CB439_2012_hemostatic-abnormalities-uncomplicated-babesiosis-babesia-rossi.md) and [CB513](articles/CB513_2015_platelet-indices-babesia-rossi-infection.md). [CB345](articles/CB345_2008_multisystemic-effects-babesiosis-management-critical-cases.md) lists haemorrhagic signs among those of 102 *B. gibsoni* dogs (frequency not given). In [CB254](articles/CB254_2024_incidence-various-types-bleeding-disorders-associated.md), a series selected for bleeding signs, 667 of 1,593 dogs with vector-borne disease had babesiosis; 129 of 541 *B. gibsoni* cases (23.8%) were grouped as pancytopenia with DIC (criteria not visible). Haemorrhage is reported mainly in cases ([CB252](articles/CB252_2024_evan-s-syndrome-hemolytic-anemia-thrombocytopenia.md), [CB272](articles/CB272_2025_urogenital-haemorrhage-secondary-babesia-gibsoni-induced.md)) and at necropsy ([CB497](articles/CB497_2015_cardiac-lesions-babesiosis.md): cardiac haemorrhage in 12 of 20 fatal *B. canis* dogs).
- **Consumptive coagulopathy and DIC.**
  - [CB374](articles/CB374_2009_alterations-some-blood-coagulation-parameters-naturally.md) (30 infected, 29 controls): thrombin-antithrombin complexes (TAT) 49.7 and 87.7 µg/L on days 0 and 2 versus 7.2; antithrombin III (AT III) lower at every time; the authors conclude DIC occurs, with no DIC score.
  - [CB438](articles/CB438_2012_mortality-virulent-babesiosis-associated-consumptive-coagulopathy.md) (72 *B. rossi* dogs, 13 died): non-survivors had lower activity of all factors and of protein C, longer PT and aPTT and higher D-dimer. Antithrombin did not differ.
  - Ukrainian programme ([CB199](articles/CB199_2020_disseminated-intravascular-coagulation-syndrome-complication-acute.md), [CB217](articles/CB217_2022_shock-status-anti-shock-infusion-therapy.md), [CB212](articles/CB212_2021_splenomegaly-development-disseminated-intravascular-coagulation-syndrome.md)): higher soluble fibrin monomer complexes and fibrin degradation products, low fibrinogen, inconsistent PT/aPTT, read as consumption-stage DIC with grade II shock. No antithrombin or formal score in [CB199](articles/CB199_2020_disseminated-intravascular-coagulation-syndrome-complication-acute.md).
  - Cases: overt DIC and organ infarcts at necropsy ([CB364](articles/CB364_2009_complicated-form-babesiosis-case-report.md)); DIC with pulmonary oedema ([CB079](articles/CB079_2021_multiple-complications-infected-babesia-gibsoni.md)).
- **Uncomplicated disease.** [CB011](articles/CB011_2017_alteration-haemostatic-parameters-uncomplicated-babesiosis.md) (25 dogs): raised TAT, low AT III. [CB439](articles/CB439_2012_hemostatic-abnormalities-uncomplicated-babesiosis-babesia-rossi.md) (19 *B. rossi*, 10 controls): most thromboelastography variables unchanged (only clotting time K longer), antithrombin lower, fibrinogen higher, read by the authors as a normocoagulable state.
- **Organ microthrombosis** (descriptive): fibrin microthrombi in several organs of [CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md) non-survivors; clot-plugged splenic sinuses in [CB212](articles/CB212_2021_splenomegaly-development-disseminated-intravascular-coagulation-syndrome.md); lung fibrin in *B. rossi* ([CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md), CH04); cerebral thrombosis in 7 fatal cases ([CB189](articles/CB189_2020_encephalic-babesiosis-clinical-epidemiological-pathological-aspects.md)); fibrin deposition with vasculitis-like change in the skin of one *B. gibsoni* dog ([CB454](articles/CB454_2013_generalized-alopecia-vasculitis-like-changes-babesiosis.md)).

*Proposed mechanisms (largely untested).*

- **Thrombin generation and anticoagulant consumption:** TAT up and AT III down in [CB374](articles/CB374_2009_alterations-some-blood-coagulation-parameters-naturally.md) and [CB011](articles/CB011_2017_alteration-haemostatic-parameters-uncomplicated-babesiosis.md). Protein C was lower in [CB438](articles/CB438_2012_mortality-virulent-babesiosis-associated-consumptive-coagulopathy.md) non-survivors and in [CB333](articles/CB333_2007_protein-c-activity-babesiosis.md) (n not reported), but in [CB011](articles/CB011_2017_alteration-haemostatic-parameters-uncomplicated-babesiosis.md) only activated protein C differed. Suggested causes (cytokines, hepatic synthesis, elastase) were not tested.
- **Endothelial activation and hypofibrinolysis:** in [CB446](articles/CB446_2013_markers-coagulation-activation-endothelial-stimulation-inflammation.md) (12 dogs), CRP, soluble ICAM-1 and fibrinogen were raised and von Willebrand factor activity lowered. In [CB004](articles/CB004_2017_blood-markers-fibrinolysis-endothelial-activation-babesiosis.md) (30 dogs), thrombomodulin, HMGB-1, VCAM-1 and suPAR exceeded controls, and plasminogen and PAI-1 were lower at admission than on day 6. [CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md) found minimal fibrinolysis with raised α2-antiplasmin.
- **Fibrinogen compensation:** fibrin-only clot strength tracked fibrinogen (r = 0.735, [CB034](articles/CB034_2019_thromboelastographic-platelet-mapping-complicated-babesia-rossi.md), 13 versus 5 dogs); agonist-induced platelet responses did not differ.
- **Platelet change:** raised mean platelet volume (MPV) with lower plateletcrit is read as activation ([CB393](articles/CB393_2010_erythrocyte-platelet-indices-babesiosis.md)). In [CB419](articles/CB419_2011_thrombocytopenia-platelet-activity-experimentally-infected-rangelia.md), platelet aggregation was lower and megakaryocytes higher, and the authors propose splenic pooling or immune-mediated loss. Immune-mediated thrombocytopenia is inferred in single cases ([CB047](articles/CB047_2019_molecular-diagnosis-babesia-canis-infection-concurrent.md), [CB252](articles/CB252_2024_evan-s-syndrome-hemolytic-anemia-thrombocytopenia.md), [CB272](articles/CB272_2025_urogenital-haemorrhage-secondary-babesia-gibsoni-induced.md)) without platelet antibody tests.
- **Sequestration consolidated by coagulation** ([CB041](articles/CB041_2019_mechanisms-involved-persistence-babesia-canis-infection.md), review) and **hyperaggregation with hypovolaemic shock** ([CB213](articles/CB213_2021_pathogenetic-therapy-vascular-platelet-hemostasis-disorders.md), [CB217](articles/CB217_2022_shock-status-anti-shock-infusion-therapy.md)). The dipyridamole-plus-dextran infusion reports give no allocation or group sizes, so they do not show that aggregation drives disease.

*Severity and outcome markers (associations only).*

- *B. rossi:* factor activities, PT, aPTT and D-dimer separated non-survivors ([CB438](articles/CB438_2012_mortality-virulent-babesiosis-associated-consumptive-coagulopathy.md), [CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md)); CRP and serum amyloid A were raised in infected dogs. No platelet index differed by outcome in [CB513](articles/CB513_2015_platelet-indices-babesia-rossi-infection.md) (12 of 96 died). In [CB504](articles/CB504_2015_platelet-activation-platelet-leukocyte-interaction-naturally.md), platelet-monocyte aggregates were above controls in survivors (58.9%) but not in the 7 non-survivors (39.2%), with no survivor-versus-non-survivor difference.
- *B. canis:* non-survivors had lower thrombocyte counts at admission ([CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md); 8 versus 7 dogs), but lactate and leukocyte count separated the groups better. Complicated and uncomplicated dogs were largely alike at admission in [CB004](articles/CB004_2017_blood-markers-fibrinolysis-endothelial-activation-babesiosis.md).
- *B. gibsoni and others:* raised MPV in [CB304](articles/CB304_2026_studies-correlation-platelet-indices-molecular-diagnosis.md) and [CB166](articles/CB166_2017_are-platelet-indices-useful-clinico-pathological.md) is diagnostic. [CB270](articles/CB270_2025_haematological-alterations-naturally-occurring-babesia-vogeli.md) calls platelet variables prognostic but reports no outcome data.

**Convergence and conflict**

- **Agreement:** thrombocytopenia (84-100%) across species and sources, with raised MPV or macroplatelets ([CB393](articles/CB393_2010_erythrocyte-platelet-indices-babesiosis.md), [CB513](articles/CB513_2015_platelet-indices-babesia-rossi-infection.md), [CB304](articles/CB304_2026_studies-correlation-platelet-indices-molecular-diagnosis.md), [CB166](articles/CB166_2017_are-platelet-indices-useful-clinico-pathological.md), [CB419](articles/CB419_2011_thrombocytopenia-platelet-activity-experimentally-infected-rangelia.md)).
- **Thrombin generation** appears in two studies ([CB374](articles/CB374_2009_alterations-some-blood-coagulation-parameters-naturally.md), [CB011](articles/CB011_2017_alteration-haemostatic-parameters-uncomplicated-babesiosis.md)). Their authors differ: [CB374](articles/CB374_2009_alterations-some-blood-coagulation-parameters-naturally.md) concludes DIC; [CB011](articles/CB011_2017_alteration-haemostatic-parameters-uncomplicated-babesiosis.md) describes a procoagulant state short of DIC. Neither used a DIC score. The notes do not say whether they share dogs.
- **Platelet "activation" is not settled.** Indices point one way. [CB504](articles/CB504_2015_platelet-activation-platelet-leukocyte-interaction-naturally.md) found platelet P-selectin no different from controls, [CB034](articles/CB034_2019_thromboelastographic-platelet-mapping-complicated-babesia-rossi.md) found agonist-induced responses unchanged, and [CB419](articles/CB419_2011_thrombocytopenia-platelet-activity-experimentally-infected-rangelia.md) found lower aggregation. [CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md) reports platelet activation without the note naming an assay. [CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md) and [CB504](articles/CB504_2015_platelet-activation-platelet-leukocyte-interaction-naturally.md) are the same cohort (CH03), so this is an inconsistency within one source.
- **Fibrinogen conflicts.** High in *B. rossi* ([CB034](articles/CB034_2019_thromboelastographic-platelet-mapping-complicated-babesia-rossi.md), [CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md), [CB439](articles/CB439_2012_hemostatic-abnormalities-uncomplicated-babesiosis-babesia-rossi.md)), in experimental *B. canis* ([CB370](articles/CB370_2009_systemic-inflammatory-responses-experimentally-infected-babesia.md), n not reported) and in [CB446](articles/CB446_2013_markers-coagulation-activation-endothelial-stimulation-inflammation.md). Low in the Ukrainian programme, which uses non-standard assays on untyped *Babesia*. No study compares these directly.
- **Clotting times differ.** APTT was shorter than in controls on days 2-3 in [CB374](articles/CB374_2009_alterations-some-blood-coagulation-parameters-naturally.md), prolonged in *B. rossi* non-survivors ([CB438](articles/CB438_2012_mortality-virulent-babesiosis-associated-consumptive-coagulopathy.md), [CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md)) and in *B. gibsoni* ([CB134](articles/CB134_2025_clinical-clinicopathological-features-108-infected-babesia.md)), and unchanged in [CB199](articles/CB199_2020_disseminated-intravascular-coagulation-syndrome-complication-acute.md) and [CB419](articles/CB419_2011_thrombocytopenia-platelet-activity-experimentally-infected-rangelia.md). Species, severity and timing are all possible explanations.
- **Platelets and outcome:** lower in *B. canis* non-survivors ([CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md), small) but not in *B. rossi* ([CB513](articles/CB513_2015_platelet-indices-babesia-rossi-infection.md)).
- **Spleen:** splenectomised dogs are not protected from thrombocytopenia ([CB021](articles/CB021_2018_first-description-pathogenicity-babesia-vogeli-experimentally.md) *B. vogeli*, [CB382](articles/CB382_2010_babesiosis-caused-large-babesia-species-7.md) six splenectomised of seven dogs, [CB527](articles/CB527_2016_fatal-babesia-canis-canis-infection-splenectomized.md)), which argues against pooling as the main route. [CB120](articles/CB120_2023_case-report-fatal-babesia-vulpes-infection.md) had thrombocytosis, confounded by *Mycoplasma* and immunosuppression.
- **What the 2006-2016 papers change:**
  - They strengthen the procoagulant picture: TAT and AT III in *B. canis canis* come from 2009 ([CB374](articles/CB374_2009_alterations-some-blood-coagulation-parameters-naturally.md)), eight years before [CB011](articles/CB011_2017_alteration-haemostatic-parameters-uncomplicated-babesiosis.md).
  - They tie consumption to death: [CB438](articles/CB438_2012_mortality-virulent-babesiosis-associated-consumptive-coagulopathy.md) (2012) predates CH03 and shows the factor and protein C signature that [CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md) later repeats. The *B. rossi* work is still one Pretoria group, but at least four sources, not one.
  - They show that high fibrinogen and preserved clot strength ([CB439](articles/CB439_2012_hemostatic-abnormalities-uncomplicated-babesiosis-babesia-rossi.md), 2012) coexist with consumption in fatal cases.
  - They include the only experimental infections in this category ([CB419](articles/CB419_2011_thrombocytopenia-platelet-activity-experimentally-infected-rangelia.md), [CB370](articles/CB370_2009_systemic-inflammatory-responses-experimentally-infected-babesia.md)), though both are thin.
- **Species:** *B. rossi* has the deepest evidence; *B. gibsoni* data are descriptive and confounded by immune-mediated cytopenia; *B. vogeli* is sparse. Do not transfer the *B. rossi* phenotype to other species.

**Unresolved questions**

- Do PCR-typed *B. canis*, *B. gibsoni* and *B. rossi* cohorts, run on one panel (PT, aPTT, Clauss fibrinogen, TAT, AT III, D-dimer, thromboelastography), show the low-factor, long-PT/aPTT signature of [CB438](articles/CB438_2012_mortality-virulent-babesiosis-associated-consumptive-coagulopathy.md) and [CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md), or the short APTT of [CB374](articles/CB374_2009_alterations-some-blood-coagulation-parameters-naturally.md)?
- Is platelet activation real? Measure platelet P-selectin, agonist-induced aggregation, MPV and immature platelet fraction in the same dogs, since [CB513](articles/CB513_2015_platelet-indices-babesia-rossi-infection.md), [CB504](articles/CB504_2015_platelet-activation-platelet-leukocyte-interaction-naturally.md), [CB034](articles/CB034_2019_thromboelastographic-platelet-mapping-complicated-babesia-rossi.md) and [CB419](articles/CB419_2011_thrombocytopenia-platelet-activity-experimentally-infected-rangelia.md) disagree.
- Is thrombocytopenia consumptive, immune-mediated or sequestrational? Test platelet-bound antibody, thrombopoietin and platelet lifespan, and compare splenectomised with intact dogs.
- Does an adapted DIC score at admission predict death independently of lactate and leukocyte count ([CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md))? Use a [CB438](articles/CB438_2012_mortality-virulent-babesiosis-associated-consumptive-coagulopathy.md)-type design with more non-survivors.
- Is the low fibrinogen from the Ukrainian programme real? Repeat with Clauss fibrinogen and species typing.
- Is protein C low in uncomplicated disease ([CB333](articles/CB333_2007_protein-c-activity-babesiosis.md)) or only in fatal disease ([CB438](articles/CB438_2012_mortality-virulent-babesiosis-associated-consumptive-coagulopathy.md); [CB011](articles/CB011_2017_alteration-haemostatic-parameters-uncomplicated-babesiosis.md) found only activated protein C changed)? Measure protein C and activated protein C in the same dogs by severity.
- Does microthrombosis cause organ failure? Use serial coagulation tests and tissue fibrin staining in an experimental model before any anticoagulant or antiplatelet trial.

**Data available**

No record below has per-dog PT, aPTT, thromboelastography or D-dimer values.

- [S-CB138](datasets.md#s-cb138): public (status 1); Supplementary DOCX, summary statistics only ([CB138](articles/CB138_2025_comprehensive-study-ticks-tick-borne-diseases.md)).
- RQ-[CB145](articles/CB145_2026_thromboinflammation-virulent-babesiosis.md): request-only (status 3); individual dog/sample-level data, 97 *B. rossi* dogs; variables not described.
- [S-CB148](datasets.md#s-cb148): public (status 1); summary laboratory statistics at T0, T1 and T2 for 23 *B. canis* dogs (about 24-26 parameters, including thrombocytes, thrombin time and fibrinogen).
- [S-CB106](datasets.md#s-cb106): public (status 1); per-dog follow-up PCR after imidocarb in 9 dogs ([CB106](articles/CB106_2023_autochthonous-babesia-canis-infections-49-germany.md)).
- [S-CB516](datasets.md#s-cb516): public (status 1); 15 natural *B. canis* cases with signalment, travel history and clinical signs, and a differential white cell figure.
- [S-CB013](datasets.md#s-cb013): public (status 1); per-dog qPCR Ct, PCV and platelet count, 96 free-ranging dogs in Australia.
- [S-CB042](datasets.md#s-cb042): public (status 1); per-dog clinical data for 48 *B. vulpes*-positive dogs (43 columns).
- [DS05](datasets.md#ds05): public (status 1); 150 *B. rossi* dogs, 13-variable admission blood count, examination and complaint ([CB126](articles/CB126_2024_prevalence-co-infections-ehrlichia-spp-or.md)).
- [DS09](datasets.md#ds09): public (status 1); serum phosphoproteome, 10 *B. canis* and 10 control dogs, with per-dog clinical and phosphopeptide values ([CB019](articles/CB019_2018_lc-ms-ms-analysis-serum-phosphoproteome.md)).
- [S-CB005](datasets.md#s-cb005): public (status 1); per-wolf location, age, cause of death and 18S PCR result, 109 Croatian wolves; no coagulation data.
- [S-CB038](datasets.md#s-cb038): public (status 1); Supplement DOCX, summary statistics only ([CB038](articles/CB038_2019_babesiosis-among-working-organised-kennels-india.md)).
- [S-CB152](datasets.md#s-cb152): public (status 1); aggregate tables of sampling locations and case counts ([CB152](articles/CB152_2026_molecular-prevalence-clinical-profiles-babesiosis-northern.md)).
- RQ-[CB031](articles/CB031_2019_seroprevalence-ehrlichiosis-microscopic-screening-babesiosis-harare.md): request-only (status 3); individual-level data, 56 dogs ([CB031](articles/CB031_2019_seroprevalence-ehrlichiosis-microscopic-screening-babesiosis-harare.md)).
- RQ-[CB134](articles/CB134_2025_clinical-clinicopathological-features-108-infected-babesia.md): request-only (status 3); individual-level data, 108 *B. gibsoni* dogs ([CB134](articles/CB134_2025_clinical-clinicopathological-features-108-infected-babesia.md)).

### REN — Renal injury and renal function

**Question this direction asks**

How often, and where in the nephron, is the kidney injured in canine babesiosis? Is the raised urea so often seen a sign of renal injury? Can injury be detected before azotaemia, and does it change outcome? Do large and small *Babesia* species produce different renal phenotypes?

**Evidence base**

The category assignment is automatic (NLP) and has not been checked by a person; the search is described in the [search log](search-log.md). There are 40 primary articles: 15 from round 4 (2006–2016) and 25 from 2016–2026. Secondary-tagged articles (61) are used only where they add evidence.
- **Design or evidence type:** 18 associational (biomarker, omics, haemodynamic and urinary-index studies; one is a fox organ-weight survey), 1 prognostic, 12 descriptive series or surveys, 7 case reports, 2 narrative reviews. None is experimental or interventional.
- **Species:** *B. canis* 15, *B. rossi* 4, *B. gibsoni* 5 (four case reports and one five-dog series), *B. canis vogeli* 1, *B. canis* plus *B. gibsoni* (suspected) 1, *B. vulpes* plus *B. gibsoni* 1, a large unnamed *Babesia* 1, wild red foxes 1, unspecified 9, multi-species reviews 2.
- **Abstract-only:** 28 of 40, including 10 of the 15 round-4 papers. Twelve were appraised from full text.
- **Independent sources:** 37. Eleven articles share eight cohorts: CH01 ([CB001](articles/CB001_2017_utility-urinary-markers-assessment-renal-dysfunction.md), [CB046](articles/CB046_2019_urinary-proteome-kidney-injury-during-babesiosis.md)), CH06 ([CB071](articles/CB071_2021_combined-untargeted-targeted-metabolomics-approaches-reveal.md), [CB103](articles/CB103_2022_serum-urine-profiling-high-throughput-tmt.md), the same 22 dogs), CH10 ([CB124](articles/CB124_2024_diagnostic-efficacy-urinary-neutrophil-gelatinase-associated.md), [CB228](articles/CB228_2023_assessment-acute-kidney-injury-suffering-babesiosis.md)), and one primary article each in CH27, CH29, CH35, CH36 and CH37. The count includes the two reviews and the fox study.
- **One research tradition:** six Polish studies ([CB425](articles/CB425_2012_changes-susppup-ratio-fractional-excretion-strong.md), [CB456](articles/CB456_2013_urinary-creatinine-serum-creatinine-ratio-renal.md), [CB463](articles/CB463_2014_association-between-decreased-blood-pressure-azotaemia.md), [CB473](articles/CB473_2014_increased-concentration-serum-tnf-alpha-correlations.md), [CB485](articles/CB485_2014_hyperaldosteronism-association-hypotension-azotaemia-babesiosis.md), [CB093](articles/CB093_2022_association-between-increased-de-ritis-quotient.md)) probably come from one Warsaw group. Dog overlap is not stated, but they are not independent in method.
- **Looks off-topic for REN (not reclassified):** [CB392](articles/CB392_2010_babesiosis-northern-portugal-molecular-characterization-vector.md) (PCR epidemiology, no biochemistry), [CB382](articles/CB382_2010_babesiosis-caused-large-babesia-species-7.md), [CB506](articles/CB506_2015_tick-borne-diseases-borreliosis-anaplasmosis-babesiosis.md), [CB063](articles/CB063_2020_red-fox-vulpes-vulpes-possible-reservoir.md), [CB042](articles/CB042_2019_prevalence-babesia-spp-clinical-characteristics-babesia.md), [CB035](articles/CB035_2019_vitamin-d-status-babesiosis.md), [CB192](articles/CB192_2020_poikilocytosis-under-spontaneous-babesiosis.md), [CB293](articles/CB293_2026_clinicopathological-spectrum-tick-borne-viral-diseases.md) and [CB494](articles/CB494_2015_babesia-canis.md).

**What has been studied**

*Syndromes.*
- **Where the raised urea comes from:** [CB325](articles/CB325_2006_elevated-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-babesiosis.md) (2006, CH29; 25 severely anaemic dogs, 13 reference dogs) found higher urea and urea:creatinine ratio while creatinine and cystatin-C stayed in reference ranges. The authors judged the extra urea probably not renal. The source was not identified. Secondary-tag studies agree: [CB412](articles/CB412_2011_changes-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-babesiosis.md) (same cohort) found normal GFR in all groups. In [CB478](articles/CB478_2014_renal-function-integrity-babesiosis.md) (20 dogs, no controls) all had raised urea nitrogen, 12 (60%) had raised creatinine, and all had USG above 1.030. In [CB330](articles/CB330_2007_biochemical-abnormalities-observed-serum-infected-large.md) (202 sera) urea nitrogen was raised in 62.4%, creatinine in a smaller share (within 18.3–32.7%).
- **Renal azotaemia and AKI:** in *B. rossi* ([CB025](articles/CB025_2018_renal-azotemia-associated-clinical-laboratory-findings.md), 152 dogs) 17% were azotaemic and 14% had renal azotaemia. A USG cut-off below 1.030 would have called only 6 of 26 azotaemic dogs renal. Renal azotaemia went with older age, collapse, hypoglycaemia, hyperphosphataemia, cerebral babesiosis, ARDS and lower survival to discharge. In CH10, AKI occurred in 15% of babesiosis dogs and in all leptospirosis dogs. In Austria ([CB061](articles/CB061_2020_complications-risk-factors-regarding-outcomes-babesiosis.md), 240 dogs, abstract) creatinine above 5 mg/dL and phosphate above 3 mmol/L were associated with death; mortality was 33.9% with renal involvement.
- **Tubular necrosis, indirect:** [CB456](articles/CB456_2013_urinary-creatinine-serum-creatinine-ratio-renal.md) (abstract): lower urinary-to-serum creatinine ratio in infected dogs, and a higher renal failure index in azotaemic ones; the index correlated with delay to treatment (r = 0.89). [CB425](articles/CB425_2012_changes-susppup-ratio-fractional-excretion-strong.md): higher fractional excretion of monovalent electrolytes in azotaemic dogs.
- **Tubular necrosis, tissue:** [CB341](articles/CB341_2007_histological-ultrastructural-studies-renal-lesions-babesia.md) (2007, abstract; 8 fatal *B. canis* cases) found proximal tubular necrosis, no significant glomerular change on histology, and capillary endothelial damage on electron microscopy. Seven of the eight dogs had received imidocarb. Small necropsy series and reports agree on tubular lesions ([CB249](articles/CB249_2024_pathological-diagnosis-babesiosis-labrador-retriever.md), [CB255](articles/CB255_2024_clinico-pathological-evaluation-babesia-gibsoni-associatedrenal.md), [CB307](articles/CB307_2026_enzyme-morphological-criteria-diagnosing-stages-hepatorenal.md)).
- **Proteinuric phenotype, *B. gibsoni*:** [CB404](articles/CB404_2011_resolution-proteinuric-nephropathy-associated-babesia-gibsoni.md) (2011, one dog) showed membranoproliferative glomerulonephritis with immune-complex deposition; proteinuria and PCR cleared after treatment that included immunosuppression. [CB018](articles/CB018_2018_azotemia-proteinuria-infected-babesia-gibsoni.md): 5 pit bull-type dogs, all hypoalbuminaemic and proteinuric, 3 azotaemic. Glomerulonephritis also appears in single necropsies ([CB097](articles/CB097_2022_pathomorphological-changes-german-boxer-infected-babesia.md), [CB188](articles/CB188_2020_pathomorphological-parasitological-molecular-diagnosis-babesia-gibsoni.md)).
- **Single clinical cases, none establishing causation:** acute renal failure ([CB520](articles/CB520_2016_clinical-management-acute-renal-failure-due.md), [CB072](articles/CB072_2021_therapeutic-management-babesia-gibsoni-induced-acute.md), [CB253](articles/CB253_2024_clinical-management-acute-kidney-injury-babesiosis.md), [CB258](articles/CB258_2024_renal-azotemia-associated-haematobiochemical-findings-labrador.md)); chronic renal failure ([CB179](articles/CB179_2019_babesiosis-associated-chronic-renal-failure-comprehensive.md)); renal secondary hyperparathyroidism ([CB303](articles/CB303_2026_rubber-jaw-syndrome-juvenile-labrador-retriever.md)).
- **Species frame:** [CB095](articles/CB095_2022_role-systemic-infections-kidney-diseases.md) (abstract) contrasts acute kidney injury with tubulointerstitial nephritis in large-species disease and rare immune-complex glomerulonephritis in small-species disease.

*Proposed mechanisms (largely untested).*
- **Hypotension, TNF-α and renal ischaemia:** [CB463](articles/CB463_2014_association-between-decreased-blood-pressure-azotaemia.md) (48 infected, 33 controls) found a mean arterial pressure below 80 mmHg in 7 dogs (14.6%). Azotaemic dogs had lower pressures, and all pressures correlated inversely with creatinine and urea. [CB473](articles/CB473_2014_increased-concentration-serum-tnf-alpha-correlations.md) (CH37, 11 dogs) found TNF-α negatively correlated with systolic, diastolic and mean pressure (-0.7246, -0.6642, -0.7151); five dogs had undetectable TNF-α. [CB485](articles/CB485_2014_hyperaldosteronism-association-hypotension-azotaemia-babesiosis.md) (14 infected, 10 controls) found higher aldosterone, but only 4 dogs were above the reference interval. [CB425](articles/CB425_2012_changes-susppup-ratio-fractional-excretion-strong.md) inferred mineralocorticoid excess from urinary indices. All are single-time-point correlations.
- **Immune-complex glomerulopathy:** [CB404](articles/CB404_2011_resolution-proteinuric-nephropathy-associated-babesia-gibsoni.md), [CB018](articles/CB018_2018_azotemia-proteinuria-infected-babesia-gibsoni.md) (see above).
- **Epithelial-mesenchymal transition:** inferred from pathway enrichment in [CB046](articles/CB046_2019_urinary-proteome-kidney-injury-during-babesiosis.md).
- **Drug effect:** imidocarb confounds [CB341](articles/CB341_2007_histological-ultrastructural-studies-renal-lesions-babesia.md) and [CB258](articles/CB258_2024_renal-azotemia-associated-haematobiochemical-findings-labrador.md).

*Severity and outcome markers (associations only).*
- **Urinary markers precede serum change:** in uncomplicated *B. rossi* ([CB435](articles/CB435_2012_assessment-renal-dysfunction-using-urinary-markers.md), 18 vs 8 dogs, abstract) urinary IgG, CRP and retinol-binding protein were higher, but serum urea and creatinine were not. [CB053](articles/CB053_2020_evaluation-acute-kidney-injury-complicated-or.md) (35 vs 10 dogs, abstract) found IgG, CRP, RBP and NGAL above control values (P < 0.001) with creatinine and SDMA unchanged. Tubular markers and UPC fell within 24 h (n = 11); all normalised by one month (n = 9). Complicated cases had higher glomerular markers, UPC and SDMA.
- **B. canis:** [CB001](articles/CB001_2017_utility-urinary-markers-assessment-renal-dysfunction.md) (10 vs 10): IgG, RBP and Tamm-Horsfall protein were higher in every infected dog. [CB024](articles/CB024_2018_glomerular-tubular-kidney-damage-markers-babesiosis.md) (42 vs 14): all markers except uromodulin differed; groups were defined by UPC, so correlations with UPC are partly circular.
- **Omics and early-AKI claims:** [CB046](articles/CB046_2019_urinary-proteome-kidney-injury-during-babesiosis.md) (one pooled sample per group) found 403 vs 176 urinary proteins. [CB071](articles/CB071_2021_combined-untargeted-targeted-metabolomics-approaches-reveal.md) found 601 discriminating urinary features. [CB103](articles/CB103_2022_serum-urine-profiling-high-throughput-tmt.md) proposed uNGAL and L-FABP as pre-azotaemic markers. [CB124](articles/CB124_2024_diagnostic-efficacy-urinary-neutrophil-gelatinase-associated.md) found uNGAL and uKIM-1 raised in IRIS grade I AKI, with babesiosis pooled with leptospirosis.
- **AST/ALT:** [CB093](articles/CB093_2022_association-between-increased-de-ritis-quotient.md) (34 dogs; 9 renal, 13 pre-renal azotaemia) linked higher AST and AST/ALT to renal azotaemia. [CB424](articles/CB424_2012_increased-ast-alt-ratio-azotaemic-infected.md) (CH34, 182 dogs) found 2.68 in azotaemic vs 1.08 in non-azotaemic dogs.
- **Outcome:** [CB025](articles/CB025_2018_renal-azotemia-associated-clinical-laboratory-findings.md) and [CB061](articles/CB061_2020_complications-risk-factors-regarding-outcomes-babesiosis.md) (above). Phosphate was also higher in non-survivors in [CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md) (15 dogs).

**Convergence and conflict**

- **Urea is not a renal marker here:** [CB325](articles/CB325_2006_elevated-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-babesiosis.md), [CB412](articles/CB412_2011_changes-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-babesiosis.md), [CB478](articles/CB478_2014_renal-function-integrity-babesiosis.md) and [CB330](articles/CB330_2007_biochemical-abnormalities-observed-serum-infected-large.md) point the same way. Urea rises more often than creatinine, and GFR markers can stay normal. The 2006–2011 work strengthens the earlier caution that azotaemia counts based on urea overstate renal disease. One conflict remains: [CB465](articles/CB465_2014_importance-haematological-changes-diagnosing-babesiosis.md) (300 dogs, abstract) links azotaemia to renal failure caused by haemolysis, which a descriptive design cannot show.
- **Cystatin-C disagrees:** it stayed in range in [CB325](articles/CB325_2006_elevated-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-babesiosis.md) (a human assay was used), but [CB501](articles/CB501_2015_assessment-renal-function-using-cystatin-c.md) (secondary) found it raised in uncomplicated *B. c. vogeli* (5.28 vs 2.30 mg/L in controls) while creatinine did not differ. Species, assay and severity differ.
- **Early urinary change:** [CB435](articles/CB435_2012_assessment-renal-dysfunction-using-urinary-markers.md) (2012) and [CB053](articles/CB053_2020_evaluation-acute-kidney-injury-complicated-or.md) (2020) agree in *B. rossi*: urinary injury markers rise while serum creatinine does not, and the change reverses. They come from the same research tradition; dog overlap is not stated. CH01 and [CB024](articles/CB024_2018_glomerular-tubular-kidney-damage-markers-babesiosis.md) agree for IgG and RBP in *B. canis*, but disagree on uromodulin.
- **Glomerular or tubular:** biomarker studies report mixed glomerular and tubular injury. [CB341](articles/CB341_2007_histological-ultrastructural-studies-renal-lesions-babesia.md) and the necropsy series show mainly tubular lesions, and glomerulonephritis appears only in single necropsies and [CB404](articles/CB404_2011_resolution-proteinuric-nephropathy-associated-babesia-gibsoni.md). Urinary IgG and albumin could also reflect failed tubular reabsorption; this is untested.
- **Haemodynamic chain:** the 2012–2014 Polish papers turn the earlier TNF-α, hypotension and ischaemia idea (from the self-review [CB157](articles/CB157_2017_over-20-years-research-babesiosis-faculty.md)) into primary data. [CB341](articles/CB341_2007_histological-ultrastructural-studies-renal-lesions-babesia.md) fits it. The evidence is small, cross-sectional and from one group. Direction is unknown, and the effects rest on minorities of dogs (7 of 48 hypotensive; 4 of 14 with high aldosterone).
- **AST/ALT:** [CB093](articles/CB093_2022_association-between-increased-de-ritis-quotient.md) and [CB424](articles/CB424_2012_increased-ast-alt-ratio-azotaemic-infected.md) (different cohorts, probably one group) agree. In [CB424](articles/CB424_2012_increased-ast-alt-ratio-azotaemic-infected.md) the ratio did not differ between anaemic and non-anaemic dogs (1.52 vs 1.76), which argues against haemolysis as the source, but muscle and liver are not excluded.
- **Frequency:** AKI or renal azotaemia in 14–17% ([CB025](articles/CB025_2018_renal-azotemia-associated-clinical-laboratory-findings.md), [CB228](articles/CB228_2023_assessment-acute-kidney-injury-suffering-babesiosis.md)); azotaemia in 50.9% in [CB291](articles/CB291_2025_haematological-changes-treatment-babesiosis-using-imidocarb.md), definition not stated.
- **Species:** *B. gibsoni* data (mostly case reports) are proteinuric or chronic; *B. rossi* and *B. canis* data are acute and reversible. *B. vogeli* has no renal-focused primary study ([CB293](articles/CB293_2026_clinicopathological-spectrum-tick-borne-viral-diseases.md) and [CB392](articles/CB392_2010_babesiosis-northern-portugal-molecular-characterization-vector.md) give only routine biochemistry or none). Designs differ, so species cannot be ranked.

**Unresolved questions**

- What raises urea in anaemic dogs? Compare urea with measured GFR (clearance), gastrointestinal bleeding and protein catabolism in *B. canis* and *B. rossi*.
- Which cystatin-C assay and cut-off is valid in canine babesiosis, and does it resolve the [CB325](articles/CB325_2006_elevated-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-babesiosis.md)/CB501 conflict?
- Does falling blood pressure or rising TNF-α precede rising creatinine? Test with serial pressures in a prospective cohort larger than 50.
- Do urinary IgG and albumin reflect glomerular leak? Compare markers with necropsy or biopsy (immunofluorescence, electron microscopy) in non-survivors.
- Does *B. gibsoni* cause immune-complex glomerulopathy? Biopsy proteinuric dogs with co-infections excluded.
- Do survivors have residual CKD (raised SDMA or UPC) at 6–12 months?
- Does imidocarb add renal injury? Compare creatinine and urinary markers before and after imidocarb versus another drug.

**Data available**

- [S-CB071](datasets.md#s-cb071) (status 1): per-dog processed urinary metabolomics for 34 dogs ([CB071](articles/CB071_2021_combined-untargeted-targeted-metabolomics-approaches-reveal.md)); groups by creatinine and UPC; no raw spectra.
- [S-CB046](datasets.md#s-cb046) (status 1): urinary protein identification lists from the pooled samples; no per-dog values.
- [S-CB093](datasets.md#s-cb093) (status 1): correlations among renal indices (PDF); summary statistics only.
- [S-CB042](datasets.md#s-cb042) (status 1): 48 *B. vulpes* dogs, signalment, serology and PCR, co-infection and clinical data; clinicopathology for only 14 dogs.
- [S-CB516](datasets.md#s-cb516) (status 1, secondary): individual descriptions and clinical signs of 15 natural *B. canis* cases; renal content not described.
- [S-CB106](datasets.md#s-cb106) (status 1, secondary): per-dog PCR after imidocarb (9 dogs) and summary laboratory statistics (23 dogs); not renal-specific.
- RQ-[CB134](articles/CB134_2025_clinical-clinicopathological-features-108-infected-babesia.md) (status 3, secondary): 108 *B. gibsoni* dogs, on request; the article reports proteinuria.
- RQ-[CB536](articles/CB536_2016_prevalence-dirofilaria-repens-cats-healthy-concurrent.md) (status 3, secondary): 58 *B. canis* dogs, on request.

None of the listed datasets is described as holding per-dog SDMA, urinary injury-marker or histology data.

### PUL — Pulmonary injury and gas exchange

**Question this direction asks**

Which lung lesions and gas-exchange failures occur in canine babesiosis, how often, and in which species? Does the clinical label "Babesia-associated ARDS" match a recognisable tissue lesion? Is the injury caused by large-vessel obstruction, microvascular and inflammatory injury, the host response, or treatment such as transfusion and fluids?

**Evidence base**

Seven articles have PUL as their NLP primary category, published from 2008 to 2023. Five are round 4 (2006-2016): [CB356](articles/CB356_2008_scintigraphic-evaluation-pulmonary-perfusion-pattern-hospitalised.md), [CB365](articles/CB365_2009_influence-anaemia-azotaemia-infected-babesia-canis.md), [CB398](articles/CB398_2010_evolution-clinical-haematological-biochemical-findings-young.md), [CB447](articles/CB447_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-acute-respiratory-distress-syndrome.md) and [CB474](articles/CB474_2014_clinico-biochemical-responses-experimental-infection-babesia.md). Two are later: [CB102](articles/CB102_2022_successful-treatment-suspect-babesia-induced-ards.md) and [CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md). By design:

- 2 single-dog case reports ([CB447](articles/CB447_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-acute-respiratory-distress-syndrome.md), [CB102](articles/CB102_2022_successful-treatment-suspect-babesia-induced-ards.md))
- 1 necropsy series with immunohistochemistry ([CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md))
- 1 prospective case-control scintigraphy study, 14 infected and 9 healthy dogs ([CB356](articles/CB356_2008_scintigraphic-evaluation-pulmonary-perfusion-pattern-hospitalised.md))
- 1 experimental infection, 5 infected and 5 control dogs ([CB474](articles/CB474_2014_clinico-biochemical-responses-experimental-infection-babesia.md))
- 2 associational or descriptive clinical studies with no lung outcome ([CB365](articles/CB365_2009_influence-anaemia-azotaemia-infected-babesia-canis.md), 127 dogs; [CB398](articles/CB398_2010_evolution-clinical-haematological-biochemical-findings-young.md), two groups of 17 dogs)

By species: B. rossi 2 ([CB356](articles/CB356_2008_scintigraphic-evaluation-pulmonary-perfusion-pattern-hospitalised.md), [CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md); both suspected); B. canis or B. canis canis 3 ([CB365](articles/CB365_2009_influence-anaemia-azotaemia-infected-babesia-canis.md), [CB447](articles/CB447_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-acute-respiratory-distress-syndrome.md), [CB474](articles/CB474_2014_clinico-biochemical-responses-experimental-infection-babesia.md); none confirmed); B. vogeli 2 ([CB398](articles/CB398_2010_evolution-clinical-haematological-biochemical-findings-young.md), [CB102](articles/CB102_2022_successful-treatment-suspect-babesia-induced-ards.md); both confirmed). Four are abstract-only ([CB365](articles/CB365_2009_influence-anaemia-azotaemia-infected-babesia-canis.md), [CB398](articles/CB398_2010_evolution-clinical-haematological-biochemical-findings-young.md), [CB447](articles/CB447_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-acute-respiratory-distress-syndrome.md), [CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md)). The seven are seven independent sources. [CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md) belongs to cohort CH04 (Onderstepoort necropsy tissue bank), but the other CH04 members ([CB054](articles/CB054_2020_pathology-spleen-lethal-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), [CB129](articles/CB129_2024_cytological-histopathological-bone-marrow-findings-natural.md), [CB147](articles/CB147_2026_liver-histopathology-naturally-acquired-babesia-rossi.md)) are not PUL primaries.

The category assignment is automatic (NLP) and was not checked by a person. Three primaries look off-topic for PUL: [CB365](articles/CB365_2009_influence-anaemia-azotaemia-infected-babesia-canis.md) (anaemia and azotaemia), [CB398](articles/CB398_2010_evolution-clinical-haematological-biochemical-findings-young.md) (haematology in young dogs with vector-borne infections) and [CB474](articles/CB474_2014_clinico-biochemical-responses-experimental-infection-babesia.md) (general clinical biochemistry; respiratory distress is one sign among many). They are kept as assigned. Only four primaries ([CB356](articles/CB356_2008_scintigraphic-evaluation-pulmonary-perfusion-pattern-hospitalised.md), [CB447](articles/CB447_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-acute-respiratory-distress-syndrome.md), [CB102](articles/CB102_2022_successful-treatment-suspect-babesia-induced-ards.md), [CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md)) study lung disease directly.

Twenty-seven further articles carry PUL as a secondary tag. About eleven report a lung finding (for example [CB032](articles/CB032_2019_babesiosis-male-boerboel-hematobiochemical-anatomic-pathological.md), [CB188](articles/CB188_2020_pathomorphological-parasitological-molecular-diagnosis-babesia-gibsoni.md) and [CB249](articles/CB249_2024_pathological-diagnosis-babesiosis-labrador-retriever.md)), mostly single necropsy reports. The digest lines for the others (for example [CB093](articles/CB093_2022_association-between-increased-de-ritis-quotient.md), [CB246](articles/CB246_2024_clinical-haematological-biochemical-alterations-affected-babesiosis.md), [CB394](articles/CB394_2010_malondialdehyde-levels-serum-infected-babesia-canis.md), [CB424](articles/CB424_2012_increased-ast-alt-ratio-azotaemic-infected.md), [CB473](articles/CB473_2014_increased-concentration-serum-tnf-alpha-correlations.md)) report no lung result. Articles used in the earlier synthesis but outside the NLP PUL set ([CB312](articles/CB312_2026_unique-case-babesiosis-hemoptysis-clinical-management.md), [CB025](articles/CB025_2018_renal-azotemia-associated-clinical-laboratory-findings.md), [CB068](articles/CB068_2020_acid-base-status-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md)) are no longer covered. See the [search log](search-log.md) for how articles were found.

**What has been studied**

*Syndromes.*
- **ARDS, B. rossi (tissue).** [CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md) examined lungs from fatal natural infections. Alveolar oedema accompanied every death. Histology showed acute interstitial pneumonia with haemorrhage and mononuclear infiltrates, and fibrin in alveoli in slightly over half of cases. MAC387 and CD204 (monocyte-macrophage) and CD3 (T cell) labelling was higher than in controls. The authors say the pattern only partly matches the exudative phase of diffuse alveolar damage. Sample size is not in the abstract. Most deaths occurred within a day of presentation.
- **ARDS, B. canis (tissue).** [CB447](articles/CB447_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-acute-respiratory-distress-syndrome.md) (France, 2013) describes one Scottish Terrier with a diffuse interstitial and alveolar radiographic pattern that died six hours after presentation. Necropsy showed cerebral babesiosis and ARDS, with red cell clumps in lung, liver and brain. The authors call it the first histology-confirmed Babesia-associated ARDS in a dog. The lesion was therefore recorded in B. canis ten years before [CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md), but from one dog and an abstract.
- **ARDS, B. vogeli (clinical).** [CB102](articles/CB102_2022_successful-treatment-suspect-babesia-induced-ards.md) describes a puppy that became hypoxaemic about 14 hours after admission for haemolytic anaemia (P:F 140). It survived on lung-protective ventilation with neuromuscular blockade and was well at three months. Confounders: about 42 mL/kg red cell transfusion (TRALI not excluded), ehrlichiosis co-infection, and an effusion with bacteria on cytology.
- **Large-vessel embolism not found.** [CB356](articles/CB356_2008_scintigraphic-evaluation-pulmonary-perfusion-pattern-hospitalised.md) (Onderstepoort, 2008) looked for wedge-shaped perfusion defects in 14 hospitalised dogs. None had them, and the perfusion pattern did not differ from 9 healthy Beagles (P = 1.00). Oxygenation indices were similar in both groups (full text). Small or partly occluding emboli and microvascular disease were not excluded. Scans were read visually, the sample was small and the groups differed in breed.
- **Respiratory distress as a sign.** In the [CB474](articles/CB474_2014_clinico-biochemical-responses-experimental-infection-babesia.md) experimental infection, respiratory distress occurred in 20% of infected dogs (one of five). Lungs were not examined. [CB332](articles/CB332_2007_abdominal-compartment-syndrome-babesiosis.md) describes respiratory distress with abdominal compartment syndrome in one dog. [CB389](articles/CB389_2010_clinical-management-babesiosis.md) (review) lists ARDS among complications linked to a poorer outcome.
- **Pulmonary oedema elsewhere.** Single necropsies or small series report oedema, haemorrhage or interstitial pneumonia in B. gibsoni ([CB188](articles/CB188_2020_pathomorphological-parasitological-molecular-diagnosis-babesia-gibsoni.md), [CB249](articles/CB249_2024_pathological-diagnosis-babesiosis-labrador-retriever.md)), B. canis ([CB097](articles/CB097_2022_pathomorphological-changes-german-boxer-infected-babesia.md)), unspecified Babesia ([CB032](articles/CB032_2019_babesiosis-male-boerboel-hematobiochemical-anatomic-pathological.md)) and 3 of 77 puppy deaths ([CB195](articles/CB195_2020_role-babesiosis-pup-mortality.md)). [CB079](articles/CB079_2021_multiple-complications-infected-babesia-gibsoni.md) reports non-cardiogenic oedema with DIC in a B. gibsoni survivor. [CB260](articles/CB260_2024_studies-clinical-syndromes-linked-babesia-gibsoni.md) assigned 9 of 24 PCR-positive dogs to a syndrome, with ARDS among the four definitions (1 dog in the earlier extraction). [CB080](articles/CB080_2021_disorders-blood-circulation-probable-cause-death.md) (6 fatal B. canis dogs, cohort CH27) reports multi-organ congestion and oedema and proposes circulatory failure.

*Proposed mechanisms (largely untested).*
- Macrophage and T-cell inflammation with intra-alveolar fibrin, suggesting coagulation-linked injury ([CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md)).
- Microvascular red cell aggregation ([CB447](articles/CB447_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-acute-respiratory-distress-syndrome.md)). This fits the negative large-vessel scan in [CB356](articles/CB356_2008_scintigraphic-evaluation-pulmonary-perfusion-pattern-hospitalised.md), but neither study tested it.
- Circulatory failure and congestion ([CB080](articles/CB080_2021_disorders-blood-circulation-probable-cause-death.md)).
- Increased vascular permeability ([CB079](articles/CB079_2021_multiple-complications-infected-babesia-gibsoni.md)).
- Transfusion-related injury (raised as a confounder in [CB102](articles/CB102_2022_successful-treatment-suspect-babesia-induced-ards.md)).

[CB365](articles/CB365_2009_influence-anaemia-azotaemia-infected-babesia-canis.md) found no difference in urea or creatinine between anaemic and non-anaemic dogs (abstract only). It has no lung content.

*Severity and outcome markers (associations only).* No PUL primary tests a marker against pulmonary outcome. [CB398](articles/CB398_2010_evolution-clinical-haematological-biochemical-findings-young.md) reports thrombocytopenia and lymphocytosis in B. vogeli-infected young dogs, but co-infections prevent attribution to the species (abstract only). Pulmonary complications appear as outcome labels in reviews such as [CB389](articles/CB389_2010_clinical-management-babesiosis.md) and [CB278](articles/CB278_2025_review-prognostic-biomarkers-babesiosis.md).

**Convergence and conflict**

- **Lesion.** Alveolar oedema with interstitial inflammation recurs in B. rossi ([CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md), CH04), B. canis ([CB447](articles/CB447_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-acute-respiratory-distress-syndrome.md), [CB097](articles/CB097_2022_pathomorphological-changes-german-boxer-infected-babesia.md), [CB080](articles/CB080_2021_disorders-blood-circulation-probable-cause-death.md)), B. gibsoni ([CB188](articles/CB188_2020_pathomorphological-parasitological-molecular-diagnosis-babesia-gibsoni.md), [CB249](articles/CB249_2024_pathological-diagnosis-babesiosis-labrador-retriever.md)) and the Boerboel ([CB032](articles/CB032_2019_babesiosis-male-boerboel-hematobiochemical-anatomic-pathological.md)). Outside [CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md) these are single dogs or small series, mostly smear-identified.
- **Species.** ARDS or an ARDS-like lesion is now reported in B. rossi, B. canis and B. vogeli. Each comes from one study or one dog, so incidence and virulence cannot be compared. [CB119](articles/CB119_2023_diverse-pathogenicity-various-babesia-parasite-species.md) ranks B. vogeli as the least pathogenic of four main species, yet the clinical ARDS survivor ([CB102](articles/CB102_2022_successful-treatment-suspect-babesia-induced-ards.md)) had B. vogeli, with confounders.
- **Mechanism.** [CB356](articles/CB356_2008_scintigraphic-evaluation-pulmonary-perfusion-pattern-hospitalised.md) found no large-vessel perfusion defects, so macroscopic pulmonary thromboembolism is not supported as a common cause of breathlessness in hospitalised dogs. The fibrin and cell-aggregation findings ([CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md), [CB447](articles/CB447_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-acute-respiratory-distress-syndrome.md)) point to microvascular or inflammatory injury. [CB080](articles/CB080_2021_disorders-blood-circulation-probable-cause-death.md) favours circulatory failure, which fluid therapy could also produce. These readings are not reconciled.
- **Gas exchange.** [CB356](articles/CB356_2008_scintigraphic-evaluation-pulmonary-perfusion-pattern-hospitalised.md) found oxygenation indices similar to controls in hospitalised dogs, whereas [CB102](articles/CB102_2022_successful-treatment-suspect-babesia-induced-ards.md) recorded P:F 140. Hypoxaemia is therefore not general, and lung failure looks like a subset of complicated cases. The note does not say whether the [CB356](articles/CB356_2008_scintigraphic-evaluation-pulmonary-perfusion-pattern-hospitalised.md) dogs were dyspnoeic.

**Unresolved questions**

- What is the incidence of ARDS (P:F below 300 with bilateral infiltrates) in the first 48 hours in prospective B. rossi versus B. canis cohorts, and does it predict death independently of renal and cerebral complications?
- Do dyspnoeic dogs with babesiosis have microvascular obstruction that scintigraphy misses? CT or contrast imaging, D-dimer and platelet counts could be paired with perfusion scans.
- Does intra-alveolar fibrin in the CH04 dogs track systemic DIC markers or the monocyte-macrophage count?
- Is the [CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md) lesion present in survivors or early in disease, or only terminal? A serial experimental design like [CB474](articles/CB474_2014_clinico-biochemical-responses-experimental-infection-babesia.md), with lung imaging and histology, could test this.
- Does the [CB447](articles/CB447_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-acute-respiratory-distress-syndrome.md) red cell aggregation pattern also appear in B. rossi lungs?
- Does transfusion volume or fluid balance in the first 24 hours predict hypoxaemia after adjusting for parasitaemia and haematocrit ([CB102](articles/CB102_2022_successful-treatment-suspect-babesia-induced-ards.md), [CB080](articles/CB080_2021_disorders-blood-circulation-probable-cause-death.md))?

**Data available**

No dataset with pulmonary data is verified. The PUL primaries have none (status 4 for [CB356](articles/CB356_2008_scintigraphic-evaluation-pulmonary-perfusion-pattern-hospitalised.md), [CB447](articles/CB447_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-acute-respiratory-distress-syndrome.md), [CB474](articles/CB474_2014_clinico-biochemical-responses-experimental-infection-babesia.md) and [CB102](articles/CB102_2022_successful-treatment-suspect-babesia-induced-ards.md); status 5 for [CB365](articles/CB365_2009_influence-anaemia-azotaemia-infected-babesia-canis.md), [CB398](articles/CB398_2010_evolution-clinical-haematological-biochemical-findings-young.md) and [CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md)). The datasets linked to this category are all status 1 (public download verified) and none holds lung data:
- [S-CB032](datasets.md#s-cb032): supplement package, a data profile only, summary statistics.
- [S-CB093](datasets.md#s-cb093): correlations between renal indices (PDF), summary statistics.
- [S-CB139](datasets.md#s-cb139): statistical model output (DOCX), summary statistics.
- [S-CB152](datasets.md#s-cb152): supplementary tables of sampling locations and case counts, aggregate study-level.

### NEU — Neurological and cerebral disease

**Question this direction asks**

How often do infected dogs develop neurological dysfunction, what lesions underlie it, and what drives it (sequestration, endothelial or inflammatory injury, metabolic or toxic factors)? Is it a distinct, high-mortality phenotype that could be recognised earlier?

**Evidence base**

Eight primary articles (2006-2026). Four are round 4 papers (2006-2016): [CB437](articles/CB437_2012_presumptive-case-cerebral-babesiosis-poland-caused.md), [CB450](articles/CB450_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-due-babesia-gibsoni-case.md), [CB507](articles/CB507_2015_babesiosis.md) and [CB535](articles/CB535_2016_magnetic-resonance-imaging-brain-involvement-using.md). The category assignment is automatic (NLP), with no human review, and was not changed here. Searches are described in the [search log](search-log.md).

- By design:
  - Three single-dog case reports: [CB437](articles/CB437_2012_presumptive-case-cerebral-babesiosis-poland-caused.md), [CB450](articles/CB450_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-due-babesia-gibsoni-case.md), [CB274](articles/CB274_2025_cerebral-babesiosis-geriatric-pomeranian-case-report.md).
  - Three clinical case series, all from one cohort: [CB279](articles/CB279_2025_molecular-diagnosis-histopathological-analysis-cerebral-babesiosis.md), [CB300](articles/CB300_2026_therapeutic-studies-post-therapeutic-follow-up.md), [CB313](articles/CB313_2026_neuroclinical-features-epidemiology-diagnosis-cerebral-babesiosis.md).
  - One descriptive imaging study, [CB535](articles/CB535_2016_magnetic-resonance-imaging-brain-involvement-using.md).
  - One narrative review, [CB507](articles/CB507_2015_babesiosis.md).
  - No primary article is a controlled or mechanistic study.
- By species: *B. gibsoni* 5 (four from India, one with no country given), *B. canis* 2 (Poland; [CB535](articles/CB535_2016_magnetic-resonance-imaging-brain-involvement-using.md) country not stated), and one multi-species review.
- Text read: four are abstract-only ([CB437](articles/CB437_2012_presumptive-case-cerebral-babesiosis-poland-caused.md), [CB450](articles/CB450_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-due-babesia-gibsoni-case.md), [CB535](articles/CB535_2016_magnetic-resonance-imaging-brain-involvement-using.md), [CB274](articles/CB274_2025_cerebral-babesiosis-geriatric-pomeranian-case-report.md)); four had full text.
- Independent sources: five original-data sources. [CB437](articles/CB437_2012_presumptive-case-cerebral-babesiosis-poland-caused.md), [CB450](articles/CB450_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-due-babesia-gibsoni-case.md), [CB535](articles/CB535_2016_magnetic-resonance-imaging-brain-involvement-using.md) and [CB274](articles/CB274_2025_cerebral-babesiosis-geriatric-pomeranian-case-report.md) are separate; [CB279](articles/CB279_2025_molecular-diagnosis-histopathological-analysis-cerebral-babesiosis.md), [CB300](articles/CB300_2026_therapeutic-studies-post-therapeutic-follow-up.md) and [CB313](articles/CB313_2026_neuroclinical-features-epidemiology-diagnosis-cerebral-babesiosis.md) are one source, cohort CH16 (Proddatur, Andhra Pradesh; 620 screened, 136 smear-positive). [CB507](articles/CB507_2015_babesiosis.md) adds no original data. They are not replication.
- Off-topic label: [CB507](articles/CB507_2015_babesiosis.md) (general babesiosis review; its agent label was SEV and its secondary tags are ERY, END and COA).
- Secondary digest: 18 articles carry an NEU secondary tag. Only three say anything specific about the brain: [CB189](articles/CB189_2020_encephalic-babesiosis-clinical-epidemiological-pathological-aspects.md), [CB493](articles/CB493_2015_babesiosis-perspective-clinical-complications-biomarkers-treatment.md) and [CB389](articles/CB389_2010_clinical-management-babesiosis.md). The others mention neurological terms in passing.
- No *B. rossi* primary article is in NEU. *B. rossi* neurological evidence here is secondary and review-level only.

**What has been studied**

*Syndromes.*
- CH16 (*B. gibsoni*, India): 11 of 136 smear-positive dogs (8.1%) were classed as cerebral, defined by parasites in cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) erythrocytes ([CB313](articles/CB313_2026_neuroclinical-features-epidemiology-diagnosis-cerebral-babesiosis.md), [CB300](articles/CB300_2026_therapeutic-studies-post-therapeutic-follow-up.md)). [CB313](articles/CB313_2026_neuroclinical-features-epidemiology-diagnosis-cerebral-babesiosis.md) notes that dogs were selected for signs, so the percentages are not prevalences.
  - Signs: ataxia, seizures, paddling, opisthotonus, head pressing, stupor, nystagmus, blindness, paraplegia.
  - Only 2 of the 11 had recovered at post-treatment assessment; full text gives 9 of 11 dead by day 28 (about 82%) on diminazene-based polytherapy ([CB300](articles/CB300_2026_therapeutic-studies-post-therapeutic-follow-up.md)).
  - Anaemia was mild (Hb about 10 g/dl in the full text) despite severe signs ([CB300](articles/CB300_2026_therapeutic-studies-post-therapeutic-follow-up.md)).
- [CB279](articles/CB279_2025_molecular-diagnosis-histopathological-analysis-cerebral-babesiosis.md) (same cohort) counts 24 cerebral cases among the same 136 dogs, with the *B. gibsoni* PCR product also found in CSF. [CB300](articles/CB300_2026_therapeutic-studies-post-therapeutic-follow-up.md) and [CB313](articles/CB313_2026_neuroclinical-features-epidemiology-diagnosis-cerebral-babesiosis.md) count 11. The reason is not given.
- Single cases:
  - *B. canis*, Poland: neurological signs with pancytopenia; died despite treatment. 18S rRNA sequence matched genotype 18S RNA-B, which the authors call the more virulent Polish strain (abstract only; no comparative data) ([CB437](articles/CB437_2012_presumptive-case-cerebral-babesiosis-poland-caused.md)).
  - *B. gibsoni*: depression, ataxia and seizures; reported good response to one dose of diminazene at 7.5 mg/kg. Species and cerebral involvement not confirmed in the abstract ([CB450](articles/CB450_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-due-babesia-gibsoni-case.md)).
  - *B. gibsoni* (smear only), India: a ten-year-old dog with ataxia and lethargy and no haematological crisis improved on atovaquone and azithromycin. No neurological work-up is described ([CB274](articles/CB274_2025_cerebral-babesiosis-geriatric-pomeranian-case-report.md)).
- Reviews: cerebral babesiosis is listed among complicated forms ([CB493](articles/CB493_2015_babesiosis-perspective-clinical-complications-biomarkers-treatment.md)), and neurological dysfunction is said to go with a poorer outcome ([CB389](articles/CB389_2010_clinical-management-babesiosis.md)). The digest does not give the source studies.

*Lesions and imaging.*
- CH16, 3 necropsies ([CB279](articles/CB279_2025_molecular-diagnosis-histopathological-analysis-cerebral-babesiosis.md)): brain and meningeal congestion, then oedema, neuronal necrosis, microglial proliferation, neutrophil infiltration, and small vessels occluded by cells with piroplasms in cerebral vessels.
- Retrospective necropsy series, 7 hyperacute fatal cases, species unspecified ([CB189](articles/CB189_2020_encephalic-babesiosis-clinical-epidemiological-pathological-aspects.md)): endothelial hypertrophy, monocytic leukostasis, meningitis, thrombosis, haemorrhage and vasculitis. Parasite load poorly explained death.
- Contrast MRI in severe *B. canis* ([CB535](articles/CB535_2016_magnetic-resonance-imaging-brain-involvement-using.md)): CNS lesions seen in every case, with enhancement at lesion rims. Amplification index 1.19 ± 0.11 centrally and 1.47 ± 0.17 at the periphery; pituitary 1.18 ± 0.05; lateral ventricle plexuses 1.12 ± 0.09. Number of dogs, controls and histology are not in the abstract.
- [CB437](articles/CB437_2012_presumptive-case-cerebral-babesiosis-poland-caused.md) reports brain histopathology and PCR at post mortem, but the abstract gives no findings.

*Proposed mechanisms (largely untested).*
- Microvascular occlusion, thrombosis and congestion, inferred from histology ([CB279](articles/CB279_2025_molecular-diagnosis-histopathological-analysis-cerebral-babesiosis.md)). The explanation for poor treatment response was asserted, not tested.
- An antigen-driven systemic response rather than parasite load ([CB189](articles/CB189_2020_encephalic-babesiosis-clinical-epidemiological-pathological-aspects.md)). No cytokine or coagulation data support it.
- Strain virulence (genotype 18S RNA-B) from one dog ([CB437](articles/CB437_2012_presumptive-case-cerebral-babesiosis-poland-caused.md)).
- Sequestration and hypoxia are stated in [CB313](articles/CB313_2026_neuroclinical-features-epidemiology-diagnosis-cerebral-babesiosis.md) as literature, without data.

*Severity and outcome markers (associations only).*
- The only outcome figure is the CH16 mortality above, in 11 dogs without a comparison group.
- No article in this category measured CRP, lactate, coagulation, cytokine or neural-injury markers in neurologically affected dogs.

**Convergence and conflict**

- Lesion pattern: two necropsy sources ([CB189](articles/CB189_2020_encephalic-babesiosis-clinical-epidemiological-pathological-aspects.md); CH16 via [CB279](articles/CB279_2025_molecular-diagnosis-histopathological-analysis-cerebral-babesiosis.md)) describe vascular and inflammatory change with thrombosis, oedema or haemorrhage. They cover 10 brains. Only CH16 confirmed species. [CB535](articles/CB535_2016_magnetic-resonance-imaging-brain-involvement-using.md) shows focal lesions in vivo in *B. canis*, but cannot say what kind of lesion.
- Parasite load: [CB189](articles/CB189_2020_encephalic-babesiosis-clinical-epidemiological-pathological-aspects.md) found load a poor explanation of death. [CB279](articles/CB279_2025_molecular-diagnosis-histopathological-analysis-cerebral-babesiosis.md) saw piroplasms in occluded vessels. The two differ in species, method and case selection, so this is not a clean contradiction.
- Anaemia: [CB300](articles/CB300_2026_therapeutic-studies-post-therapeutic-follow-up.md) reports mild anaemia with severe signs, whereas [CB437](articles/CB437_2012_presumptive-case-cerebral-babesiosis-poland-caused.md) had pancytopenia. No study compared cerebral with non-cerebral dogs.
- Outcome: CH16 shows about 82% death by day 28. The three single-case reports describe recovery ([CB450](articles/CB450_2013_cerebral-babesiosis-due-babesia-gibsoni-case.md), [CB274](articles/CB274_2025_cerebral-babesiosis-geriatric-pomeranian-case-report.md)) or death ([CB437](articles/CB437_2012_presumptive-case-cerebral-babesiosis-poland-caused.md)). Case reports favour survivors and have unclear case definitions, so they cannot test the CH16 figure.
- Internal conflicts in CH16: 24 versus 11 cerebral cases. [CB313](articles/CB313_2026_neuroclinical-features-epidemiology-diagnosis-cerebral-babesiosis.md) and [CB300](articles/CB300_2026_therapeutic-studies-post-therapeutic-follow-up.md) give no CSF sampling details or CSF data, and [CB279](articles/CB279_2025_molecular-diagnosis-histopathological-analysis-cerebral-babesiosis.md) warns that CSF erythrocytes may be blood contamination.
- Species: *B. canis* and *B. gibsoni* both produce cerebral syndromes, but the evidence is single cases and one imaging abstract. Mechanisms are not shown to be shared.

**Unresolved questions**

- Is the CSF-parasite case definition valid? Test with paired CSF and blood qPCR load, CSF red cell counts, and the CSF sampling method, and explain why CH16 gives 24 and 11 cases.
- Does brain parasite burden (immunohistochemistry or qPCR) predict signs or death, or is it independent of endothelial lesions ([CB189](articles/CB189_2020_encephalic-babesiosis-clinical-epidemiological-pathological-aspects.md) versus [CB279](articles/CB279_2025_molecular-diagnosis-histopathological-analysis-cerebral-babesiosis.md))?
- What do the MRI lesions in *B. canis* ([CB535](articles/CB535_2016_magnetic-resonance-imaging-brain-involvement-using.md)) represent? Pair imaging with histology and report the number of dogs.
- Is genotype 18S RNA-B associated with neurological signs? Genotype consecutive Polish dogs and record neurological status ([CB437](articles/CB437_2012_presumptive-case-cerebral-babesiosis-poland-caused.md)).
- Do cerebral dogs differ in Hb, platelets and parasitaemia from non-cerebral dogs of the same cohort, given the mild anaemia in [CB300](articles/CB300_2026_therapeutic-studies-post-therapeutic-follow-up.md)?
- Does treatment change survival in CSF-positive dogs? CH16 has no comparison arm.
- Do ataxia cases without CSF or imaging ([CB274](articles/CB274_2025_cerebral-babesiosis-geriatric-pomeranian-case-report.md)) have cerebral babesiosis at all once other causes are excluded?

**Data available**

- RQ-[CB300](articles/CB300_2026_therapeutic-studies-post-therapeutic-follow-up.md): status 3 (request-only). [CB300](articles/CB300_2026_therapeutic-studies-post-therapeutic-follow-up.md) says data are available on request; 11 cerebral *B. gibsoni* dogs from CH16; granularity unknown.
- [S-CB091](datasets.md#s-cb091): status 1 (public download verified). Supplementary tables S1-S4 of [CB091](articles/CB091_2022_multi-platforms-strategies-metabolomics-approaches-investigation.md) (secondary NEU tag, cohort CH06): serum metabolomics, 12 *B. canis* dogs against 12 healthy, 1802 untargeted peak features and 205 targeted analytes, individual sample level, processed. It has no neurological label.

### CAR — Cardiac injury and haemodynamics

**Question this direction asks**

Does canine babesiosis injure the myocardium or impair cardiac function, and how often? Do the natriuretic peptide, troponin, ECG and echocardiography findings add prognostic information beyond anaemia and other organ dysfunction? Is low arterial pressure in complicated disease a cardiac or a circulatory problem?

**Evidence base**

- 18 primary articles, all of natural infection or necropsy material. None is an experiment, and none uses multivariable or survival analysis. Search and screening are described in the [search log](search-log.md).
- By design: 3 case-control biomarker or clinical studies ([CB434](articles/CB434_2012_nt-probnp-cardiac-troponin-i-virulent.md), [CB014](articles/CB014_2017_cardiac-disorders-infected-babesia-canis.md), [CB261](articles/CB261_2024_babesiosis-li-k-peklerde-kardiyak-troponin.md)); 1 correlation study ([CB483](articles/CB483_2014_increased-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-negative.md)); 2 necropsy series ([CB497](articles/CB497_2015_cardiac-lesions-babesiosis.md), [CB146](articles/CB146_2026_cardiac-involvement-european-babesiosis-pathological-perspective.md)); 4 descriptive ECG or echocardiography series ([CB159](articles/CB159_2017_electrocardiographic-evaluation-anemic-blood-parasitosis.md), [CB244](articles/CB244_2024_electrocardiographic-changes-babesiosis-ehrlichiosis-affected.md), [CB263](articles/CB263_2024_transthoracic-echocardiographic-assessmentof-cardiac-dysfunction-babesiosis.md), [CB289](articles/CB289_2025_prevalence-different-types-arrhythmias-babesiosis-ehrlichiosis.md)); 2 other descriptive series where the heart is secondary ([CB219](articles/CB219_2022_effect-external-blood-parasites-fertility-german.md), [CB311](articles/CB311_2026_infectious-aetiopathology-peripheral-oedema-clinico-pathological.md)); 4 single case reports ([CB526](articles/CB526_2016_unique-case-babesia-gibsoni-infected-paraplegia.md), [CB284](articles/CB284_2025_myocardial-dysfunction-due-babesiosis.md), [CB216](articles/CB216_2022_clinical-management-babesiosis-mongeral-case-report.md), [CB266](articles/CB266_2025_diagnosis-treatment-associated-complications-labrador-retriever.md)); 2 narrative reviews ([CB259](articles/CB259_2024_complications-multiple-organ-dysfunctions-babesiosis-review.md), [CB318](articles/CB318_2026_review-clinical-utility-haemato-biochemical-biomarkers.md)).
- By species: B. canis 5; B. rossi 1 ([CB434](articles/CB434_2012_nt-probnp-cardiac-troponin-i-virulent.md), suspected); B. gibsoni 6 ([CB526](articles/CB526_2016_unique-case-babesia-gibsoni-infected-paraplegia.md), [CB284](articles/CB284_2025_myocardial-dysfunction-due-babesiosis.md), [CB266](articles/CB266_2025_diagnosis-treatment-associated-complications-labrador-retriever.md), [CB311](articles/CB311_2026_infectious-aetiopathology-peripheral-oedema-clinico-pathological.md), the survey [CB289](articles/CB289_2025_prevalence-different-types-arrhythmias-babesiosis-ehrlichiosis.md) which is mostly B. gibsoni, and the review [CB318](articles/CB318_2026_review-clinical-utility-haemato-biochemical-biomarkers.md)); Babesia unspecified 5; multi-species review 1.
- Round 4 (2006-2016): 4 articles ([CB434](articles/CB434_2012_nt-probnp-cardiac-troponin-i-virulent.md), [CB483](articles/CB483_2014_increased-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-negative.md), [CB497](articles/CB497_2015_cardiac-lesions-babesiosis.md), [CB526](articles/CB526_2016_unique-case-babesia-gibsoni-infected-paraplegia.md)). Three are on topic, and they supply the only B. rossi cardiac biomarker study and the largest B. canis necropsy series.
- Abstract-only: 13 of 18. Full text was read for [CB284](articles/CB284_2025_myocardial-dysfunction-due-babesiosis.md), [CB289](articles/CB289_2025_prevalence-different-types-arrhythmias-babesiosis-ehrlichiosis.md), [CB311](articles/CB311_2026_infectious-aetiopathology-peripheral-oedema-clinico-pathological.md), [CB259](articles/CB259_2024_complications-multiple-organ-dysfunctions-babesiosis-review.md) and [CB318](articles/CB318_2026_review-clinical-utility-haemato-biochemical-biomarkers.md).
- Independent sources: 17. [CB263](articles/CB263_2024_transthoracic-echocardiographic-assessmentof-cardiac-dysfunction-babesiosis.md) and [CB284](articles/CB284_2025_myocardial-dysfunction-due-babesiosis.md) share cohort CH18 (the same Chennai critical care unit, probably the same dogs). [CB261](articles/CB261_2024_babesiosis-li-k-peklerde-kardiyak-troponin.md) (CH15) and [CB497](articles/CB497_2015_cardiac-lesions-babesiosis.md) (CH38) each share a cohort with an article outside this category.
- Only [CB434](articles/CB434_2012_nt-probnp-cardiac-troponin-i-virulent.md), [CB014](articles/CB014_2017_cardiac-disorders-infected-babesia-canis.md) and [CB261](articles/CB261_2024_babesiosis-li-k-peklerde-kardiyak-troponin.md) have healthy controls. [CB159](articles/CB159_2017_electrocardiographic-evaluation-anemic-blood-parasitosis.md), [CB244](articles/CB244_2024_electrocardiographic-changes-babesiosis-ehrlichiosis-affected.md) and [CB289](articles/CB289_2025_prevalence-different-types-arrhythmias-babesiosis-ehrlichiosis.md) pool Babesia with Ehrlichia.
- The category assignment is automatic (NLP). [CB526](articles/CB526_2016_unique-case-babesia-gibsoni-infected-paraplegia.md) (paraplegia), [CB219](articles/CB219_2022_effect-external-blood-parasites-fertility-german.md) (sex hormones) and [CB216](articles/CB216_2022_clinical-management-babesiosis-mongeral-case-report.md) (a case of haemolysis with no cardiac result) look off-topic. [CB311](articles/CB311_2026_infectious-aetiopathology-peripheral-oedema-clinico-pathological.md) (peripheral oedema, 4 of 30 dogs B. gibsoni) and the two low-confidence reviews are only partly on topic. They are not reclassified here.
- The secondary digest adds 23 articles. Only a few carry cardiac data ([CB368](articles/CB368_2009_prospective-randomized-comparison-oxyglobin-hb-200.md), [CB518](articles/CB518_2016_reliability-clinical-monitoring-diagnosis-babesiosis-nigeria.md), [CB234](articles/CB234_2023_physiological-clinico-hemato-biochemical-changes-babesiosis.md), [CB080](articles/CB080_2021_disorders-blood-circulation-probable-cause-death.md)), and the rest are reviews or general clinicopathology.

**What has been studied**

*Syndromes.*
- Myocardial stress and injury markers: in 45 dogs in three severity groups of 15, median NT-proBNP was 246, 650 and 638 pmol/l against 106 pmol/l in 15 controls. Median cTnI was 0.39, 0.4 and 1.45 ng/ml, and only the complicated group differed from controls ([CB434](articles/CB434_2012_nt-probnp-cardiac-troponin-i-virulent.md); abstract only). In 50 B. canis dogs, troponin I was raised in 19 and CK-MB in all (23.17-369.62 U/L); CK-MB is not cardiac-specific in dogs ([CB014](articles/CB014_2017_cardiac-disorders-infected-babesia-canis.md)). hs-cTnI and hs-cTnT were higher in 15 PCR-confirmed B. canis dogs than in 10 controls (p<0.01 and p<0.05; no concentrations given) ([CB261](articles/CB261_2024_babesiosis-li-k-peklerde-kardiyak-troponin.md); CH15). A B. gibsoni case had raised cTnI and a reported valvular lesion, with competing explanations ([CB266](articles/CB266_2025_diagnosis-treatment-associated-complications-labrador-retriever.md)).
- Echocardiography: 31 of 92 critical care dogs had dysfunction (24 diastolic, 2 systolic, 5 both) ([CB263](articles/CB263_2024_transthoracic-echocardiographic-assessmentof-cardiac-dysfunction-babesiosis.md); CH18). One fatal B. gibsoni case had systolic and diastolic dysfunction with myocardial cell death and haemorrhage at histology ([CB284](articles/CB284_2025_myocardial-dysfunction-due-babesiosis.md); CH18).
