- ECG: sinus tachycardia in 20 of 23 anaemic dogs and ST deviation in 5 ([CB159](articles/CB159_2017_electrocardiographic-evaluation-anemic-blood-parasitosis.md), pooled with E. canis). Low QRS voltage and tall T waves in 6 of 15 dogs each ([CB244](articles/CB244_2024_electrocardiographic-changes-babesiosis-ehrlichiosis-affected.md), pooled). In a survey of 465 smear-positive dogs, 108 (23.2%) had ECG abnormalities, mainly tachycardia (44.4%), low-voltage QRS (20.3%) and atrial standstill (16.6%); its subgroup counts do not add up (58+35+13=106) ([CB289](articles/CB289_2025_prevalence-different-types-arrhythmias-babesiosis-ehrlichiosis.md)). Heart rate was higher in 63 smear-positive than in 40 smear-negative Nigerian dogs ([CB518](articles/CB518_2016_reliability-clinical-monitoring-diagnosis-babesiosis-nigeria.md)). Tachycardia was universal in 16 cases ([CB234](articles/CB234_2023_physiological-clinico-hemato-biochemical-changes-babesiosis.md)). Anaemic B. rossi dogs were tachycardic at presentation ([CB368](articles/CB368_2009_prospective-randomized-comparison-oxyglobin-hb-200.md); 12 dogs).
- Necropsy: of 20 Croatian B. canis dogs, 6 had pericardial effusion, 12 had epicardial, endocardial or myocardial haemorrhage, 16 had interfibre oedema, 6 had focal cardiomyocyte necrosis and 4 had myocarditis ([CB497](articles/CB497_2015_cardiac-lesions-babesiosis.md); CH38). Zagreb archive cases showed haemorrhage, effusion, intravascular monocytic leukostasis and necrosis ([CB146](articles/CB146_2026_cardiac-involvement-european-babesiosis-pathological-perspective.md); n not given). Six Polish B. canis dogs had cardiomyocyte necrosis with multi-organ congestion and effusions ([CB080](articles/CB080_2021_disorders-blood-circulation-probable-cause-death.md); CH27). All of these describe fatal disease.
- Arterial pressure: in 48 B. canis dogs, urea:creatinine ratio correlated negatively with systolic, diastolic and mean arterial pressure (r -0.3182 to -0.3909). There was no significant correlation in the 23 azotaemic dogs alone ([CB483](articles/CB483_2014_increased-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-negative.md)). Blood pressure changes after Oxyglobin and red cell transfusion were similar over 72 hours ([CB368](articles/CB368_2009_prospective-randomized-comparison-oxyglobin-hb-200.md)).

*Proposed mechanisms (largely untested).*
- Clustering of parasitised erythrocytes in myocardial capillaries was seen in 4 of 20 dogs and offered as a cause of haemorrhage ([CB497](articles/CB497_2015_cardiac-lesions-babesiosis.md)). [CB146](articles/CB146_2026_cardiac-involvement-european-babesiosis-pathological-perspective.md) infers microvascular injury from leukostasis. [CB080](articles/CB080_2021_disorders-blood-circulation-probable-cause-death.md) proposes circulatory failure as the probable cause of death. [CB289](articles/CB289_2025_prevalence-different-types-arrhythmias-babesiosis-ehrlichiosis.md) attributes ECG changes to anaemic hypoxia, inflammatory injury and metabolic disturbance. [CB483](articles/CB483_2014_increased-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-negative.md) suggests hypotension contributes only in part to the raised urea:creatinine ratio. [CB311](articles/CB311_2026_infectious-aetiopathology-peripheral-oedema-clinico-pathological.md) raises vasculitis from Doppler findings in 6 dogs without histology, and it is not linked to Babesia. None was tested against a cardiac endpoint.

*Severity and outcome markers (associations only).*
- NT-proBNP was higher in the two more severe groups than in the mild group ([CB434](articles/CB434_2012_nt-probnp-cardiac-troponin-i-virulent.md)). In [CB014](articles/CB014_2017_cardiac-disorders-infected-babesia-canis.md), the two deaths had the highest CK-MB (367.33 and 369.62 U/L) and markedly high troponin I. In [CB263](articles/CB263_2024_transthoracic-echocardiographic-assessmentof-cardiac-dysfunction-babesiosis.md), 8 of 31 dogs with dysfunction survived (26%), but survival without dysfunction is not given. Reviews list cardiac dysfunction among complications linked to poorer outcome ([CB389](articles/CB389_2010_clinical-management-babesiosis.md), [CB493](articles/CB493_2015_babesiosis-perspective-clinical-complications-biomarkers-treatment.md)). [CB318](articles/CB318_2026_review-clinical-utility-haemato-biochemical-biomarkers.md) quotes a troponin accuracy figure taken from human myocardial infarction work.

**Convergence and conflict**

- *B. rossi* cardiac evidence dates from 2012: [CB434](articles/CB434_2012_nt-probnp-cardiac-troponin-i-virulent.md) (South Africa) shows NT-proBNP rising with severity in suspected *B. rossi* disease. No *B. rossi* necropsy or echocardiography study was found, and *B. vogeli* is uncharacterised.
- Troponin is raised in B. canis ([CB014](articles/CB014_2017_cardiac-disorders-infected-babesia-canis.md); [CB261](articles/CB261_2024_babesiosis-li-k-peklerde-kardiyak-troponin.md), independent groups) and in the most severe B. rossi group ([CB434](articles/CB434_2012_nt-probnp-cardiac-troponin-i-virulent.md)). The proportion differs: 19 of 50 dogs in [CB014](articles/CB014_2017_cardiac-disorders-infected-babesia-canis.md), but only the complicated group differed from controls in [CB434](articles/CB434_2012_nt-probnp-cardiac-troponin-i-virulent.md). Assays and severity grading differ, so the two are not directly comparable.
- Four necropsy sources agree that fatal cases show myocardial haemorrhage, oedema or necrosis ([CB497](articles/CB497_2015_cardiac-lesions-babesiosis.md), [CB146](articles/CB146_2026_cardiac-involvement-european-babesiosis-pathological-perspective.md), [CB080](articles/CB080_2021_disorders-blood-circulation-probable-cause-death.md), [CB284](articles/CB284_2025_myocardial-dysfunction-due-babesiosis.md)). [CB497](articles/CB497_2015_cardiac-lesions-babesiosis.md) and [CB146](articles/CB146_2026_cardiac-involvement-european-babesiosis-pathological-perspective.md) are both Croatian and overlap is not stated. Samples are fatal cases only, so lesion frequency in survivors is unknown.
- ECG series agree only on non-specific changes (tachycardia, low voltage, T-wave and ST change), which anaemia, fever and hypoxia can explain. Sinus arrhythmia can be normal in dogs. Three series pool Ehrlichia.
- Interpretation conflicts: [CB014](articles/CB014_2017_cardiac-disorders-infected-babesia-canis.md) judges cardiac changes of limited clinical importance. [CB263](articles/CB263_2024_transthoracic-echocardiographic-assessmentof-cardiac-dysfunction-babesiosis.md) reports 26% survival with dysfunction, but calls dysfunction a "good" prognostic indicator, probably a wording error. [CB497](articles/CB497_2015_cardiac-lesions-babesiosis.md) concludes that B. canis is about as pathogenic as B. rossi; no study in this category tests that.
- The only haemodynamic data in this category are [CB483](articles/CB483_2014_increased-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-negative.md) and [CB368](articles/CB368_2009_prospective-randomized-comparison-oxyglobin-hb-200.md).

**Unresolved questions**

- Do admission NT-proBNP and cTnI predict death after adjusting for PCV, creatinine and lactate, in a prospective PCR-speciated cohort? [CB434](articles/CB434_2012_nt-probnp-cardiac-troponin-i-virulent.md) gives only group differences in 15 dogs per group.
- Does NT-proBNP rise before, or independently of, cTnI? Serial samples with renal markers would test this.
- Is the diastolic-predominant dysfunction in [CB263](articles/CB263_2024_transthoracic-echocardiographic-assessmentof-cardiac-dysfunction-babesiosis.md) intrinsic or a loading effect of anaemia and fluids? Serial tissue-Doppler echocardiography before and after transfusion would show it.
- Is cardiac biomarker elevation species-dependent (B. rossi, B. canis, B. gibsoni, B. vogeli) when measured with one assay?
- Do ECG changes in PCR-confirmed Babesia-only dogs differ from matched controls after adjusting for PCV and heart rate?
- Does low arterial pressure reflect hypovolaemia, vasodilation or pump failure? Paired MAP, lactate and echocardiography, with troponin in non-azotaemic dogs, would partition it (extending [CB483](articles/CB483_2014_increased-serum-urea-creatinine-ratio-negative.md)).
- Does myocardial injury resolve in survivors? Troponin kinetics through treatment are not reported.

**Data available**

No dataset is linked to a CAR primary article; all have dataset status 4 or 5, and none is request-only. The context file lists two datasets for secondary-digest articles:
- [S-CB038](datasets.md#s-cb038) (article [CB038](articles/CB038_2019_babesiosis-among-working-organised-kennels-india.md)): status 1, public download verified; summary statistics only (Supplement mmc1.docx).
- [S-CB106](datasets.md#s-cb106) (article [CB106](articles/CB106_2023_autochthonous-babesia-canis-infections-49-germany.md), SRC-M): status 1; per-dog PCR follow-up after imidocarb in 9 dogs and summary laboratory statistics in 23 dogs. The listed variables show no troponin, ECG or echocardiography data.

### MET — Hepatic, metabolic, endocrine and acid-base disturbances

**Question this direction asks**

Which hepatic, endocrine, electrolyte, glucose, lipid and acid-base derangements accompany canine babesiosis? Are they primary lesions, or downstream of haemolysis, hypoxia, hypotension and inflammation? Which carry prognostic information beyond renal and haematological markers?

**Evidence base**

Fifty primary articles, 2006–2026 (see the [search log](search-log.md)). The category assignment is automatic (NLP), with no human check. Nineteen articles are round 4 (2006–2016). Thirty-one of the 50 are abstract-only, including 12 of the 19 round-4 papers.

- Design: 21 descriptive series or small case-control studies; 15 associational studies with a comparison group; 2 outcome-linked series ([CB338](articles/CB338_2007_endocrine-predictors-mortality-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), 95 dogs; [CB040](articles/CB040_2019_clinical-pathological-description-320-cases-naturally.md), 320 dogs); 4 experimental or randomised studies of 3–18 dogs ([CB105](articles/CB105_2022_serial-changes-concentrations-cortisol-thyroid-hormones.md) is the only one on host physiology); 1 assay comparison; 5 case reports; 2 narrative reviews.
- Species, as reported (often by smear only): B. canis 18, B. rossi 7, B. gibsoni 7, B. vogeli 3, unspecified Babesia 13, B. canis plus B. gibsoni 1, multi-species review 1.
- Independent sources: 46. [CB091](articles/CB091_2022_multi-platforms-strategies-metabolomics-approaches-investigation.md) and [CB137](articles/CB137_2025_metabolomics-insights-into-salivary-profile-babesia.md) share CH06; [CB280](articles/CB280_2025_main-biochemical-abnormalities-acute-form-uncomplicated.md) and [CB308](articles/CB308_2026_babesia-canis-b-gibsoni.md) share CH19; [CB338](articles/CB338_2007_endocrine-predictors-mortality-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), [CB340](articles/CB340_2007_plasma-insulin-concentrations-hypoglycaemic-babesia-canis.md) and [CB508](articles/CB508_2015_serum-pancreatic-specific-lipase-concentrations-naturally.md) share CH31. These 7 articles are 3 sources. Most cohort links are "probable", and the Warsaw cohorts CH30, CH34, CH35 and CH37 may overlap.
- Off-topic NLP assignments, not reclassified: [CB359](articles/CB359_2009_evaluation-efficacy-bruceine-natural-quassinoid-compound.md) (trial), [CB125](articles/CB125_2024_effects-gonadectomy-incidence-rate-babesiosis-risk.md) (gonadectomy), [CB536](articles/CB536_2016_prevalence-dirofilaria-repens-cats-healthy-concurrent.md) (Dirofilaria prevalence), [CB031](articles/CB031_2019_seroprevalence-ehrlichiosis-microscopic-screening-babesiosis-harare.md) (seroprevalence), [CB533](articles/CB533_2016_review-babesiosis-european-perspective.md) (practitioner review), [CB312](articles/CB312_2026_unique-case-babesiosis-hemoptysis-clinical-management.md) (haemoptysis case), and the renal-led [CB190](articles/CB190_2020_transient-acquired-fanconi-syndrome-unusual-rare.md), [CB231](articles/CB231_2023_pathogenetic-relationship-between-kidney-pathologies-microcirculatory.md) and [CB478](articles/CB478_2014_renal-function-integrity-babesiosis.md).

**What has been studied**

*Syndromes.*
- **Liver enzymes and bilirubin.** The commonest finding in about 15 descriptive series. Raised AST: 92.6% of 202 Warsaw dogs ([CB330](articles/CB330_2007_biochemical-abnormalities-observed-serum-infected-large.md), abstract only, CH30) and 83.6% of 55 Lviv dogs ([CB280](articles/CB280_2025_main-biochemical-abnormalities-acute-form-uncomplicated.md)). Raised ALT: 64.9% and 25.5%. Raised ALP: 31.7% and 80.0%. [CB280](articles/CB280_2025_main-biochemical-abnormalities-acute-form-uncomplicated.md) does not discuss haemolysis as a source of AST. In 230 B. canis dogs, ALT, AST and ALP did not differ between anaemic and non-anaemic dogs ([CB409](articles/CB409_2011_liver-enzyme-activity-infected-babesia-canis.md), CH34, no uninfected controls). Cholestatic markers (GGT) are said to follow hepatocellular enzymes from day 2–3, without numbers ([CB366](articles/CB366_2009_informational-activity-indicating-liver-enzyme-during.md)). Fatal B. rossi livers show cholestasis, Kupffer cell hypertrophy and centrilobular necrosis, attributed to haemolysis and hypoxia, not primary hepatic failure ([CB147](articles/CB147_2026_liver-histopathology-naturally-acquired-babesia-rossi.md), 10 vs 4 controls, secondary-tagged). In 45 Ukrainian dogs, GGT was about four times normal at stage III ([CB307](articles/CB307_2026_enzyme-morphological-criteria-diagnosing-stages-hepatorenal.md), abstract only, no controls).
- **Pancreas.** In 87 B. rossi dogs, 24 (28%) had cPL above 400 µg/L on admission. cPL was higher with SIRS (median 158 vs 75 µg/L, P = 0.018) and not linked to death (6 deaths); pancreatitis was not confirmed by imaging ([CB508](articles/CB508_2015_serum-pancreatic-specific-lipase-concentrations-naturally.md), CH31). Only 2 of 20 B. gibsoni dogs passed the same cut-off ([CB045](articles/CB045_2019_retrospective-study-intercurrent-pancreatitis-babesia-gibsoni.md)).
- **Glucose and insulin.** Of 94 B. rossi dogs, 16 were hypoglycaemic, 62 normoglycaemic and 16 hyperglycaemic. Median insulin was 10.7, 10.7 and 21.7 pmol/L (no significant difference), mostly below the assay limit; two dogs had inappropriately high insulin ([CB340](articles/CB340_2007_plasma-insulin-concentrations-hypoglycaemic-babesia-canis.md), abstract only). Hypoglycaemic dogs had higher cortisol before and after ACTH and lower thyroxine ([CB342](articles/CB342_2007_response-pituitary-adrenal-pituitary-thyroidal-axes.md), 12 per group, survivors only). Glucose was low in 18.3% ([CB330](articles/CB330_2007_biochemical-abnormalities-observed-serum-infected-large.md)) and 9.1% ([CB280](articles/CB280_2025_main-biochemical-abnormalities-acute-form-uncomplicated.md)), and high in 30.9% ([CB280](articles/CB280_2025_main-biochemical-abnormalities-acute-form-uncomplicated.md)).
- **Endocrine axes.** B. rossi non-survivors (7 of 95) had higher cortisol and ACTH and lower total and free T4; TSH did not differ ([CB338](articles/CB338_2007_endocrine-predictors-mortality-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), CH31). In B. canis, 23 dogs had low TT4 and FT4 correlated with urea and creatinine ([CB441](articles/CB441_2012_euthyroid-sick-syndrome-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), CH35, no controls). In 13 dogs vs 10 controls, T3, T4, fT4 and TSH were lower and IL-6 higher, with IL-6 inversely related to T3 and TSH ([CB503](articles/CB503_2015_low-t3-syndrome-babesiosis-associated-increased.md), CH37, abstract only). Aldosterone was higher and blood pressure lower in 14 B. canis dogs, but only 4 had values above the reference interval ([CB485](articles/CB485_2014_hyperaldosteronism-association-hypotension-azotaemia-babesiosis.md), secondary-tagged).
- **Electrolytes and acid-base.** In 70 B. canis dogs, low sodium, low potassium and high chloride were common ([CB431](articles/CB431_2012_strong-monovalent-electrolyte-imbalances-serum-infected.md), abstract only). In 40 early-stage dogs, most were alkalaemic and nearly all hypochloraemic ([CB423](articles/CB423_2012_electrolyte-level-blood-ph-infected-various.md)). Half of 14 dogs had mixed disorders, typically metabolic acidosis (lactate, chloride) with alkalosis (hypochloraemia, hypoalbuminaemia) ([CB068](articles/CB068_2020_acid-base-status-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md)). Twelve anaemic B. rossi dogs showed hypoalbuminaemic alkalosis with dilutional acidosis, and central venous oxygen tension stayed low for 72 h after either transfusion ([CB368](articles/CB368_2009_prospective-randomized-comparison-oxyglobin-hb-200.md)). Three of 21 non-azotaemic dogs met SIADH criteria ([CB044](articles/CB044_2019_hyponatraemia-syndrome-inappropriate-antidiuretic-hormone-secretion.md)).
- **Lipids and metabolome.** In 29 dogs vs 10 controls, SAA was higher (168.3 vs 3.2 µg/mL), cholesterol lower (4.79 vs 6.15 mmol/L) and ApoA-1 higher ([CB039](articles/CB039_2019_association-acute-babesia-canis-infection-serum.md)). Serum and saliva metabolomics (CH06) point to glutathione, amino-acid and purine pathways ([CB091](articles/CB091_2022_multi-platforms-strategies-metabolomics-approaches-investigation.md), [CB137](articles/CB137_2025_metabolomics-insights-into-salivary-profile-babesia.md)).

*Proposed mechanisms (largely untested).*
- Hypoglycaemia in B. rossi is not usually insulin-driven ([CB340](articles/CB340_2007_plasma-insulin-concentrations-hypoglycaemic-babesia-canis.md)). The authors suggest glucose use, glycogen stores and liver function; none was measured.
- Non-thyroidal-illness physiology: cortisol rose and T4 fell with normal TSH in experimental B. rossi infection ([CB105](articles/CB105_2022_serial-changes-concentrations-cortisol-thyroid-hormones.md), 5 dogs), earlier at high dose (cortisol peak 96 vs 108 h; T4 nadir 108 vs 144 h). IL-6 suppression of the thyroid axis rests on a correlation ([CB503](articles/CB503_2015_low-t3-syndrome-babesiosis-associated-increased.md)) and a self-review ([CB157](articles/CB157_2017_over-20-years-research-babesiosis-faculty.md)).
- Hypotension-triggered vasopressin ([CB044](articles/CB044_2019_hyponatraemia-syndrome-inappropriate-antidiuretic-hormone-secretion.md); vasopressin not measured) and aldosterone-driven hypokalaemia ([CB485](articles/CB485_2014_hyperaldosteronism-association-hypotension-azotaemia-babesiosis.md), [CB157](articles/CB157_2017_over-20-years-research-babesiosis-faculty.md)).

*Severity and outcome markers (associations only).*
- B. rossi, 320 dogs ([CB040](articles/CB040_2019_clinical-pathological-description-320-cases-naturally.md), abstract only): death was associated with hypoglycaemia, hyperlactataemia, azotaemia, hyperbilirubinaemia, hypercortisolaemia and low thyroxine. No independent predictor emerged; ROC cut-offs were not validated.
- B. canis: phosphate above 3 mmol/L and albumin below 2.2 g/dL with death in 240 dogs ([CB061](articles/CB061_2020_complications-risk-factors-regarding-outcomes-babesiosis.md), secondary-tagged); lactate separated 8 non-survivors from 7 survivors ([CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md), CH21, secondary-tagged).

**Convergence and conflict**

- **Cortisol and T4 (best agreement).** Higher cortisol and lower T4 go with death or severity in four B. rossi sources: the 2005 cohort ([CB338](articles/CB338_2007_endocrine-predictors-mortality-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), CH31), [CB342](articles/CB342_2007_response-pituitary-adrenal-pituitary-thyroidal-axes.md), the 320-dog series ([CB040](articles/CB040_2019_clinical-pathological-description-320-cases-naturally.md)) and the experiment ([CB105](articles/CB105_2022_serial-changes-concentrations-cortisol-thyroid-hormones.md)). The 2007 Onderstepoort work is therefore strengthened, but all four come from one hospital. In B. canis, low T4 appears in two small Warsaw series ([CB441](articles/CB441_2012_euthyroid-sick-syndrome-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), [CB503](articles/CB503_2015_low-t3-syndrome-babesiosis-associated-increased.md)) with no outcome link. TSH was normal in B. rossi ([CB338](articles/CB338_2007_endocrine-predictors-mortality-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md), [CB105](articles/CB105_2022_serial-changes-concentrations-cortisol-thyroid-hormones.md)) and low in [CB503](articles/CB503_2015_low-t3-syndrome-babesiosis-associated-increased.md). [CB204](articles/CB204_2021_euthyroid-sick-syndrome-changes-thyroid-hormones.md) cannot be attributed to Babesia, because E. canis was in every dog.
- **Glucose.** The old contrast of B. rossi hypoglycaemia against Lviv hyperglycaemia does not hold: 16 of 94 B. rossi dogs were also hyperglycaemic ([CB340](articles/CB340_2007_plasma-insulin-concentrations-hypoglycaemic-babesia-canis.md)). High insulin does not explain hypoglycaemia. [CB340](articles/CB340_2007_plasma-insulin-concentrations-hypoglycaemic-babesia-canis.md) had no healthy controls, and secondary [CB486](articles/CB486_2014_serum-cortisol-insulin-concentrations-naturally-infected.md) reports higher insulin in B. canis than in healthy dogs (abstract). These are unreconciled.
- **Liver.** AST rises more often than ALT in both large series ([CB330](articles/CB330_2007_biochemical-abnormalities-observed-serum-infected-large.md), [CB280](articles/CB280_2025_main-biochemical-abnormalities-acute-form-uncomplicated.md)). Histology in B. rossi ([CB147](articles/CB147_2026_liver-histopathology-naturally-acquired-babesia-rossi.md)) and suspected B. canis ([CB307](articles/CB307_2026_enzyme-morphological-criteria-diagnosing-stages-hepatorenal.md)) agrees on cholestasis, but both sampled severe or fatal cases. [CB409](articles/CB409_2011_liver-enzyme-activity-infected-babesia-canis.md) argues against mild anaemia alone as the cause.
- **Chloride.** [CB431](articles/CB431_2012_strong-monovalent-electrolyte-imbalances-serum-infected.md) found high chloride common, [CB423](articles/CB423_2012_electrolyte-level-blood-ph-infected-various.md) low chloride in nearly all, and [CB068](articles/CB068_2020_acid-base-status-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md) both. Timing or case mix may matter. Only [CB068](articles/CB068_2020_acid-base-status-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md) and [CB368](articles/CB368_2009_prospective-randomized-comparison-oxyglobin-hb-200.md) measured blood gases.
- **Pancreas.** B. rossi (28% above cut-off, [CB508](articles/CB508_2015_serum-pancreatic-specific-lipase-concentrations-naturally.md)) looks higher than B. gibsoni (2 of 20, [CB045](articles/CB045_2019_retrospective-study-intercurrent-pancreatitis-babesia-gibsoni.md)), but this compares separate studies.

**Unresolved questions**

- Are cortisol and T4 predictive independently of lactate, urea and bilirubin? [CB040](articles/CB040_2019_clinical-pathological-description-320-cases-naturally.md) found no independent predictor. Test with free T4, T3 and ACTH in prospective B. rossi and B. canis cohorts.
- What causes hypoglycaemia when insulin is low? Measure lactate, bile acids and cytokines against serial glucose in hypoglycaemic and normoglycaemic dogs.
- How much hyperbilirubinaemia is cholestatic rather than haemolytic? Use fractionated bilirubin, bile acids and GGT over days, with histology.
- Is hyponatraemia vasopressin-mediated? Measure copeptin and osmolality against MAP, and aldosterone against potassium, in the same dogs.
- Does chloride direction depend on time since onset? Take serial blood gases with strong-ion analysis on days 0–3 in one cohort.
- What is the true pancreatitis frequency per species, with cPL plus ultrasound under one protocol?

**Data available**

- [S-CB091](datasets.md#s-cb091): status 1. Processed serum metabolomics, 24 dogs.
- [S-CB137](datasets.md#s-cb137): status 1. Salivary metabolomics, 28 samples.
- [DS04](datasets.md#ds04): status 1. Liver histology, IHC, haematology and biochemistry; 10 fatal B. rossi dogs, 4 controls ([CB147](articles/CB147_2026_liver-histopathology-naturally-acquired-babesia-rossi.md)).
- [S-CB147](datasets.md#s-cb147): status 1. Per-case, per-zone liver IHC counts, 10 cases.
- [S-CB032](datasets.md#s-cb032): status 1. Summary statistics, one case report.
- [S-CB516](datasets.md#s-cb516): status 1. 15 natural B. canis cases, individual signs and outcome.
- [S-CB042](datasets.md#s-cb042): status 1. 48 B. vulpes dogs, individual clinical data.
- [S-CB106](datasets.md#s-cb106): status 1. Follow-up PCR after imidocarb, 9 dogs; summary laboratory values, 23 dogs.
- [S-CB046](datasets.md#s-cb046), [S-CB093](datasets.md#s-cb093), [S-CB038](datasets.md#s-cb038), [S-CB139](datasets.md#s-cb139): status 1. Summary level only (protein lists, correlations, model output).
- [P-PXD005117](datasets.md#p-pxd005117), [P-PRJEB15861](datasets.md#p-prjeb15861): status 2. B. canis proteome and genome (parasite side).
- RQ-[CB031](articles/CB031_2019_seroprevalence-ehrlichiosis-microscopic-screening-babesiosis-harare.md), RQ-[CB536](articles/CB536_2016_prevalence-dirofilaria-repens-cats-healthy-concurrent.md), RQ-[CB134](articles/CB134_2025_clinical-clinicopathological-features-108-infected-babesia.md), RQ-[CB222](articles/CB222_2022_evaluation-acute-phase-proteins-suffering-various.md): status 3. Request-only dog-level data.

No endocrine, electrolyte, acid-base or pancreatic primary study has a verified dataset.

### OXO — Oxidative stress, host-parasite interaction, omics and virulence

**Question this direction asks**
Does an oxidant-antioxidant imbalance accompany, or drive, erythrocyte and organ injury in canine babesiosis? Which parasite factors and genotypes separate mild from severe disease? Secondarily: do antioxidant adjuncts change recovery? Host omics belong to this label, but the evidence in it is now almost wholly parasite-side.

**Evidence base**
- 21 primary articles (2006-2026), plus 16 articles that carry OXO as a secondary NLP tag. Searches are in the [search log](search-log.md).
- By design: 9 redox or biomarker case-control studies or case series; 1 prognostic case-control study; 3 randomised treatment studies (2 antioxidant, 1 antiparasitic); 3 experimental infections (parasite genome, parasite peroxiredoxin, drug and detection trial); 1 parasite-genotype study with retrospective clinical comparison; 1 clinical survey; 3 narrative reviews.
- By evidence type: 10 associational, 5 descriptive, 2 mechanistic (experimental), 1 prognostic, 3 review.
- By species: *B. gibsoni* 8, *B. canis* 6 (one of them a survey of mixed *B. canis*-like cases), *B. vogeli* 2, *B. rossi* 1, *Babesia* sp. unspecified 1, multi-species reviews 3.
- Round 4 (2006-2016): 9 articles ([CB335](articles/CB335_2007_babesia-gibsoni-detection-during-experimental-infections.md), [CB353](articles/CB353_2008_erythrocytic-antioxidant-defense-lipid-peroxides-level.md), [CB379](articles/CB379_2009_preliminary-evaluation-brema1-gene-tool-associating.md), [CB453](articles/CB453_2013_clinical-signs-seasonal-occurrence-causative-agents.md), [CB477](articles/CB477_2014_investigation-8-hydroxy-2-deoxyguanosine-total.md), [CB481](articles/CB481_2014_relationship-between-paraoxonase-1-activity-high.md), [CB499](articles/CB499_2015_lipid-peroxidation-naturally-infected-babesia-canis.md), [CB515](articles/CB515_2015_nitric-oxide-protein-oxidation-total-antioxidant.md), [CB529](articles/CB529_2016_therapeutic-effects-atovaquone-proguanil-combination-azithromycin.md)). Abstract-only: 16 of 21; full text was read for 5 ([CB529](articles/CB529_2016_therapeutic-effects-atovaquone-proguanil-combination-azithromycin.md), [CB010](articles/CB010_2017_genome-wide-analysis-gene-expression-protein.md), [CB077](articles/CB077_2021_antioxidant-status-blood-zinc-copper-concentrations.md), [CB229](articles/CB229_2023_investigation-thiol-disulphide-homeostasis-total-oxidant.md), [CB135](articles/CB135_2025_molecular-identification-thioredoxin-peroxidase-babesia-gibsoni.md)).
- Independent sources: 18 primary studies, with no two primary articles in the same cohort. The cohorts CH08 ([CB074](articles/CB074_2021_prognostic-markers-their-discriminant-score-predicting.md)), CH14 ([CB229](articles/CB229_2023_investigation-thiol-disulphide-homeostasis-total-oxidant.md)), CH21 ([CB010](articles/CB010_2017_genome-wide-analysis-gene-expression-protein.md)) and CH25 ([CB077](articles/CB077_2021_antioxidant-status-blood-zinc-copper-concentrations.md)) each have other members outside this category ([CB202](articles/CB202_2020_serum-hepcidin-clinical-prognostic-marker-discriminate.md) and [CB286](articles/CB286_2025_low-haemoglobin-density-anaemic-indicator-babesiosis.md); [CB242](articles/CB242_2023_evaluation-total-lipid-bound-sialic-acids.md); [CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md); [CB069](articles/CB069_2020_elevated-serum-manganese-concentration-possible-predisposing.md)). The 3 reviews add no data. [CB142](articles/CB142_2025_effect-n-acetylcysteine-oxidative-stress-hematological.md) and [CB151](articles/CB151_2026_n-acetylcysteine-adjunct-therapy-ameliorates-oxidative.md) (both NAC in *B. gibsoni*) and [CB051](articles/CB051_2020_molecular-diagnosis-clinico-hemato-biochemical-alterations.md) and secondary [CB154](articles/CB154_2026_comprehensive-assessment-babesia-gibsoni-infection-clinicopathological.md) (both Mizoram) have no assigned cohort; overlap is not stated.
- The category assignment is automatic (NLP). Four primary articles are low confidence ([CB453](articles/CB453_2013_clinical-signs-seasonal-occurrence-causative-agents.md), [CB076](articles/CB076_2021_relationship-between-changes-hematological-parameters-levels.md), [CB142](articles/CB142_2025_effect-n-acetylcysteine-oxidative-stress-hematological.md), [CB151](articles/CB151_2026_n-acetylcysteine-adjunct-therapy-ameliorates-oxidative.md)). [CB335](articles/CB335_2007_babesia-gibsoni-detection-during-experimental-infections.md), [CB453](articles/CB453_2013_clinical-signs-seasonal-occurrence-causative-agents.md) and [CB529](articles/CB529_2016_therapeutic-effects-atovaquone-proguanil-combination-azithromycin.md) look off-topic for OXO: they are detection, survey and antiparasitic treatment studies. [CB074](articles/CB074_2021_prognostic-markers-their-discriminant-score-predicting.md) and [CB287](articles/CB287_2025_hematological-biochemical-determinants-anemic-syndrome-animals.md) look closer to SEV and ERY (the agents' labels). None has been reclassified.

**What has been studied**
*Syndromes.* Anaemia is the commonest presentation in the redox studies, for example 15 PCR-confirmed *B. gibsoni* cases in Mizoram ([CB051](articles/CB051_2020_molecular-diagnosis-clinico-hemato-biochemical-alterations.md)) and eight *B. canis* cases with severe thrombocytopenia ([CB076](articles/CB076_2021_relationship-between-changes-hematological-parameters-levels.md)). Complicated disease and multiple organ dysfunction syndrome (MODS) appear in [CB499](articles/CB499_2015_lipid-peroxidation-naturally-infected-babesia-canis.md) and, in secondary evidence, [CB006](articles/CB006_2017_relation-antioxidant-status-admission-disease-severity.md) (5 MODS dogs, 4 deaths). The French survey [CB453](articles/CB453_2013_clinical-signs-seasonal-occurrence-causative-agents.md) (836 reported cases) gives the clinical picture: lethargy and anorexia 98%, fever 80%, pallor 54%, altered urine 45%, splenomegaly 33%. Only 70 were tested and 25 (36%) were PCR-positive, so many cases may not have been babesiosis.

*Redox biomarkers (associational).*
- Lipid peroxidation was higher in infected dogs in every study that measured it. [CB353](articles/CB353_2008_erythrocytic-antioxidant-defense-lipid-peroxides-level.md) (*B. gibsoni*, 10 v 6): 6.01 v 1.89 nmol MDA/mg haemoglobin. [CB499](articles/CB499_2015_lipid-peroxidation-naturally-infected-babesia-canis.md) (*B. canis canis*, 30 v 25): raised on day 1, lower but still above controls on day 7. [CB051](articles/CB051_2020_molecular-diagnosis-clinico-hemato-biochemical-alterations.md) and [CB076](articles/CB076_2021_relationship-between-changes-hematological-parameters-levels.md) agree. Secondary: [CB394](articles/CB394_2010_malondialdehyde-levels-serum-infected-babesia-canis.md) (36.90 v 8.13 µmol/l, CH33), [CB006](articles/CB006_2017_relation-antioxidant-status-admission-disease-severity.md), [CB083](articles/CB083_2021_molecular-characterization-assessment-hematobiochemical-oxidative-indices.md) and [CB154](articles/CB154_2026_comprehensive-assessment-babesia-gibsoni-infection-clinicopathological.md).
- Other oxidative markers: serum 8-hydroxy-2'-deoxyguanosine was higher in *B. vogeli* ([CB477](articles/CB477_2014_investigation-8-hydroxy-2-deoxyguanosine-total.md), P<0.05; group sizes not given). [CB515](articles/CB515_2015_nitric-oxide-protein-oxidation-total-antioxidant.md) found higher NOx, AOPP and FRAP overall, but only 5 dogs had *B. vogeli* alone. [CB229](articles/CB229_2023_investigation-thiol-disulphide-homeostasis-total-oxidant.md) (7 v 7, CH14): lower antioxidant capacity, higher oxidative stress index; thiol-disulphide and ischaemia-modified albumin unchanged; AUCs of 0.980 and 1.0 are probably overfitted.
- Trace elements: zinc and copper were lower in both [CB077](articles/CB077_2021_antioxidant-status-blood-zinc-copper-concentrations.md) (zinc 0.478 v 1.241, copper 0.722 v 1.392 µg/mL; 15 v 7; CH25) and [CB353](articles/CB353_2008_erythrocytic-antioxidant-defense-lipid-peroxides-level.md) (which also found lower iron, 89.87 v 126.44 µg/g). They may reflect acute-phase redistribution or anorexia.
- Paraoxonase 1: [CB481](articles/CB481_2014_relationship-between-paraoxonase-1-activity-high.md) (29 v 15) found low PON1 and HDL on admission, HDL recovering by day 1, PON1 rising and CRP falling by day 7. [CB076](articles/CB076_2021_relationship-between-changes-hematological-parameters-levels.md) and secondary [CB057](articles/CB057_2020_evidence-acute-phase-reaction-asymptomatic-naturally.md) also report low PON1.
- After treatment, erythrocyte MDA stayed high at day 7 ([CB499](articles/CB499_2015_lipid-peroxidation-naturally-infected-babesia-canis.md)) and day 15 ([CB076](articles/CB076_2021_relationship-between-changes-hematological-parameters-levels.md), n=8). Both used imidocarb, with no untreated comparison.

*Proposed mechanisms (largely untested).* Reviews [CB114](articles/CB114_2023_host-oxidative-stress-piroplasmosis-review-domestic.md), [CB287](articles/CB287_2025_hematological-biochemical-determinants-anemic-syndrome-animals.md) and [CB319](articles/CB319_2026_host-oxidative-responses-piroplasmosis-current-perspectives.md) propose that haemoglobin oxidation and membrane peroxidation promote haemolysis and anaemia. None of the 18 primary studies tests this. [CB319](articles/CB319_2026_host-oxidative-responses-piroplasmosis-current-perspectives.md) cites experimental canine work on accelerated red cell clearance, but that work is not among our primary articles. [CB287](articles/CB287_2025_hematological-biochemical-determinants-anemic-syndrome-animals.md) lists LDH as an oxidative marker, though it mainly reflects haemolysis. No primary study measured haemolysis against oxidative markers over time. Each correlation equally fits oxidative change as a consequence of haemolysis, inflammation or anorexia.

*Severity and outcome markers (associations only).* In [CB499](articles/CB499_2015_lipid-peroxidation-naturally-infected-babesia-canis.md), MDA correlated with outcome, was higher in complicated disease on day 7, and was higher in MODS. [CB006](articles/CB006_2017_relation-antioxidant-status-admission-disease-severity.md) (secondary; 40 infected: 29 uncomplicated, 6 one complication, 5 MODS) found lower SOD, GPx and catalase and higher MDA with MODS and death. [CB074](articles/CB074_2021_prognostic-markers-their-discriminant-score-predicting.md) (*B. gibsoni*, 108 dogs, 30 controls, CH08): catalase, haemoglobin, platelets, urea, ALT, lactate and others correlated with survival; a discriminant score (cut-off 0.141) gave 79% sensitivity and 83% specificity, with no external validation. Secondary [CB202](articles/CB202_2020_serum-hepcidin-clinical-prognostic-marker-discriminate.md) (hepcidin, AUC about 0.984) is the same CH08 family and not independent. [CB077](articles/CB077_2021_antioxidant-status-blood-zinc-copper-concentrations.md) and [CB229](articles/CB229_2023_investigation-thiol-disulphide-homeostasis-total-oxidant.md) did not analyse severity, so their prognostic claims are inferred.

*Parasite factors and virulence.*
- [CB010](articles/CB010_2017_genome-wide-analysis-gene-expression-protein.md) (CH21): genome of a virulent *B. canis* isolate with exported VESA, SA and Bc28 families, plus profilin and histones. One strain, one RNA-seq sample from a splenectomised dog, three pooled Beagles for proteomics, no avirulent comparator.
- [CB135](articles/CB135_2025_molecular-identification-thioredoxin-peroxidase-babesia-gibsoni.md): a *B. gibsoni* peroxiredoxin (BgTPx-2) protected plasmid DNA in a cell-free assay; antibody was detectable on day 4 in infected Beagles. Its role in the parasite is not tested.
- [CB379](articles/CB379_2009_preliminary-evaluation-brema1-gene-tool-associating.md) (Onderstepoort, 141 *B. rossi* samples): 12 BrEMA1 genotypes. The four commonest (19, 28, 29, 11; 36.2%, 20.6%, 20.6%, 12.7%) were also linked to the poorest prognosis. No statistics are given in the abstract.
- [CB335](articles/CB335_2007_babesia-gibsoni-detection-during-experimental-infections.md) (abstract only): parasite detected in tissues of chronically infected dogs, which the authors suggest may be sequestration. Secondary [CB369](articles/CB369_2009_detection-erythrocyte-binding-igm-igg-flow.md) (CH32): erythrocyte-bound IgG or IgM in 4 of 6 *B. c. vogeli* dogs but 0 of 24 *B. c. canis* dogs, with different anaemia types.

*Interventions.* NAC (70 mg/kg) plus triple antimicrobials in *B. gibsoni*: [CB142](articles/CB142_2025_effect-n-acetylcysteine-oxidative-stress-hematological.md) (12 randomised, 6 controls; only antioxidant capacity and MCHC differed significantly) and [CB151](articles/CB151_2026_n-acetylcysteine-adjunct-therapy-ameliorates-oxidative.md) (12 v 12; lower lipid peroxide and higher glutathione and antioxidant capacity reported; the abstract gives no effect sizes or primary-endpoint result). The antiparasitic studies, [CB529](articles/CB529_2016_therapeutic-effects-atovaquone-proguanil-combination-azithromycin.md) (8 dogs, 4 per arm) and [CB335](articles/CB335_2007_babesia-gibsoni-detection-during-experimental-infections.md), lie outside the pathophysiology question.

**Convergence and conflict**
- Raised lipid peroxidation in infected dogs is consistent across *B. gibsoni* ([CB353](articles/CB353_2008_erythrocytic-antioxidant-defense-lipid-peroxides-level.md), [CB051](articles/CB051_2020_molecular-diagnosis-clinico-hemato-biochemical-alterations.md), [CB154](articles/CB154_2026_comprehensive-assessment-babesia-gibsoni-infection-clinicopathological.md)), *B. canis* ([CB499](articles/CB499_2015_lipid-peroxidation-naturally-infected-babesia-canis.md), [CB076](articles/CB076_2021_relationship-between-changes-hematological-parameters-levels.md), [CB394](articles/CB394_2010_malondialdehyde-levels-serum-infected-babesia-canis.md), [CB006](articles/CB006_2017_relation-antioxidant-status-admission-disease-severity.md)) and *B. vogeli* ([CB083](articles/CB083_2021_molecular-characterization-assessment-hematobiochemical-oxidative-indices.md)). The earliest are [CB353](articles/CB353_2008_erythrocytic-antioxidant-defense-lipid-peroxides-level.md) (2008) and [CB394](articles/CB394_2010_malondialdehyde-levels-serum-infected-babesia-canis.md) (2010), so the finding predates 2016. Low zinc and copper ([CB353](articles/CB353_2008_erythrocytic-antioxidant-defense-lipid-peroxides-level.md), [CB077](articles/CB077_2021_antioxidant-status-blood-zinc-copper-concentrations.md)) and low PON1 ([CB481](articles/CB481_2014_relationship-between-paraoxonase-1-activity-high.md), [CB076](articles/CB076_2021_relationship-between-changes-hematological-parameters-levels.md), [CB057](articles/CB057_2020_evidence-acute-phase-reaction-asymptomatic-naturally.md)) also recur.
- Enzyme direction conflicts. Erythrocyte SOD and catalase were higher in [CB353](articles/CB353_2008_erythrocytic-antioxidant-defense-lipid-peroxides-level.md) (*B. gibsoni*) and [CB077](articles/CB077_2021_antioxidant-status-blood-zinc-copper-concentrations.md) (*B. canis*) but lower in [CB051](articles/CB051_2020_molecular-diagnosis-clinico-hemato-biochemical-alterations.md) (*B. gibsoni*) and [CB006](articles/CB006_2017_relation-antioxidant-status-admission-disease-severity.md). Total antioxidant capacity was lower in [CB229](articles/CB229_2023_investigation-thiol-disulphide-homeostasis-total-oxidant.md) and [CB051](articles/CB051_2020_molecular-diagnosis-clinico-hemato-biochemical-alterations.md), only a non-significant trend in [CB477](articles/CB477_2014_investigation-8-hydroxy-2-deoxyguanosine-total.md), and higher (FRAP) in the mixed-infection [CB515](articles/CB515_2015_nitric-oxide-protein-oxidation-total-antioxidant.md). Sampling stage, severity, sample type and assay differ, as [CB319](articles/CB319_2026_host-oxidative-responses-piroplasmosis-current-perspectives.md) notes.
- MDA and severity conflict: [CB499](articles/CB499_2015_lipid-peroxidation-naturally-infected-babesia-canis.md) and [CB006](articles/CB006_2017_relation-antioxidant-status-admission-disease-severity.md) link MDA to complicated disease or MODS, but [CB394](articles/CB394_2010_malondialdehyde-levels-serum-infected-babesia-canis.md) (35 dogs, 7 complicated) found no difference by complication or anaemia grade. [CB394](articles/CB394_2010_malondialdehyde-levels-serum-infected-babesia-canis.md) and [CB499](articles/CB499_2015_lipid-peroxidation-naturally-infected-babesia-canis.md) are both Zagreb studies; overlap is not stated.
- [CB379](articles/CB379_2009_preliminary-evaluation-brema1-gene-tool-associating.md)'s genotype link cannot separate genotype from frequency and has not been replicated. [CB010](articles/CB010_2017_genome-wide-analysis-gene-expression-protein.md) has no avirulent comparator. Redox findings should not be moved between species; *B. rossi* has no redox data here, and antioxidant trials exist only for *B. gibsoni*.

**Unresolved questions**
- Does erythrocyte lipid peroxidation precede the haematocrit fall? Sample from admission, before treatment, with haemoglobin-normalised MDA, reticulocytes and haemolysis indices ([CB499](articles/CB499_2015_lipid-peroxidation-naturally-infected-babesia-canis.md) started at day 1 of treatment).
- Is the SOD and catalase discordance ([CB353](articles/CB353_2008_erythrocytic-antioxidant-defense-lipid-peroxides-level.md), [CB077](articles/CB077_2021_antioxidant-status-blood-zinc-copper-concentrations.md) v [CB051](articles/CB051_2020_molecular-diagnosis-clinico-hemato-biochemical-alterations.md), [CB006](articles/CB006_2017_relation-antioxidant-status-admission-disease-severity.md)) an artefact of assay, normalisation or stage? Re-measure with one assay in one cohort, stratified by days of illness and severity.
- Does MDA grade severity within *B. canis*? Run a prospective cohort with MODS defined in advance, to settle [CB499](articles/CB499_2015_lipid-peroxidation-naturally-infected-babesia-canis.md) and [CB006](articles/CB006_2017_relation-antioxidant-status-admission-disease-severity.md) against [CB394](articles/CB394_2010_malondialdehyde-levels-serum-infected-babesia-canis.md).
- Does the raised MDA after imidocarb ([CB499](articles/CB499_2015_lipid-peroxidation-naturally-infected-babesia-canis.md) day 7, [CB076](articles/CB076_2021_relationship-between-changes-hematological-parameters-levels.md) day 15) show ongoing red cell damage or red cell turnover? Follow beyond 15 days with reticulocyte adjustment and clinical sequelae.
- Does NAC change parasite clearance, transfusion need or survival in a blinded, adequately powered trial, with parasitaemia reported ([CB142](articles/CB142_2025_effect-n-acetylcysteine-oxidative-stress-hematological.md) and [CB151](articles/CB151_2026_n-acetylcysteine-adjunct-therapy-ameliorates-oxidative.md) had 12 per arm)?
- Does BrEMA1 genotype predict outcome after adjustment for treatment delay and host factors in an independent *B. rossi* sample, and are the [CB010](articles/CB010_2017_genome-wide-analysis-gene-expression-protein.md) candidates (VESA, SA, Bc28) expressed in natural infection?

**Data available**
- [P-PXD005117](datasets.md#p-pxd005117): status 2 (public record verified, file retrieval unverified); *B. canis* secreted proteome in virulent infection, raw and processed omics ([CB010](articles/CB010_2017_genome-wide-analysis-gene-expression-protein.md)).
- [P-PRJEB15861](datasets.md#p-prjeb15861): status 2; PacBio SMRT genome of *B. canis* BcH-CHIPZ, sequence and annotation only ([CB010](articles/CB010_2017_genome-wide-analysis-gene-expression-protein.md)).
- [S-CB006](datasets.md#s-cb006): status 1 (public download verified); summary statistics of biochemistry and haematology, control v babesiosis ([CB006](articles/CB006_2017_relation-antioxidant-status-admission-disease-severity.md), secondary).
- No per-dog redox dataset is available.

### SEV — Multisystem clinical phenotype, severity and outcome

**Question this direction asks**
What clinical and clinicopathological phenotype does each canine *Babesia* species produce, how should uncomplicated, complicated and multiple organ dysfunction (MODS) disease be told apart, and which routine variables mark the dogs that die? Complications here are outcomes, not explained mechanisms.

**Evidence base**
- 59 primary articles (2006–2026). 26 are round 4 (2006–2016); 33 are 2017–2026. The window and screening are in the [search log](search-log.md).
- Design:
  - 36 primary studies: 2 experimental ([CB320](articles/CB320_2006_immunity-against-babesia-rossi-infection-vaccinated.md) vaccination and challenge, [CB021](articles/CB021_2018_first-description-pathogenicity-babesia-vogeli-experimentally.md)), 10 associational or prognostic, 24 descriptive.
  - 11 case reports or case series of 1–3 animals, including two wolf reports.
  - 12 narrative reviews, which add no data.
- Species, in the 47 articles that are not reviews:
  - *B. canis* 20 (one with *B. vulpes*), *B. gibsoni* 7, *B. rossi* 2, *B. vogeli* 2, *B. conradae* 1, mixed species 3, unspecified 12.
  - Many calls rest on smear morphology alone.
- Text read: 32 notes are abstract-only (17 of the 26 round-4 notes); 27 used full text.
- Independent sources: no two primary articles share a recorded cohort, so the ceiling is 47 sources with original data. It is a ceiling. The Zagreb *B. canis* papers ([CB358](articles/CB358_2008_white-blood-cell-count-neutrophil-lymphocyte.md), [CB399](articles/CB399_2010_systemic-inflammatory-response-syndrome-multiple-organ.md), [CB394](articles/CB394_2010_malondialdehyde-levels-serum-infected-babesia-canis.md), [CB006](articles/CB006_2017_relation-antioxidant-status-admission-disease-severity.md)) come from one clinic and overlap is not stated. Several primary articles belong to cohorts that also include secondary-digest papers: CH31, CH32, CH33, CH34, CH38, CH21, CH14, CH20.
- The category is assigned automatically (NLP). Four primary articles are high confidence, 48 low, and 7 had no NLP category and carry the agents' SEV label ([CB358](articles/CB358_2008_white-blood-cell-count-neutrophil-lymphocyte.md), [CB430](articles/CB430_2012_babesiosis-europe-how-many-diseases.md), [CB444](articles/CB444_2013_t-rkiye-de-k-peklerde-babesia.md), [CB448](articles/CB448_2013_babesiose-canina-uma-vis-o-geral.md), [CB457](articles/CB457_2013_babesios-analysis-scientific-literature.md), [CB043](articles/CB043_2019_clinical-occurrence-babesiosis-coastal-area-black.md), [CB108](articles/CB108_2023_clinical-presentation-diagnostic-investigations-treatment-protocols.md)). Several look off-topic for SEV: [CB320](articles/CB320_2006_immunity-against-babesia-rossi-infection-vaccinated.md) (vaccine efficacy), [CB041](articles/CB041_2019_mechanisms-involved-persistence-babesia-canis-infection.md) (sequestration hypothesis), [CB117](articles/CB117_2023_proteomics-identification-overexpressed-serum-proteins-babesiacanis.md) (serum proteomics), [CB285](articles/CB285_2025_hemolytic-anemia-caused-protozoan-parasite-babesia.md) (treatment effect), [CB537](articles/CB537_2016_methodical-guidelines-diagnostics-treatment-prevention-babesiosis.md) (guidelines), [CB518](articles/CB518_2016_reliability-clinical-monitoring-diagnosis-babesiosis-nigeria.md) (diagnostic reliability), [CB218](articles/CB218_2022_ophthalmic-manifestations-tick-borne-infections.md) (eye disease). [CB352](articles/CB352_2008_adrenal-response-low-dose-acth-stimulation.md), [CB394](articles/CB394_2010_malondialdehyde-levels-serum-infected-babesia-canis.md), [CB424](articles/CB424_2012_increased-ast-alt-ratio-azotaemic-infected.md) and [CB501](articles/CB501_2015_assessment-renal-function-using-cystatin-c.md) are marker studies that fit MET, OXO or REN better. None is reclassified here.
- The earlier version rested on 2016–2026 series, mostly of *B. gibsoni* and *B. canis*. The *B. rossi* evidence now in this category is the 2005–2008 Onderstepoort endocrine work (CH31). The Onderstepoort coagulation, cytokine and renal studies fall in other categories and are not read here.

**What has been studied**

*Syndromes.*
- *B. canis*, Europe:
  - [CB106](articles/CB106_2023_autochthonous-babesia-canis-infections-49-germany.md), Germany, 49 dogs: all thrombocytopenic, 85% anaemic, 52% intravascular haemolysis, 37% SIRS; 7 died or were euthanised.
  - [CB357](articles/CB357_2008_babesia-canis-canis-babesia-canis-vogeli.md), Italy, 45 PCR-positive dogs (CH32; abstract only): *B. c. canis* gave thrombocytopenia, non-regenerative anaemia, haemoglobinuria (13/19) and hypoalbuminaemia (17/26). The 11 *B. c. vogeli* dogs had no uniform pattern.
  - [CB518](articles/CB518_2016_reliability-clinical-monitoring-diagnosis-babesiosis-nigeria.md), Nigeria: in 103 suspected dogs, temperature and packed cell volume did not separate smear-positive from smear-negative dogs.
- Grading:
  - [CB399](articles/CB399_2010_systemic-inflammatory-response-syndrome-multiple-organ.md), Croatia, 332 dogs (abstract only): 68% met SIRS criteria; 33 (10%) had MODS, kidney in 30, liver 20, muscle 19, lung 16, brain 3; 11 of 33 survived, falling with organ count (45% with two organs, 10% with three, none with four).
  - [CB043](articles/CB043_2019_clinical-occurrence-babesiosis-coastal-area-black.md), Romania (abstract only): recovery 81.6% (uncomplicated), 70.1% (one organ), 38.4% (MODS, 13 dogs).
  - [CB372](articles/CB372_2009_septic-shock-babesiosis.md) (CH33): 10 of 86 admissions had septic shock; all died within four days, against 2.6% mortality in the rest.
- Organs: kidney is the commonest MODS organ in [CB399](articles/CB399_2010_systemic-inflammatory-response-syndrome-multiple-organ.md). Azotaemia was linked to a higher AST/ALT ratio (2.68 vs 1.08; [CB424](articles/CB424_2012_increased-ast-alt-ratio-azotaemic-infected.md), CH34). [CB501](articles/CB501_2015_assessment-renal-function-using-cystatin-c.md) found higher cystatin-C in 16 uncomplicated *B. vogeli* dogs while creatinine did not differ. [CB332](articles/CB332_2007_abdominal-compartment-syndrome-babesiosis.md) reports abdominal compartment syndrome in one dog.
- Brain: [CB189](articles/CB189_2020_encephalic-babesiosis-clinical-epidemiological-pathological-aspects.md) (7 necropsies): leukostasis, thrombosis and vasculitis, with a heavy parasite load in only one dog.
- Indian series, mostly *B. gibsoni*: seven small series ([CB171](articles/CB171_2018_clinical-haemato-biochemical-ultrasonographical-studies-naturally.md), [CB176](articles/CB176_2018_clinico-haemato-biochemical-changes-therapeutic-management.md), [CB234](articles/CB234_2023_physiological-clinico-hemato-biochemical-changes-babesiosis.md), [CB237](articles/CB237_2023_clinical-haemato-biochemical-evaluation-comparison-polymerase.md), [CB294](articles/CB294_2026_clinical-signs-ultrasonographic-findings-babesiosis.md), [CB301](articles/CB301_2026_haemato-biochemical-changes-naturally-occurring-babesiosis.md), [CB310](articles/CB310_2026_clinico-pathological-ultrasonographical-studies-babesia-gibsoni.md); 7–28 dogs each; [CB310](articles/CB310_2026_clinico-pathological-ultrasonographical-studies-babesia-gibsoni.md) found 23 PCR-positive among 1,628 examined) repeat anaemia, thrombocytopenia, azotaemia, raised ALT and low albumin. None tests outcome. [CB038](articles/CB038_2019_babesiosis-among-working-organised-kennels-india.md): 51 PCR-positive kennel dogs (47 *B. gibsoni*, 4 *B. vogeli*), frequent relapse.
- *B. conradae*: [CB026](articles/CB026_2018_babesia-conradae-infection-coyote-hunting-infected.md), 29 PCR-positive of 55 sighthounds, 16 co-infected; cytopenias with hypoalbuminaemia.
- Young and wild canids:
  - Pups: [CB391](articles/CB391_2010_note-incidence-babesia-canis-10-day.md) (10-day pup) and [CB195](articles/CB195_2020_role-babesiosis-pup-mortality.md) (3 of 77 pup deaths, aged 20–45 days; helminths and bacteria co-present).
  - Wolves: [CB471](articles/CB471_2014_fatal-acute-babesiosis-captive-grey-wolves.md) (4 of 12 captive wolves died) and [CB139](articles/CB139_2025_fatal-babesiosis-free-ranging-iberian-wolf.md) (one wild wolf, two co-infections, cachexia).
- Necropsy case reports ([CB097](articles/CB097_2022_pathomorphological-changes-german-boxer-infected-babesia.md), [CB188](articles/CB188_2020_pathomorphological-parasitological-molecular-diagnosis-babesia-gibsoni.md), [CB249](articles/CB249_2024_pathological-diagnosis-babesiosis-labrador-retriever.md), [CB290](articles/CB290_2025_electrolyte-imbalances-coagulopathy-multiorgan-dysfunction-concurrent.md)): lung oedema, tubular or glomerular injury, hepatic change, splenomegaly.

*Proposed mechanisms (largely untested).*
- Sequestration and immunothrombosis: [CB041](articles/CB041_2019_mechanisms-involved-persistence-babesia-canis-infection.md) is a hypothesis review from one author's model. [CB220](articles/CB220_2022_features-hepatopathy-hematological-complications-acute-spontaneous.md) (abstract only) proposes a loop of DIC, shock and hepatopathy.
- Shock and circulatory failure: [CB522](articles/CB522_2016_septic-shock-similarities-differences-postmortem-findings.md) (5 dogs, CH38) and [CB080](articles/CB080_2021_disorders-blood-circulation-probable-cause-death.md) (6 dogs, CH27) describe lesion patterns; the shock interpretation is the authors'.
- Mediators: TNF-α correlated inversely with arterial pressure in 11 dogs ([CB473](articles/CB473_2014_increased-concentration-serum-tnf-alpha-correlations.md), CH37). PGE2 stayed high in MODS but not SIRS ([CB470](articles/CB470_2014_serum-concentrations-eicosanoids-lipids-naturally-infected.md)). Plasma proteins differed between SIRS and MODS in 5 dogs per group ([CB528](articles/CB528_2016_plasma-biomarkers-sirs-mods-associated-babesiosis.md)).
- Asplenia: two of three splenectomised dogs became critically ill in [CB021](articles/CB021_2018_first-description-pathogenicity-babesia-vogeli-experimentally.md); fatal cases in splenectomised dogs ([CB527](articles/CB527_2016_fatal-babesia-canis-canis-infection-splenectomized.md); [CB120](articles/CB120_2023_case-report-fatal-babesia-vulpes-infection.md), with *Mycoplasma*). [CB357](articles/CB357_2008_babesia-canis-canis-babesia-canis-vogeli.md) lists splenectomised or immunocompromised dogs among its *B. vogeli* cases.
- Co-infection: [CB290](articles/CB290_2025_electrolyte-imbalances-coagulopathy-multiorgan-dysfunction-concurrent.md) (*B. gibsoni* with *Leptospira*) proposes synergy; the case cannot show it.

*Severity and outcome markers (associations only).*
- Endocrine (CH31): higher cortisol with death in [CB352](articles/CB352_2008_adrenal-response-low-dose-acth-stimulation.md) (4 deaths among 68) and in [CB338](articles/CB338_2007_endocrine-predictors-mortality-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md) (7 of 95, with higher ACTH and lower thyroxine; TSH no different). No adrenal insufficiency; deltas and cortisol:ACTH did not separate outcomes.
- [CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md) (7 survivors, 8 non-survivors; CH21): raised lactate, triglyceride and phosphate; low haematocrit, leukocytes, protein and platelets. All non-survivors were hyperlactataemic, 6 of 7 survivors were not. Cut-offs were set on the same dogs. Parasitaemia did not differ.
- [CB061](articles/CB061_2020_complications-risk-factors-regarding-outcomes-babesiosis.md) (240 dogs): creatinine >5 mg/dL, phosphate >3 mmol/L, albumin <2.2 g/dL, temperature <38 °C; mortality 33.9% with renal involvement, 40.0% with pancreatitis.
- Oxidative: [CB006](articles/CB006_2017_relation-antioxidant-status-admission-disease-severity.md) (40 infected, 30 controls; 5 MODS, 4 deaths): low SOD and GPx and high MDA with MODS and death.
- Haematology: neutrophil to lymphocyte ratio higher in 30 complicated than 30 uncomplicated dogs ([CB358](articles/CB358_2008_white-blood-cell-count-neutrophil-lymphocyte.md); no outcome analysis).
- Parasite genotype: four of 12 BrEMA1 genotypes were linked to poorer prognosis in 141 *B. rossi* samples ([CB379](articles/CB379_2009_preliminary-evaluation-brema1-gene-tool-associating.md); retrospective).

**Convergence and conflict**
- Organ count tracks outcome in two independent *B. canis* sources from different countries, [CB399](articles/CB399_2010_systemic-inflammatory-response-syndrome-multiple-organ.md) (2010) and [CB043](articles/CB043_2019_clinical-occurrence-babesiosis-coastal-area-black.md) (2019), with a similar MODS survival of about a third. The 2010 paper makes the gradient older and better supported than the earlier version showed. It is shown only for *B. canis*.
- Phosphate is higher in non-survivors in two unrelated groups ([CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md) Swiss, [CB061](articles/CB061_2020_complications-risk-factors-regarding-outcomes-babesiosis.md) Viennese). Renal dysfunction dominates MODS in [CB399](articles/CB399_2010_systemic-inflammatory-response-syndrome-multiple-organ.md) and [CB372](articles/CB372_2009_septic-shock-babesiosis.md) (both Croatian). Lactate rests on one small series, [CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md), so the earlier version's lactate convergence no longer stands.
- Cortisol rises with death in CH31, which is one cohort in two papers. Whether it adds to lactate or organ count is not tested.
- Parasitaemia: higher in complicated disease in [CB358](articles/CB358_2008_white-blood-cell-count-neutrophil-lymphocyte.md), not different by outcome in [CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md). [CB189](articles/CB189_2020_encephalic-babesiosis-clinical-epidemiological-pathological-aspects.md) found low brain parasite load in six of seven fatal cerebral cases, and [CB041](articles/CB041_2019_mechanisms-involved-persistence-babesia-canis-infection.md) argues severe disease occurs with few circulating parasites. These sit uneasily with load as the driver.
- Oxidative markers conflict. MDA did not separate complicated from uncomplicated dogs in [CB394](articles/CB394_2010_malondialdehyde-levels-serum-infected-babesia-canis.md) (7 complicated), yet was higher in MODS and fatal cases in [CB006](articles/CB006_2017_relation-antioxidant-status-admission-disease-severity.md) (5 MODS). Possible overlap between the Zagreb groups is not excluded.
- Asplenia is the most consistent host factor: experiment ([CB021](articles/CB021_2018_first-description-pathogenicity-babesia-vogeli-experimentally.md)), two fatal cases ([CB527](articles/CB527_2016_fatal-babesia-canis-canis-infection-splenectomized.md), [CB120](articles/CB120_2023_case-report-fatal-babesia-vulpes-infection.md)) and [CB357](articles/CB357_2008_babesia-canis-canis-babesia-canis-vogeli.md). All are three animals or fewer; no spleen-intact comparison exists for the fatal cases.
- Mortality is not comparable: 6–7% in the *B. rossi* admission cohort ([CB352](articles/CB352_2008_adrenal-response-low-dose-acth-stimulation.md), [CB338](articles/CB338_2007_endocrine-predictors-mortality-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md)), 9% in Portugal (4 of 45; [CB392](articles/CB392_2010_babesiosis-northern-portugal-molecular-characterization-vector.md)), 14% in [CB106](articles/CB106_2023_autochthonous-babesia-canis-infections-49-germany.md), 8 of 15 in [CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md) (selected), 33.9% with renal involvement in [CB061](articles/CB061_2020_complications-risk-factors-regarding-outcomes-babesiosis.md). Referral, case definition and treatment differ.
- Species: [CB021](articles/CB021_2018_first-description-pathogenicity-babesia-vogeli-experimentally.md) found mild disease in intact *B. vogeli* dogs, but [CB357](articles/CB357_2008_babesia-canis-canis-babesia-canis-vogeli.md) includes young natural *B. vogeli* dogs with haemolytic anaemia. [CB119](articles/CB119_2023_diverse-pathogenicity-various-babesia-parasite-species.md) ranks species indirectly; [CB448](articles/CB448_2013_babesiose-canina-uma-vis-o-geral.md) still uses the older scheme that treats *B. rossi* as a *B. canis* subspecies. Indian *B. gibsoni* series are species-clean only when PCR-confirmed ([CB038](articles/CB038_2019_babesiosis-among-working-organised-kennels-india.md), [CB301](articles/CB301_2026_haemato-biochemical-changes-naturally-occurring-babesiosis.md), [CB310](articles/CB310_2026_clinico-pathological-ultrasonographical-studies-babesia-gibsoni.md)).
- Clinical indices alone are weak: [CB518](articles/CB518_2016_reliability-clinical-monitoring-diagnosis-babesiosis-nigeria.md) found temperature and packed cell volume uninformative for diagnosis.

**Unresolved questions**
- Do admission lactate and phosphate ([CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md), [CB061](articles/CB061_2020_complications-risk-factors-regarding-outcomes-babesiosis.md)) predict death in a new multi-centre cohort with cut-offs fixed beforehand and compared with organ count?
- Do cortisol, ACTH and thyroxine (CH31) add information after adjusting for lactate and organ count?
- Does the MODS survival gradient ([CB399](articles/CB399_2010_systemic-inflammatory-response-syndrome-multiple-organ.md), [CB043](articles/CB043_2019_clinical-occurrence-babesiosis-coastal-area-black.md)) hold in *B. gibsoni* and *B. rossi* under one SIRS and MODS definition?
- Are splenectomised dogs at higher risk of fatal natural infection than spleen-intact dogs of the same species ([CB021](articles/CB021_2018_first-description-pathogenicity-babesia-vogeli-experimentally.md), [CB527](articles/CB527_2016_fatal-babesia-canis-canis-infection-splenectomized.md), [CB120](articles/CB120_2023_case-report-fatal-babesia-vulpes-infection.md))?
- Is brain capillary parasite burden at necropsy different in cerebral and non-cerebral fatal cases ([CB189](articles/CB189_2020_encephalic-babesiosis-clinical-epidemiological-pathological-aspects.md), [CB195](articles/CB195_2020_role-babesiosis-pup-mortality.md))?
- Are the SOD, GPx and MDA differences by severity ([CB006](articles/CB006_2017_relation-antioxidant-status-admission-disease-severity.md), [CB394](articles/CB394_2010_malondialdehyde-levels-serum-infected-babesia-canis.md)) reproducible in an independent MODS group of 20 or more?

**Data available**
- [S-CB516](datasets.md#s-cb516): status 1; 15 natural cases, individual dogs: outcome, signs, travel history, signalment; laboratory data only as a figure of differential counts, so lactate values are not available.
- [S-CB106](datasets.md#s-cb106): status 1; per-dog follow-up PCR after imidocarb (9 dogs) and a summary laboratory table (23 dogs, T0/T1/T2); no severity labels.
- [S-CB006](datasets.md#s-cb006): status 1; summary statistics of biochemistry and haematology, controls versus infected.
- [S-CB038](datasets.md#s-cb038): status 1; supplement of summary statistics (Word file).
- [S-CB139](datasets.md#s-cb139): status 1; model output (Word file), wolf survey.
- RQ-[CB065](articles/CB065_2020_differences-serum-protein-electrophoretic-pattern-naturally.md): request-only, 57 dogs at dog level (*B. gibsoni* v *B. canis*, protein electrophoresis); not obtained.

## Human disease comparison

Human comparator papers ([HU notes](index.md#supporting-references-excluded-from-canine-counts)) were selected per category as search leads, not equivalences. Strength: **directly demonstrated in both** = the same pathway or marker was measured in both hosts; **mechanistically plausible** = a pathway demonstrated in humans with consistent but indirect canine evidence; **speculative** = analogy only. Shared clinical signs alone were not accepted as a connection.

| Category | Shared pathway | Canine evidence | Human evidence | Key differences | Strength |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ERY | Immune-mediated (antibody-driven) erythrocyte destruction layered on parasite-driven haemolysis, with asplenia/splenectomy as a risk modifier | Canine babesiosis includes Coombs-positive haemolysis in a B. canis pup ([CB096](articles/CB096_2022_sirs-mods-imha-associated-babesia-canis.md), n=1) and flow-cytometric IMHA/IMTP (Evans syndrome) in a smear-negative, PCR-positive B. canis dog that improved on antiparasitic plus immunosuppressive therapy ([CB047](articles/CB047_2019_molecular-diagnosis-babesia-canis-infection-concurrent.md), n=1). In 55 dogs with IMHA, concurrent PCR-confirmed B. gibsoni independently predicted death ([CB153](articles/CB153_2026_concurrent-babesia-gibsoni-infection-negative-prognostics.md), HR 2.6, p=0.024), and secondary IMHA raised recurrence risk in B. gibsoni ([CB067](articles/CB067_2020_analysis-risk-factors-associated-recurrence-babesiosis.md), n=145). Splenectomised dogs developed severe or life-threatening anaemia with B. vogeli ([CB021](articles/CB021_2018_first-description-pathogenicity-babesia-vogeli-experimentally.md), n=6) and marrow haemophagocytic syndrome with B. vulpes ([CB132](articles/CB132_2025_successful-treatment-severe-babesia-vulpes-infection.md), n=1, immunosuppression plus antiprotozoals). A narrative review argues canine babesial anaemia is largely host-immune driven ([CB107](articles/CB107_2023_pathogenesis-anemia-babesiosis-possible-contribution-pro.md)). Regenerative response is inadequate in B. rossi ([CB115](articles/CB115_2023_regenerative-response-naturally-experimentally-infected-babesia.md)). | PMID 28273010 ([HU001](articles/HU001_2017_post-babesiosis-warm-autoimmune-hemolytic-anemia.md)): in 86 babesiosis patients, 6 (all asplenic, P<0.001) developed warm AIHA 2-4 weeks after diagnosis, after clinical response and parasitaemia clearance; all had DAT positive for IgG and C3 with warm autoantibodies; 4 of 6 needed immunosuppression. Supporting only (not selected papers): case reports and a literature review (PMID 34007364, PMID 40790611) report the same pattern of IgG-positive DAT, asplenia and steroid response. | Human data are from B. microti in a US hospital cohort, with the key phenomenon being post-clearance autoimmune haemolysis in asplenic patients; canine evidence is almost entirely single-case reports (CB047, CB096) or IMHA cohorts where Babesia is a co-factor rather than the cause (CB153, CB067), mostly B. canis/gibsoni/vogeli/rossi in intact dogs. Complement (C3) coating, the post-clearance timing and the autoantibody specificity have not been shown in dogs in the digest. Canine IMHA labelling relies on Coombs/spherocytosis/flow cytometry, with less standardised criteria; whether antibody is autoantibody or parasite-antigen-adsorbed is unresolved in both hosts. Dog spleen status was explicit only in the experimental/case-report splenectomy studies. | **mechanistically plausible** — Antibody-mediated red cell destruction in the setting of Babesia infection has been documented in both hosts (DAT/Coombs-positive haemolysis, response to immunosuppression), and asplenia worsens disease in both. But the canine documentation is two n=1 case reports plus co-factor cohorts, and the human defining feature (post-clearance WAHA with IgG+C3 DAT, n=6 of 86) has no canine cohort counterpart, so it is not 'directly demonstrated in both'. |
| ERY | Intravascular haemolysis releasing cell-free haemoglobin and free haem, depleting scavengers (haptoglobin, haemopexin), with oxidative injury to kidney and other organs | The specific axis (cell-free Hb, free haem, haptoglobin, haemopexin) was not measured in any ERY or ERY (secondary) study in the digest. Indirect indicators of haemolysis and oxidative stress: hyperbilirubinaemia in 90% ([CB099](articles/CB099_2022_occurrence-babesiosis-rhine-main-area-hesse.md)) and pigmenturia in 26% ([CB149](articles/CB149_2026_retrospective-analysis-anamnesis-clinical-signs-therapy.md), from the digest's other sections as cited in the digest), haemolysis-related pathways in whole-blood RNA-seq of experimental B. rossi infection ([CB082](articles/CB082_2021_experimental-babesia-rossi-infection-induces-hemolytic.md), n=5), lowered SOD/GPx/catalase with raised MDA tied to MODS and death ([CB006](articles/CB006_2017_relation-antioxidant-status-admission-disease-severity.md)), oxidative-stress changes in B. canis, B. gibsoni and B. vogeli ([CB051](articles/CB051_2020_molecular-diagnosis-clinico-hemato-biochemical-alterations.md), [CB075](articles/CB075_2021_prevalence-genetic-biochemical-evaluation-immune-response.md), [CB076](articles/CB076_2021_relationship-between-changes-hematological-parameters-levels.md), [CB077](articles/CB077_2021_antioxidant-status-blood-zinc-copper-concentrations.md), [CB083](articles/CB083_2021_molecular-characterization-assessment-hematobiochemical-oxidative-indices.md)), a review concluding oxidative erythrocyte injury contributes to haemolysis ([CB114](articles/CB114_2023_host-oxidative-stress-piroplasmosis-review-domestic.md)), and liver lesions attributed to haemolysis and hypoxia ([CB147](articles/CB147_2026_liver-histopathology-naturally-acquired-babesia-rossi.md), n=10). One serum proteomics study found hemopexin more abundant in infected dogs ([CB117](articles/CB117_2023_proteomics-identification-overexpressed-serum-proteins-babesiacanis.md), 3 vs 3 dogs), the opposite direction to the depletion seen in falciparum malaria and consistent with an acute-phase rise. | PMID 28449641 ([HU002](articles/HU002_2017_cell-free-hemoglobin-mediated-oxidative-stress.md)): in 107 adults with severe falciparum malaria, CFH correlated with haemolysis markers and parasite burden (PfHRP2) and with F2-isoprostanes; AKI (58%) patients had higher CFH (8.8 vs 5.1 microM), F2-isoprostanes and isofurans, and lipid-peroxidation markers predicted dialysis and death. PMID 26691827 ([HU003](articles/HU003_2015_dysregulation-haem-haemopexin-axis-associated-severe.md)): in 87 Ugandan children, haemopexin was lower and haemin higher in severe malarial anaemia and cerebral malaria than uncomplicated malaria, and haemin and CFH predicted death among 58 severe cases; Hpx-null mice had higher mortality. | Falciparum malaria involves cytoadherent sequestration, PfHRP2-measured parasite burden, high-density parasitaemia and haem-induced endothelial and renal injury; much of the CFH burden is attributed to schizont rupture and, in the studies, a role for NO scavenging. B. canis, B. rossi and others differ in host-cell invasion, lack the same sequestration biology, and, to the knowledge of this review (not verified in the digest), do not form haemozoin, so haem handling may differ. Canine studies used oxidative markers (SOD, MDA) rather than haem or scavenger assays; antioxidant enzyme patterns in dogs are inconsistent in direction across studies (CB006 low versus CB077 raised erythrocyte SOD/CAT). Human malaria data are adult (Bangladesh) and paediatric (Uganda) cohorts with severe malaria, not equivalent to canine babesiosis; neither human paper is a babesiosis study, and no human babesiosis study of CFH/haptoglobin/haemopexin was found in the search. | **speculative** — Haemolysis and oxidative stress are shown in canine babesiosis, but the cell-free Hb/haem/haptoglobin/haemopexin axis itself has not been measured in dogs in the digest, and the single canine hemopexin finding (CB117, n=3 vs 3) points the opposite way. The human evidence is from falciparum malaria only, with no human babesiosis counterpart located, so the mechanism is inferred by analogy. |
| INF | Circulating pro-inflammatory cytokine/chemokine response (TNF, IL-8/CXCL8-family, IL-1beta, IL-6, MCP-1) scaling with disease severity and death: dysregulated innate cytokine response in an intra-erythrocytic parasitaemia | [CB037](articles/CB037_2019_disease-severity-blood-cytokine-concentrations-natural.md) (B. rossi, n=55 PCR-positive dogs plus 17 controls): IL-6, MCP-1 and TNF-alpha rose with severity and were higher in the 9 non-survivors, while IL-8 fell with severity. [CB017](articles/CB017_2018_mcp-1-kc-like-il-8.md) (B. canis, n=31 infected, 16 healthy): day-1 MCP-1 and KC-like (the canine CXCL1/IL-8 functional homologue) were higher in complicated dogs (MCP-1 AUC 0.906); IL-8 correlated inversely with RBC and haematocrit in uncomplicated dogs. [CB098](articles/CB098_2022_kinetics-inflammatory-response-during-experimental-babesia.md)/CB101 (experimental B. rossi, 5 infected Beagles): inflammation (cytokines, CRP) began before clinical disease, was dose-dependent, and treatment transiently amplified cytokine and CRP responses; [CB082](articles/CB082_2021_experimental-babesia-rossi-infection-induces-hemolytic.md) (same model) whole-blood RNA-seq showed innate and interferon pathway activation. [CB109](articles/CB109_2023_haematological-indices-immune-response-profiles-naturally.md) (B. canis, n=32 babesiosis in gene-expression subset): highest IFN-gamma and SOCS3 expression in severe disease. [CB107](articles/CB107_2023_pathogenesis-anemia-babesiosis-possible-contribution-pro.md) narrative review argues cytokines drive the anaemia. All natural-infection data are associational and single-centre. | PMID 36759905 ([HU004](articles/HU004_2023_elevated-plasma-interleukin-8-risk-factor.md)) (Royo 2023; n=70 paediatric cerebral malaria, 20 deaths): TNF, IL-1beta, IL-8, IL-10, CXCL9, granzyme B and angiopoietin-2 higher in those who died; admission IL-8 independent mortality risk factor (aOR 14.2). Supporting context for severe-malaria generally (not among the three selected papers, not independently verified here): PMID 35467747 (TNF), 35396564 (IL-6) and 39567619 (MCP-1/CCL2) are systematic reviews reporting higher levels in severe than non-severe malaria, but with I2 above 97%. No primary human babesiosis study measuring cytokine levels against severity was found in PubMed (see search_log); the only human babesiosis host-response severity signal found was falling LDL/HDL in admitted patients (PMID 37111499, n=39 retrospective), which is an acute-phase/lipoprotein finding rather than a cytokine finding. | 1) The canine and human cohorts used different outcomes: dogs were classified by 'complicated' (organ dysfunction) or survival, children by cerebral malaria death. 2) IL-8 is discordant: low IL-8 tracked severity in B. rossi dogs (CB037) but high IL-8 predicted death in children (Royo); dogs' KC-like in CB017 was higher in complicated cases, so canine IL-8/KC data themselves conflict, and assay platforms and canine cross-reactivity differ. 3) P. falciparum infected red cells cytoadhere and sequester in the brain microvasculature, whereas Babesia sequestration in dogs is only proposed (CB041) and cerebral babesiosis is rare. 4) Host age, splenic status, co-infection control (Royo excluded co-infection; many canine cohorts did not), and treatment timing differ; CB098 shows treatment itself alters cytokine kinetics. 5) Mortality is small in the canine series (9 non-survivors in CB037) and 20 in Royo, so both estimates are imprecise. 6) Neither dataset shows causation; a cytokine storm as such has not been defined or demonstrated in canine babesiosis. 7) Human babesiosis is the closer parasite comparator, but no equivalent primary cytokine-severity data exist. | **directly demonstrated in both** — Same measured variable (elevated circulating TNF and chemokines in natural-infection non-survivors or severe cases vs milder cases) is reported in dogs (CB037, CB017) and humans (PMID 36759905). Rated at the level of an association, not a causal cytokine-storm mechanism. IL-8 direction is contradictory, and the human data come from malaria, not babesiosis, so confidence in cross-species transfer is moderate. |
| INF | Neutrophil activation and degranulation (myeloperoxidase, elastase, proteinase 3 granule proteins) associated with severe disease and death | [CB050](articles/CB050_2020_neutrophil-myeloperoxidase-index-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md) (B. rossi, retrospective analysis of two cohorts, n=140 infected, 20 controls): mean peroxidase index of neutrophils (MPXI) was higher in infected dogs (P=0.033) and highest in non-survivors (P=0.011) than controls, and correlated with pro-inflammatory cytokines in non-survivors; mortality 11%. [CB073](articles/CB073_2021_systemic-inflammatory-response-syndrome-naturally-infected.md) (B. canis, n=54): leukopenia and monocytopenia, not parasite load or acute-phase proteins, were independent correlates of SIRS. [CB082](articles/CB082_2021_experimental-babesia-rossi-infection-induces-hemolytic.md) (experimental B. rossi, n=5): whole-blood RNA-seq showed innate immune pathway activation. [CB054](articles/CB054_2020_pathology-spleen-lethal-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md) and [CB111](articles/CB111_2023_lung-pathology-natural-babesia-rossi-infection.md) (necropsy series) show macrophage expansion in spleen and increased macrophages/T cells in lung; no canine study measured neutrophil elastase, MPO protein release, degranulation transcripts or NETs directly. MPXI is a haematology-analyser readout of peroxidase staining intensity whose link to activation or degranulation is inferred. | PMID 26884431 ([HU005](articles/HU005_2016_activated-neutrophils-are-associated-pediatric-cerebral.md)) (Feintuch 2016; Malawian children with cerebral malaria): neutrophil transcripts were the most upregulated genes in retinopathy-positive (cerebral sequestration) disease; plasma neutrophil elastase, MPO and proteinase 3 were higher (n=51 vs 25 per full text), neutrophil chemotaxis impaired, no NETs in autopsy brain. PMID 41738283 ([HU006](articles/HU006_2026_whole-blood-transcriptomics-reveals-sepsis-mortality.md)) (Giannini 2026; sepsis, n=161 plus 479 validation): MPO, ELANE, PRTN3, DEFA4 and CEACAM8 transcripts were more than 1.8-fold higher in 30-day non-survivors after adjustment for neutrophil count, and the neutrophil degranulation pathway was replicated in the second cohort.
